Is video ke detailed notes Hindi wali English (Hinglish) mein bana raha hoon. Notes ka format Teacher Asks → Student Answers rahega, examples aur interview Q&A ke saath, taaki aap directly apni `.txt` file mein copy-paste karke revision kar sako.

# Spring AI – Part 9: ChatClient Fluent API, Responses & Entity Mapping

Video ka main topic:

1. `ChatClient` Fluent API
2. Prompt pass karne ke 3 tarike
3. `System Message` aur `User Message`
4. `ChatResponse`, `Generation`, aur `AssistantMessage`
5. Response metadata, token usage aur rate limits
6. AI response ko Java POJO mein convert karna
7. Single object aur `List` of objects return karna

## MODULE 1: ChatClient Fluent API

Q1. Teacher: Spring AI mein ChatClient kya hai?

Answer: `ChatClient` Spring AI ka high-level API hai, jiska use karke hum AI model ya LLM ko prompt bhejte hain aur uska response receive karte hain.

Simple flow:

`User Request → Controller → Service → ChatClient → AI Model → Response`

Example:

```
String response = chatClient.prompt()
        .user("Tell me about Java")
        .call()
        .content();
```

Yahan:

- `prompt()` — prompt banane ka process start karta hai.
- `user()` — user ka message set karta hai.
- `call()` — AI request execute karta hai.
- `content()` — response ka text return karta hai.

Q2. Teacher: Fluent API kya hoti hai?

Answer: Fluent API mein hum multiple methods ko chain karke ek readable statement mein kaam karte hain.

Example:

```
String response = chatClient.prompt()
        .user("What is Spring Boot?")
        .call()
        .content();
```

Is style ko method chaining bhi kehte hain. Isse code readable aur concise hota hai.

Q3. Teacher: Controller ke andar saara AI logic likhne ke bajay Service kyun banate hain?

Answer: Service banane se code organized aur maintainable rehta hai.

- Controller: HTTP request aur response handle karta hai.
- Service: Business logic aur AI interaction handle karti hai.
- ChatClient: AI model ko prompt bhejta hai.

Example structure:

```
controller/
    ChatController.java

service/
    ChatService.java
    ChatServiceImpl.java
```

Isse same service ko multiple controllers ya doosre components se reuse kar sakte hain.

## MODULE 2: ChatClient ko Service mein use karna

Q4. Teacher: ChatClient ko Service class mein kaise inject karenge?

Answer: Video mein `ChatClient.Builder` ko constructor ke through inject karke `ChatClient` object banaya gaya hai.

Example:

```
@Service
public class ChatServiceImpl implements ChatService {

    private final ChatClient chatClient;

    public ChatServiceImpl(ChatClient.Builder builder) {
        this.chatClient = builder.build();
    }

    @Override
    public String chat(String query) {
        return chatClient.prompt()
                .user(query)
                .call()
                .content();
    }
}
```

Yahan:

- `@Service` Spring ko batata hai ki ye service component hai.
- `ChatClient.Builder` Spring se inject hota hai.
- `builder.build()` se `ChatClient` object banta hai.
- `query` user se aane wala dynamic prompt hai.
- `content()` AI ka text response return karta hai.

Q5. Teacher: Interface aur implementation class ka kya use hai?

Answer: Interface method ka contract define karta hai, aur implementation class us method ka actual logic likhti hai.

```
public interface ChatService {
    String chat(String query);
}
```

Implementation:

```
@Service
public class ChatServiceImpl implements ChatService {

    private final ChatClient chatClient;

    public ChatServiceImpl(ChatClient.Builder builder) {
        this.chatClient = builder.build();
    }

    @Override
    public String chat(String query) {
        return chatClient.prompt()
                .user(query)
                .call()
                .content();
    }
}
```

Q6. Teacher: Controller se Service ko kaise call karenge?

Answer: Controller mein `ChatService` inject karenge aur uske `chat()` method ko call karenge.

```
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api")
public class ChatController {

    private final ChatService chatService;

    public ChatController(ChatService chatService) {
        this.chatService = chatService;
    }

    @GetMapping("/chat")
    public String chat(@RequestParam String q) {
        return chatService.chat(q);
    }
}
```

Example API request:

```
GET http://localhost:8080/api/chat?q=Tell me about Java
```

Expected response: AI Java ke baare mein text return karega. Exact response model aur prompt par depend karega.

Important: Video mein pehle hard-coded prompt use karke concept samjhaya gaya, phir query ko dynamic banaya gaya. Real application mein user ki query ko service tak pass karna useful hai.

## MODULE 3: Prompt Pass Karne Ke 3 Tarike

Q7. Teacher: ChatClient mein prompt pass karne ke kitne tarike video mein bataye gaye hain?

Answer: Video mein mainly 3 approaches explain ki gayi hain:

1. `prompt()` — empty prompt request se start karna, phir `.system()` aur `.user()` jaise methods se messages set karna.
2. `prompt(String)` — directly string prompt pass karna, agar aapki Spring AI version mein ye overload available ho.
3. `prompt(Prompt)` — pehle se bane `Prompt` object ko pass karna.

Exact available overloads Spring AI version par depend kar sakte hain.

Q8. Teacher: Empty `prompt()` ka use kaise hota hai?

Answer: Jab humein system aur user messages ko separately configure karna ho, tab empty `prompt()` se start kar sakte hain.

```
String response = chatClient.prompt()
        .system("You are an expert in cricket.")
        .user("Tell me about Virat Kohli.")
        .call()
        .content();
```

Yahan do messages hain:

- System message: AI ko guide karta hai ki use kis tarah respond karna chahiye.
- User message: Actual question ya instruction deta hai.

Q9. Teacher: System message aur User message mein difference kya hai?

Answer:

| System Message                                | User Message                          |
| --------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- |
| AI ka behaviour guide karta hai.              | Actual question ya task deta hai.     |
| Role, tone ya expertise define kar sakta hai. | User ki requirement batata hai.       |
| Example: `You are an expert in cricket.`      | Example: `Tell me about Virat Kohli.` |

System message response ko guide karta hai, lekin output ki accuracy guarantee nahi karta.

Q10. Teacher: Direct string prompt ka example do.

Answer: Video mein string ko directly prompt ke roop mein pass karne ka tarika bhi bataya gaya hai.

Version ke hisaab se API available ho to example:

```
String response = chatClient.prompt("Tell me about Java")
        .call()
        .content();
```

Is approach mein simple prompt bhejne ke liye alag se `.user()` call likhne ki zaroorat nahi pad sakti.

Q11. Teacher: `Prompt` object kya represent karta hai?

Answer: `Prompt` ek object hai jo AI request ke messages aur prompt-related information ko represent karta hai. Agar prompt pehle se kisi variable mein bana hua hai, to use ChatClient ko pass kar sakte hain.

Conceptual example:

```
Prompt prompt = new Prompt(
        new UserMessage("Tell me about Java")
);

String response = chatClient.prompt(prompt)
        .call()
        .content();
```

Yeh example Spring AI ke commonly used types ko illustrate karta hai; constructor aur method signatures apne project ki version ke saath verify karein.

Q12. Teacher: `Message` aur `Prompt` mein kya difference hai?

Answer:

- Message: Ek individual message represent karta hai, jaise user message ya system message.
- Prompt: AI request ke liye ek ya multiple messages ko represent kar sakta hai.

Example:

```
System Message: You are a Java teacher.
User Message: Explain HashMap.
Prompt: System Message + User Message
```

Is tarah AI ko question ke saath required context bhi diya ja sakta hai.

## MODULE 4: Call, Content Aur ChatResponse

Q13. Teacher: `call()` ka kya kaam hai?

Answer: `call()` synchronous AI interaction execute karne ke liye use hota hai. Iske baad hum response ko alag-alag forms mein retrieve kar sakte hain.

```
chatClient.prompt()
        .user("Explain Java")
        .call()
        .content();
```

Q14. Teacher: `content()` kya return karta hai?

Answer: `content()` AI ke response ka text return karta hai, yani jab humein sirf answer chahiye tab ye convenient hai.

```
String answer = chatClient.prompt()
        .user("What is Spring Boot?")
        .call()
        .content();
```

Agar sirf text chahiye, to generally itna kaafi hai.

Q15. Teacher: Kya AI response hamesha sirf String hota hai?

Answer: Nahi. Application mein kabhi sirf text chahiye hota hai, lekin kabhi response ke saath metadata, token usage, model information ya doosri details bhi chahiye hoti hain.

Video mein isliye `ChatResponse` ka concept explain kiya gaya hai.

Q16. Teacher: `ChatResponse` kya hai?

Answer: `ChatResponse` AI model se aane wale response ko structured form mein represent karta hai. Ismein generated output ke saath metadata aur generations ki information ho sakti hai.

Video mein dikhaya gaya conceptual flow:

```
ChatClient
    |
    v
call()
    |
    v
ChatResponse
    |
    +----> Result / Generation
    |          |
    |          v
    |      AssistantMessage
    |          |
    |          v
    |        Text
    |
    +----> Metadata
```

Q17. Teacher: `Generation` kya hota hai?

Answer: Video mein `Generation` ko generated assistant output ke ek result ke roop mein explain kiya gaya hai. `ChatResponse` mein ek ya multiple generations represent ho sakti hain, depending on the response and API structure.

Q18. Teacher: `AssistantMessage` kya hai?

Answer: Ye AI assistant ki taraf se generate hua message represent karta hai. Is message se actual text retrieve kiya ja sakta hai.

Video mein dikhaya gaya detailed extraction flow:

```
ChatResponse
    |
    v
getResult()
    |
    v
Generation
    |
    v
getOutput()
    |
    v
AssistantMessage
    |
    v
getText()
    |
    v
Actual Response Text
```

Conceptually, commonly used API pattern:

```
ChatResponse response = chatClient.prompt()
        .user("Tell me about Java")
        .call()
        .chatResponse();

String answer = response.getResult()
        .getOutput()
        .getText();
```

Iske exact method names aur availability ko apne Spring AI version mein verify karein. Agar sirf String chahiye, to `.content()` wala simpler approach use kar sakte hain.

## MODULE 5: Metadata, Token Usage Aur Rate Limits

Q19. Teacher: Response metadata kya hota hai?

Answer: Metadata response ke baare mein additional information hoti hai. Video mein `ChatResponse` ke metadata se chat ID, token usage, model information aur rate-limit information access karna dikhaya gaya hai.

Q20. Teacher: Token usage kya hota hai?

Answer: AI model input aur output ko process karne ke liye tokens use karta hai.

Video mein example:

- Prompt tokens: `15`
- Completion tokens: `548`
- Total tokens: `563`

Calculation:

```
Total Tokens = Prompt Tokens + Completion Tokens
Total Tokens = 15 + 548 = 563
```

Ye video ka example hai, fixed token usage nahi. Actual tokens prompt aur response ke size par depend karte hain.

Q21. Teacher: Prompt tokens aur Completion tokens mein kya difference hai?

Answer:

- Prompt tokens: Model ko input mein bheje gaye text aur applicable context ke tokens.
- Completion tokens: Model ne response generate karte waqt use kiye tokens.
- Total tokens: Input aur output token usage ka total, as reported by the provider/API.

Q22. Teacher: Token usage important kyun hai?

Answer: Hosted AI providers ke case mein token usage billing aur cost estimation ke liye important ho sakta hai. Token usage monitor karke hum AI requests ki cost samajh sakte hain.

Example:

```
Request 1 → 500 total tokens
Request 2 → 1,000 total tokens
Request 3 → 2,000 total tokens
```

Agar provider token-based billing use karta hai, to actual cost uske pricing rules aur input/output token rates par depend karegi.

Important: Local Ollama model ke liye hosted API jaisi per-token billing zaroori nahi hoti. Video ka charging example external provider, jaise OpenAI, ke context mein hai.

Q23. Teacher: Rate limit kya hoti hai?

Answer: Rate limit batati hai ki kisi time window mein API ko kitni requests ya tokens use karne ki permission hai.

Video mein example:

```
Request Limit: 500
Requests Remaining: 499
```

Ye sirf illustrative output hai. Actual limits provider, account, model aur time window ke hisaab se change hoti hain.

Q24. Teacher: Metadata kaise retrieve karenge?

Answer: Video mein `ChatResponse` ke metadata ko access karne ke liye `getMetadata()` approach explain ki gayi hai.

Conceptual example:

```
ChatResponse response = chatClient.prompt()
        .user("Tell me about Java")
        .call()
        .chatResponse();

var metadata = response.getMetadata();

System.out.println(metadata);
```

Specific fields, jaise token usage, model information aur rate limits, provider aur Spring AI version ke hisaab se available ho sakte hain.

Q25. Teacher: Agar sirf answer chahiye aur metadata nahi, to kya karein?

Answer: Directly `.content()` use karein.

```
String answer = chatClient.prompt()
        .user("Explain Java Collections")
        .call()
        .content();
```

Isse code short aur readable rehta hai.

## MODULE 6: AI Response Ko Java Object Mein Convert Karna

Q26. Teacher: AI response ko POJO mein convert karne ki zaroorat kyun hoti hai?

Answer: Kabhi AI ka answer plain text mein chahiye hota hai, lekin application ko structured data chahiye hota hai.

Example, AI se Java ke baare mein answer aaya:

```
Title: Java Programming Language
Content: Java is an object-oriented programming language.
Created Year: 1995
```

Agar humein is information ko application mein alag fields mein use karna hai, to ise Java object mein map karna useful hai.

Q27. Teacher: POJO kya hota hai?

Answer: POJO (Plain Old Java Object) ek simple Java class hoti hai jo data represent kar sakti hai.

Example:

```
public class Tutorial {

    private String title;
    private String content;
    private String createdYear;

    public Tutorial() {
    }

    public String getTitle() {
        return title;
    }

    public void setTitle(String title) {
        this.title = title;
    }

    public String getContent() {
        return content;
    }

    public void setContent(String content) {
        this.content = content;
    }

    public String getCreatedYear() {
        return createdYear;
    }

    public void setCreatedYear(String createdYear) {
        this.createdYear = createdYear;
    }
}
```

Video mein similar model class banayi gayi thi, jismein title, content aur created year fields thi.

Q28. Teacher: ChatClient ke `entity()` method ka use kya hai?

Answer: Video mein `entity()` method use karke AI response ko specified Java class ke object mein convert karna dikhaya gaya hai.

Example:

```
Tutorial tutorial = chatClient.prompt()
        .user("Tell me about Java")
        .call()
        .entity(Tutorial.class);
```

Ab response ko `Tutorial` object ke roop mein receive karne ka intention hai, jisse hum fields access kar sakte hain:

```
System.out.println(tutorial.getTitle());
System.out.println(tutorial.getContent());
System.out.println(tutorial.getCreatedYear());
```

Important: Ye object conversion automatic aur guaranteed accurate nahi maana jaana chahiye. AI output ko validate karna zaroori hai. Exact `entity()` API aur mapping support Spring AI version aur configured converters par depend kar sakte hain.

Q29. Teacher: Kya AI response ko Controller se directly return kar sakte hain?

Answer: Haan. Agar service ek `Tutorial` object return karti hai, to Spring MVC us object ko configured JSON converter ke through JSON response mein serialize kar sakta hai.

Example:

```
@GetMapping("/tutorial")
public Tutorial getTutorial(@RequestParam String q) {
    return chatService.chat(q);
}
```

Yahan example ke liye `chatService.chat(q)` ka return type `Tutorial` hona chahiye. Agar service abhi `String` return karti hai, to interface aur implementation ke return types ko bhi update karna padega.

Possible JSON response:

```
{
  "title": "Java Programming Language",
  "content": "Java is an object-oriented programming language.",
  "createdYear": "1995"
}
```

Ye illustrative output hai, guaranteed AI response nahi.

Q30. Teacher: Video mein `entity()` ka return type kaise change kiya gaya?

Answer: Pehle service `String` return kar rahi thi. Jab AI response ko `Tutorial` object mein map karna tha, to service interface, implementation aur controller ke return types ko `Tutorial` mein change kiya gaya.

```
public interface ChatService {
    Tutorial chat(String query);
}
```

Implementation ka relevant method:

```
@Override
public Tutorial chat(String query) {
    return chatClient.prompt()
            .user(query)
            .call()
            .entity(Tutorial.class);
}
```

Controller:

```
@GetMapping("/tutorial")
public Tutorial tutorial(@RequestParam String q) {
    return chatService.chat(q);
}
```

Isse poora flow consistent rehta hai:

```
Controller → Service → ChatClient → Tutorial Object → JSON Response
```

## MODULE 7: List of Java Objects Return Karna

Q31. Teacher: Agar humein ek object ke bajay multiple objects chahiye, to kya karenge?

Answer: Java mein `List<Tutorial>` use karenge. Example: AI se five programming languages ki details mangni hain aur har language ka title, content aur created year chahiye.

Expected structure:

```
List<Tutorial>
    |
    |-- Tutorial 1: Python
    |-- Tutorial 2: Java
    |-- Tutorial 3: JavaScript
    |-- Tutorial 4: C++
    |-- Tutorial 5: Ruby
```

Ye video ke example mein dikhayi gayi list mapping ki concept hai. Names aur dates actual model output ke hisaab se different ho sakte hain.

Q32. Teacher: `List<Tutorial>` aur `Tutorial` mein kya difference hai?

Answer:

- `Tutorial` ek single object represent karta hai.
- `List<Tutorial>` multiple `Tutorial` objects ka collection represent karti hai.

Example:

```
Tutorial tutorial;
```

Ek tutorial.

```
List<Tutorial> tutorials;
```

Multiple tutorials.

Q33. Teacher: Java mein `List<Tutorial>` ke liye sirf `Tutorial.class` kyun sufficient nahi hai?

Answer: Java generics ki wajah se runtime par `List<Tutorial>` aur `List<String>` jaise parameterized types ki complete type information normal `List.class` se preserve nahi hoti. Isliye video mein `ParameterizedTypeReference` use kiya gaya.

Conceptual example:

```
List<Tutorial> tutorials = chatClient.prompt()
        .user("List five programming languages with title, content and created year.")
        .call()
        .entity(new ParameterizedTypeReference<List<Tutorial>>() {});
```

Is example ka purpose ye batana hai ki expected response type `List<Tutorial>` hai. Exact overload aapke Spring AI version ke saath verify karein.

Q34. Teacher: `ParameterizedTypeReference` kya karta hai?

Answer: Ye generic type ki information represent karne mein help karta hai, jaise `List<Tutorial>`. Sirf `List.class` pass karne se list ke andar expected element type ki information nahi milti.

Example:

```
new ParameterizedTypeReference<List<Tutorial>>() {}
```

Iska meaning hai: “Mujhe ek list chahiye jiske elements `Tutorial` type ke hon.”

Q35. Teacher: List of objects ke liye interface aur service kaise change honge?

Answer: Return type ko `List<Tutorial>` karenge.

Interface:

```
import java.util.List;

public interface ChatService {
    List<Tutorial> chat(String query);
}
```

Implementation ka relevant method:

```
@Override
public List<Tutorial> chat(String query) {
    return chatClient.prompt()
            .user(query)
            .call()
            .entity(new ParameterizedTypeReference<List<Tutorial>>() {});
}
```

Controller:

```
@GetMapping("/tutorials")
public List<Tutorial> tutorials(@RequestParam String q) {
    return chatService.chat(q);
}
```

Is code mein `Tutorial` class aur required imports available hone chahiye. Agar aapke Spring AI version mein `entity()` ka supported overload alag hai, to us version ke API ke according code adjust karein.

Q36. Teacher: Kya hum kisi bhi topic ki list maang sakte hain?

Answer: Haan, prompt mein topic aur expected information specify kar sakte hain.

Examples:

```
List five programming languages.
```

```
List five Java programming books with title, content and published year.
```

```
List five Indian cricketers with title, content and career start year.
```

Lekin AI ke generated facts ko verify karna zaroori hai. Video mein bhi ek example ke liye incorrect year ka response observe kiya gaya tha. Structured output ka matlab automatically factually correct output nahi hai.

## MODULE 8: Video Ka Complete Practical Flow

Q37. Teacher: Video mein simple text response se structured response tak kya progression dikhayi gayi?

Answer: Video mein step-by-step ye progression dikhayi gayi:

Step 1: Prompt

User ka question ya instruction

Step 2: ChatClient

Prompt configure karke AI request execute karna

Step 3: Response

Text ke liye `content()`, detailed response ke liye `ChatResponse`

String

Simple text answer

POJO / List

Structured Java data

Q38. Teacher: `content()` aur `entity()` kab use karenge?

Answer:

| Method                                                        | Use case                                                                                 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `content()`                                                   | Jab sirf text response chahiye.                                                          |
| `entity(Tutorial.class)`                                      | Jab ek Java object mein response chahiye.                                                |
| `entity(new ParameterizedTypeReference<List<Tutorial>>() {})` | Jab multiple typed objects ki list chahiye aur API version ye overload support karta ho. |
| `chatResponse()`                                              | Jab response object aur uski additional information access karni ho.                     |

# MODULE 9: Important Interview Questions with Answers

Q39. Teacher: ChatClient kya hai?

Answer: `ChatClient` Spring AI ka high-level fluent API hai jo AI model ke saath interaction karne ke liye use hota hai.

Q40. Teacher: Fluent API ka main benefit kya hai?

Answer: Fluent API method chaining se code ko readable aur concise banati hai.

Q41. Teacher: `prompt()` aur `call()` mein kya difference hai?

Answer: `prompt()` request banane aur configure karne ke liye use hota hai. `call()` synchronous request execute karta hai.

Q42. Teacher: System message ka kya use hai?

Answer: System message model ko desired behaviour, role, tone ya instructions ke baare mein guide karta hai.

Q43. Teacher: User message kya hota hai?

Answer: User message actual question ya instruction hota hai jiska model ko response generate karna hota hai.

Q44. Teacher: `content()` kya return karta hai?

Answer: Ye generated response ka text return karta hai.

Q45. Teacher: `ChatResponse` kya represent karta hai?

Answer: Ye AI response ko structured form mein represent karta hai, jismein generated output aur metadata jaise details ho sakti hain.

Q46. Teacher: `Generation` kya represent karta hai?

Answer: Ye generated assistant output ke ek result ko represent karta hai.

Q47. Teacher: `AssistantMessage` kya hota hai?

Answer: Ye AI assistant ki taraf se generate kiya gaya message represent karta hai.

Q48. Teacher: Metadata kya hota hai?

Answer: Metadata response ke baare mein additional information hoti hai, jaise token usage aur model-related details, jo provider aur API support par depend karti hain.

Q49. Teacher: Prompt tokens aur completion tokens kya hote hain?

Answer: Prompt tokens input processing se related hote hain, jabki completion tokens generated output se related hote hain.

Q50. Teacher: `entity()` method ka kya use hai?

Answer: Iska use AI response ko specified Java type, jaise `Tutorial`, mein map karne ke liye kiya ja sakta hai, jab configured Spring AI API aur converters isko support karte hain.

Q51. Teacher: POJO kya hai?

Answer: POJO ek simple Java object hai jo application ka data represent kar sakta hai.

Q52. Teacher: `Tutorial.class` kyun pass karte hain?

Answer: Taaki expected response type `Tutorial` specify kiya ja sake.

Q53. Teacher: `List<Tutorial>` ko map karne ke liye `ParameterizedTypeReference` kyun use hota hai?

Answer: Ye `List<Tutorial>` jaise generic type ki information represent karne mein help karta hai.

Q54. Teacher: AI response ko Java object mein map karne ka kya benefit hai?

Answer: Data ko fields ke through access kar sakte hain aur application mein structured form mein use kar sakte hain, instead of manually parsing plain text.

Q55. Teacher: Kya structured output hamesha correct hota hai?

Answer: Nahi. Object ka format sahi hone ke baad bhi uske values incorrect ya incomplete ho sakte hain. Validation aur factual verification zaroori hai.

Q56. Teacher: `ChatClient` ko Service class mein rakhne ka kya benefit hai?

Answer: Isse controller aur AI logic separate rehte hain. Code ko test, maintain aur reuse karna easier hota hai.

# MODULE 10: Quick Revision Notes

```
SPRING AI PART 9 – QUICK REVISION

1. ChatClient:
   AI model ko prompt bhejne aur response receive karne ke liye high-level API.

2. Fluent API:
   Methods ko chain karke readable code likhna.

3. prompt():
   Prompt request configure karna.

4. system():
   AI ko behaviour aur role ke instructions dena.

5. user():
   User ka question ya instruction dena.

6. call():
   Synchronous AI request execute karna.

7. content():
   AI response ka text lena.

8. ChatResponse:
   Structured response object; output aur metadata access karne mein useful.

9. Generation:
   Generated assistant output ka result.

10. AssistantMessage:
    AI assistant ka generated message.

11. Metadata:
    Token usage aur doosri response details, agar provider/API supply kare.

12. Prompt Tokens:
    Input ke tokens.

13. Completion Tokens:
    Generated output ke tokens.

14. Total Tokens:
    Input + Output token usage, provider ke reporting rules ke according.

15. POJO:
    Simple Java object for representing data.

16. entity(Tutorial.class):
    Response ko Tutorial type mein map karne ka approach.

17. List<Tutorial>:
    Multiple Tutorial objects ka collection.

18. ParameterizedTypeReference:
    List<Tutorial> jaise generic type ko represent karne mein useful.

19. Controller:
    HTTP request aur response handle karta hai.

20. Service:
    AI interaction aur application logic handle karti hai.

IMPORTANT:
Structured response ka matlab guaranteed correct data nahi.
AI output ko validate karna zaroori hai.
Spring AI method signatures version ke according verify karein.
```

## Final understanding

Is video ka sabse important lesson hai ki Spring AI mein hum sirf AI se text response hi nahi lete. Hum ChatClient Fluent API se prompt configure kar sakte hain, `ChatResponse` se detailed response information access kar sakte hain aur supported `entity()` mapping ke through response ko single Java object ya list of objects mein convert kar sakte hain.

Aapke Spring Boot project mein practical sequence hoga: ChatClient → Prompt → Call → Content/ChatResponse → Entity Mapping.
========================================================================================================================
Is video ke detailed notes Spring AI Part 10 – Prompt Defaults ke hain. Hinglish mein Teacher Asks → Student Answers format use kiya hai, with Java examples aur interview questions ke answers. Ye notes aap directly `.txt` file mein copy-paste kar sakte ho.

# Spring AI – Part 10: Prompt Defaults

Video ka main topic: AI model ke options aur prompts ko alag-alag levels par configure karna.

1. Model options — temperature, model, max tokens
2. Global options — `application.properties`
3. Prompt-specific options — particular request ke liye
4. ChatClient default options — ek client ki har request ke liye
5. `ChatClient` ko Spring Bean banana
6. Default system message, tools aur advisors

## MODULE 1: Chat Options Kya Hote Hain?

Q1. Teacher: Spring AI mein Chat Options kya hote hain?

Answer: Chat options woh settings hain jo decide karne mein help karti hain ki AI model response kaise generate karega.

Video mein ye options discuss kiye gaye hain:

- Model: Kaunsa AI model use hoga.
- Temperature: Response ki randomness ya creativity ko influence karti hai.
- Max Tokens: Model ke generated output ki length limit karne ke liye use ho sakta hai.
- Frequency Penalty: Kuch providers mein repeated tokens ya phrases ko discourage karne ke liye use hota hai.
- Number of Completions: Kuch model APIs mein multiple outputs request karne ke liye available hota hai.

Har option har provider ya model mein supported ho, zaroori nahi.

Q2. Teacher: Temperature kya hota hai?

Answer: Temperature model ke response ki randomness ko influence karti hai.

- Low temperature: Aksar more predictable aur consistent output.
- High temperature: Aksar more varied ya creative output.

Example:

```
Temperature = 0.2
Task: Explain Java HashMap.
Expected style: More predictable answer.

Temperature = 0.9
Task: Write a creative story.
Expected style: More varied answer.
```

Ye examples general intuition ke liye hain. Temperature factual accuracy guarantee nahi karti, aur iska effect model ke according change ho sakta hai.

Q3. Teacher: Max Tokens kya hota hai?

Answer: `Max Tokens` generated response ki maximum token length ko control karne ke liye use hota hai, agar provider is option ko support karta hai.

Example:

```
Question: Explain Spring Boot.
Max output tokens: 100
```

Model ka response limited ho sakta hai. Tokens words ke exactly equal nahi hote.

Q4. Teacher: Kya sabhi chat options har AI provider mein same hote hain?

Answer: Nahi. Spring AI common options ko represent kar sakta hai, lekin provider-specific options aur support differ kar sakte hain.

Video mein isi liye `ChatOptions` aur provider-specific `OpenAiChatOptions` ka difference dikhaya gaya hai.

## MODULE 2: Global Options – application.properties

Q5. Teacher: Chat options ko globally configure kaise kar sakte hain?

Answer: Video mein `application.properties` ke through model options configure karna dikhaya gaya hai.

Example:

```
spring.ai.openai.chat.options.model=gpt-4o-mini
spring.ai.openai.chat.options.temperature=0.2
spring.ai.openai.chat.options.max-tokens=100
```

Is example mein model, temperature aur max tokens configure kiye gaye hain.

Important: Ye properties OpenAI starter ke example ke liye hain. Actual property names, supported model options aur model availability aapke Spring AI version aur provider ke according verify karein. Video mein `spring.ai.chat.options` jaise generic prefix ka bhi mention hai, lekin har provider ke liye wahi prefix assume nahi karna chahiye.

Q6. Teacher: Global configuration ka kya benefit hai?

Answer: Global configuration se common settings ko ek central place par define kar sakte hain, instead of har request mein same options baar-baar pass karna.

Example:

```
application.properties
        |
        v
Default model options
        |
        v
Application ke supported AI requests
```

Ye configuration unhi clients aur requests par apply hogi jinke auto-configuration ya custom setup mein ye properties effective hain.

Q7. Teacher: Kya global options har request ke liye final hote hain?

Answer: Zaroori nahi. Kuch settings ko specific prompt ya client level par override kiya ja sakta hai, agar corresponding API aur provider support kare.

Video mein isi difference ko samjhaya gaya hai:

- Global configuration
- Prompt-specific configuration
- ChatClient ke default options

## MODULE 3: Prompt-Specific Options

Q8. Teacher: Prompt-specific options kya hote hain?

Answer: Jab humein kisi particular request ke liye model ya temperature jaise options change karne hote hain, tab prompt-specific options use kar sakte hain.

Example: Java interview question ke liye predictable response chahiye, lekin story generation ke liye creative response chahiye.

```
Request 1: Explain Java Streams
Temperature: 0.2

Request 2: Write a creative story
Temperature: 0.8
```

Is tarah har request ke liye alag options configure kiye ja sakte hain.

Q9. Teacher: Spring AI mein prompt ke saath ChatOptions kaise pass karte hain?

Answer: Video mein `Prompt` object ke saath messages aur chat options pass karne ka approach dikhaya gaya hai.

OpenAI-specific example:

```
OpenAiChatOptions options = OpenAiChatOptions.builder()
        .model("gpt-4o-mini")
        .temperature(0.2)
        .maxTokens(100)
        .build();

Prompt prompt = new Prompt(
        List.of(new UserMessage("Explain Java Streams")),
        options
);
```

Is example mein:

- `model()` — model select karta hai.
- `temperature()` — response randomness configure karta hai.
- `maxTokens()` — output limit set karta hai, agar supported ho.
- `Prompt` — message aur options ko ek request object mein rakhta hai.

Is code ke constructor aur option method names ko apne Spring AI version ke according verify karein.

Q10. Teacher: Prompt-specific options ki limitation kya hai?

Answer: Agar har jagah same configuration chahiye, to har prompt ke saath options repeat karna inconvenient ho sakta hai.

Example:

```
Service A → same model, same temperature
Service B → same model, same temperature
Service C → same model, same temperature
```

Har request mein same settings likhne ke bajay `ChatClient` ke default options set kar sakte hain.

## MODULE 4: ChatClient Default Options

Q11. Teacher: ChatClient default options kya hote hain?

Answer: Ye woh settings hoti hain jo `ChatClient` banate waqt configure ki jaati hain. Us client se ki gayi requests ke liye ye defaults use ho sakte hain, jab tak kisi request ke liye supported override apply na ho.

Video mein isi approach ko recommend kiya gaya hai jab same `ChatClient` multiple places par use hota hai.

Q12. Teacher: ChatClient ke default options kaise set karte hain?

Answer: Video mein `ChatClient.Builder` ke `defaultOptions()` method ka use dikhaya gaya hai.

Example:

```
ChatClient chatClient = chatClientBuilder
        .defaultOptions(
                OpenAiChatOptions.builder()
                        .model("gpt-4o-mini")
                        .temperature(0.2)
                        .maxTokens(100)
                        .build()
        )
        .build();
```

Ab is client ke requests ke liye ye options defaults ke roop mein configure hain.

Important: Ye OpenAI-specific example hai. `OpenAiChatOptions` ko use karne ke liye appropriate OpenAI classes aur dependency required hain. API signatures Spring AI version ke saath verify karein.

Q13. Teacher: Prompt-specific options aur ChatClient default options mein kya difference hai?

Answer:

| Prompt-specific options                                      | ChatClient default options                             |
| ------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| Particular request ke liye set hote hain.                    | Client create karte waqt set hote hain.                |
| Different requests ke liye different settings ho sakti hain. | Same client par common settings reuse hoti hain.       |
| Har request ko alag configure karne mein useful.             | Multiple places par same client use karne mein useful. |

Example:

```
ChatClient defaults:
Model = gpt-4o-mini
Temperature = 0.2

Request A:
Default options use kare.

Request B:
Agar supported ho, request-specific option se
temperature override kare.
```

Q14. Teacher: ChatClient defaults ka main benefit kya hai?

Answer: Settings ko baar-baar repeat nahi karna padta. Code cleaner hota hai aur same client use karne wale components mein configuration consistent rakhna easier hota hai.

## MODULE 5: ChatClient Ko Spring Bean Banana

Q15. Teacher: ChatClient ko Service class mein hi create karne ke bajay Configuration class mein kyun rakh sakte hain?

Answer: Agar humein configured `ChatClient` ko multiple services mein reuse karna hai, to use Spring Bean banana useful hai.

Video mein `ChatClient.Builder` ko configuration class mein use karke default options ke saath `ChatClient` Bean create karna dikhaya gaya hai.

Example:

```
@Configuration
public class AiConfig {

    @Bean
    public ChatClient chatClient(ChatClient.Builder builder) {

        return builder
                .defaultOptions(
                        OpenAiChatOptions.builder()
                                .model("gpt-4o-mini")
                                .temperature(0.2)
                                .maxTokens(100)
                                .build()
                )
                .build();
    }
}
```

Q16. Teacher: Configuration class se ChatClient Bean kaise inject karenge?

Answer: Jab `ChatClient` Spring Bean ke roop mein available hai, to Spring constructor injection ke through use service mein provide kar sakta hai.

```
@Service
public class ChatServiceImpl implements ChatService {

    private final ChatClient chatClient;

    public ChatServiceImpl(ChatClient chatClient) {
        this.chatClient = chatClient;
    }

    @Override
    public String chat(String query) {
        return chatClient.prompt()
                .user(query)
                .call()
                .content();
    }
}
```

Yahan service ko `ChatClient.Builder` ki jagah configured `ChatClient` mil raha hai.

Q17. Teacher: Bean approach ka benefit kya hai?

Answer:

1. Common configuration ek jagah rakhi ja sakti hai.
2. Multiple services same configured client reuse kar sakti hain.
3. Service classes mein client creation ka code repeat nahi karna padta.
4. Default model options maintain karna easier hota hai.

Note: Ye example ek primary `ChatClient` Bean assume karta hai. Agar project mein multiple `ChatClient` Beans hain, to appropriate `@Qualifier` ya primary Bean configuration ki zaroorat ho sakti hai.

## MODULE 6: Default System Message

Q18. Teacher: Default system message kya hota hai?

Answer: Default system message ek common instruction hai jo `ChatClient` ke through hone wali requests ke behaviour ko guide karne ke liye set ki jaati hai.

Video mein coding assistant ka example diya gaya hai:

```
You are a helpful coding assistant.
You are an expert in coding.
```

Agar application coding assistant hai, to har request ke liye same instructions repeat karne ke bajay default system message set kar sakte hain.

Q19. Teacher: Default system message kaise configure karte hain?

Answer: Video mein `defaultSystem()` method ka use dikhaya gaya hai.

Example:

```
ChatClient chatClient = builder
        .defaultSystem(
                "You are a helpful coding assistant. " +
                "You are an expert in coding."
        )
        .build();
```

Ab is client ke prompts ke liye default system instruction configure ho gayi hai.

Q20. Teacher: Default system message ka practical benefit kya hai?

Answer: Maan lo aapki application mein 10 services hain aur sabhi Java coding assistant ka use karti hain. Har jagah same system instruction repeat karne ke bajay ek common default set kar sakte hain.

```
ChatClient
   |
   +-- Service A: Explain Java
   +-- Service B: Explain Spring Boot
   +-- Service C: Review Java code
   +-- Service D: Explain Microservices

Common default instruction:
"You are an expert coding assistant."
```

Isse repeated configuration kam hoti hai. Lekin actual behaviour model aur request ke context par depend karega.

Q21. Teacher: Kya hum default system message ke saath request-specific instruction bhi de sakte hain?

Answer: Haan, appropriate API ke through request-specific system instruction bhi configure ki ja sakti hai. Lekin default aur request-specific messages ka interaction API version aur prompt construction par depend karega. Isliye conflict hone par exact behaviour verify karna chahiye.

## MODULE 7: Other ChatClient Defaults

Q22. Teacher: Video mein default system message ke alawa aur kaun se defaults mention kiye gaye hain?

Answer: Video mein ye additional defaults mention kiye gaye hain:

- Default User: Common default user message configure karna.
- Default Tool Callbacks: Tool-calling behaviour ke liye callbacks configure karna.
- Default Tools: Client ke liye common tools configure karna.
- Default Tool Context: Tools ke liye context provide karna.
- Default Tool Names: Relevant tool names configure karna.
- Default Advisors: Client ki requests ke saath common advisors apply karna.

Exact methods aur availability Spring AI version ke according verify karni chahiye.

Q23. Teacher: Default tools ka kya use hai?

Answer: Jab AI application external functions ya services ke through kaam karti hai, to tool calling use ho sakti hai. Common tools ko client defaults mein configure karna useful ho sakta hai, taaki multiple requests mein unhe reuse kiya ja sake.

Example:

```
User: What is the weather in Pune?
          |
          v
AI decides whether a weather tool is needed
          |
          v
Weather tool provides information
          |
          v
AI prepares a response
```

Ye sirf tool-calling ka illustrative example hai; actual tool configuration aur execution application par depend karta hai.

Q24. Teacher: Advisors kya hote hain?

Answer: Video mein advisors ko interceptors ke jaise explain kiya gaya hai. Ye AI interaction ke request/response flow mein additional processing ya behaviour add karne ke liye use ho sakte hain.

Video mein `defaultAdvisors()` ka mention hai, jisse ek client ke liye common advisors configure karne ka concept samjhaya gaya hai.

Example use cases:

- Request processing mein additional context add karna.
- Chat history ya memory se related processing.
- Common request/response behaviour implement karna.

Ye use cases conceptual examples hain; particular advisor ka behaviour uski implementation par depend karta hai.

# MODULE 8: Configuration Ke Different Levels

Q25. Teacher: Spring AI mein options configure karne ke kaun se levels video mein bataye gaye hain?

Answer: Video mein four main approaches explain ki gayi hain.

| Level                        | Kab use karein?                                                                                   |
| ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Application properties       | Application ke supported default model options centrally configure karne ke liye.                 |
| Prompt-specific              | Particular request ke liye settings change karne ke liye.                                         |
| ChatClient defaults          | Ek client ke multiple requests ke liye common settings rakhne ke liye.                            |
| Service-specific client/bean | Kisi particular service ya group of services ke liye separately configured client rakhne ke liye. |

Q26. Teacher: Kaunsa approach kab choose karna chahiye?

Answer: Requirement ke according choose karein:

- Agar poori application ke supported default options configure karne hain, to `application.properties` consider karein.
- Agar ek request ko different settings chahiye, to prompt-specific options use karein.
- Agar same settings baar-baar reuse karni hain, to `ChatClient` defaults set karein.
- Agar alag services ko alag model ya behaviour chahiye, to separately configured clients/Beans bana sakte hain.

Ek important point: application properties, client defaults aur prompt-specific options ka exact precedence har setup mein assume nahi karna chahiye. Apne Spring AI version aur provider ke behaviour ko verify karein.

# MODULE 9: Important Interview Questions with Answers

Q27. Teacher: What are ChatOptions in Spring AI?

Answer: ChatOptions model aur response generation se related settings represent karte hain, jaise supported model selection, temperature aur maximum output tokens.

Q28. Teacher: What is temperature in an LLM?

Answer: Temperature response generation ki randomness ko influence karti hai. Lower temperature often more predictable responses deti hai, while higher temperature more varied responses produce kar sakti hai.

Q29. Teacher: What is the difference between global and prompt-specific options?

Answer: Global options central configuration ke liye use hote hain, jabki prompt-specific options ek particular request ke liye configure kiye jaate hain.

Q30. Teacher: What is `defaultOptions()` in ChatClient?

Answer: Ye supported ChatClient Builder API ka method hai jisse client create karte waqt common chat options configure kiye ja sakte hain.

Q31. Teacher: Why should we create ChatClient as a Spring Bean?

Answer: Taaki configured client ko multiple services mein dependency injection ke through reuse kiya ja sake aur configuration duplication kam ho.

Q32. Teacher: What is `defaultSystem()`?

Answer: Ye ChatClient ke liye default system instruction configure karne ka method hai, jab aapke Spring AI version mein ye API available ho.

Q33. Teacher: What is the advantage of a default system message?

Answer: Common role ya instructions ko multiple requests mein repeat karne ki zaroorat kam hoti hai.

Q34. Teacher: Can different ChatClients use different models?

Answer: Haan, appropriate provider configuration aur separate clients/Beans ke through different models configure kiye ja sakte hain, provided the models and APIs are supported.

Q35. Teacher: What is the purpose of `application.properties` in Spring AI?

Answer: Iska use supported provider configuration aur application settings ko external configuration file mein rakhne ke liye hota hai.

Q36. Teacher: What are advisors in Spring AI?

Answer: Advisors AI interaction ke request/response flow mein additional processing ya behaviour add karne ke liye use hote hain.

Q37. Teacher: Should we configure every option globally?

Answer: Nahi. Common settings ke liye global configuration useful hai, lekin request-specific requirements ke liye prompt-specific options better ho sakte hain.

   agar provider support karta ho.

   application.properties mein supported options configure karna.

   Particular AI request ke liye settings configure karna.

7. ChatClient Default Options:
   Client create karte waqt common options set karna.

8. defaultOptions():
   ChatClient ke default chat options configure karne ke liye.

    Common system instruction set karna.

11. Default Tools:
    Common tool configuration reuse karna.

12. Default Advisors:
    Client ke requests ke liye common advisor behaviour configure karna.

Part 10 ka main takeaway: `ChatClient` ke default options aur default system message se aap apne Spring AI project ki common configuration ek jagah rakh sakte ho. Prompt-specific options tab use karo jab particular request ko different settings chahiye. Isse code reusable aur maintainable ban sakta hai.

Is video mein hum Spring AI Prompt Templates aur Dynamic Prompts seekhenge. Is baar har concept ko sirf define nahi karenge, balki ye bhi samjhenge ki problem kya hai, code usko kaise solve karta hai, aur real project mein iska use kab hota hai.

# Spring AI – Part 11: Dynamic Templates

Video ka main topic: User ke question ko ek predefined template ke saath combine karke complete prompt banana.

## MODULE 1: Prompt aur Prompt Template mein difference

Q1. Teacher: Sabse pehle batao, Prompt kya hota hai?

Answer: Prompt woh input hai jo hum AI model ko bhejte hain, taaki model uske basis par response generate kare.

Example:

```
User: Write a Java program to check prime number.
```

Yahan user ka question hi prompt ban sakta hai.

Lekin ek problem hai: agar hum AI Coding Assistant bana rahe hain, to hum chahte hain ki AI hamesha Java mein code likhe. User ne language specify nahi ki, to model Python ya kisi aur language mein answer de sakta hai.

Isliye hum user ke question ko directly bhejne ke bajay uske saath extra instructions add karte hain.

Q2. Teacher: Prompt Template ki zaroorat kyun padi?

Answer: Maan lo aap coding assistant bana rahe ho. Aap chahte ho ki har question par AI:

- Coding expert ki tarah behave kare.
- Java language mein program likhe.
- User ke actual question ka answer de.

Agar har baar manually instructions jodoge, to code repeat hoga aur maintain karna difficult hoga.

Is problem ko solve karne ke liye Prompt Template use karte hain.

Ek simple example dekho:

Template (fixed instructions + placeholder)

You are an expert Java developer.

Always write programs in Java.

User question: `{query}`

User ki query

Write a program to check prime number.

Final prompt jo AI ko bheja jayega

You are an expert Java developer.

Always write programs in Java.

User question: Write a program to check prime number.

Ab har user question ke saath same instructions automatically add ki ja sakti hain.

Yaad rakhna:

- Prompt: AI ko bheja jaane wala complete input.
- Prompt Template: Ek reusable format jismein placeholders hote hain.
- Placeholder: Template ka woh part jahan runtime par actual value insert hoti hai.

## MODULE 2: String Concatenation vs Prompt Template

Q3. Teacher: Kya hum Java String concatenation se bhi prompt bana sakte hain?

Answer: Haan, bilkul. Simple example dekho:

```
String query = "Write a program to check prime number.";

String prompt =
        "You are an expert Java developer. " +
        "Always write programs in Java. " +
        "User question: " + query;
```

Is code se bhi complete prompt ban jayega.

Q4. Teacher: Phir Prompt Template kyun use karein?

Answer: Maan lo kal aapko aur instructions add karni hain:

- Java mein code likho.
- Code explain karo.
- Time complexity batao.
- Space complexity batao.
- Example output do.

String concatenation se ye possible hai, lekin prompt bada hone par code difficult to read aur maintain ho sakta hai.

Prompt Template mein fixed instructions ek jagah rehti hain aur dynamic values placeholders ke through insert hoti hain.

Example template:

```
You are an expert Java developer.

For the following question:
{query}

Give:
1. Java code
2. Explanation
3. Time complexity
4. Space complexity
```

Ab sirf `query` ki value change hogi; baaki instructions same rahengi.

Important: String concatenation galat nahi hai. Simple cases mein woh bhi kaam karta hai. Prompt Template ka main benefit reusable structure aur dynamic values ko manage karna hai.

## MODULE 3: Dynamic Prompt kaise kaam karta hai?

Q5. Teacher: Dynamic Prompt ka matlab kya hai?

Answer: Dynamic prompt ka matlab hai ki prompt ka kuch hissa runtime par change hota hai.

Maan lo aap ek AI assistant bana rahe ho jo different users ke questions answer karta hai.

```
User 1: Write a Java program for prime number.
User 2: Explain HashMap.
User 3: Explain Spring Boot.
```

Aap har question ke liye naya prompt manually nahi banana chahte. Aap ek common template banaoge aur har request mein alag question pass karoge.

Template:

```
You are an expert Java developer.
Answer this question: {query}
```

Runtime par:

```
{query} = "Explain HashMap"
```

Final prompt:

```
You are an expert Java developer.
Answer this question: Explain HashMap
```

Q6. Teacher: Runtime kya hota hai?

Answer: Jab application actually execute ho rahi hoti hai aur user request bhejta hai, us time ko runtime keh sakte hain.

Example:

1. Application start hoti hai.
2. User API call karta hai.
3. User ka question `query` variable mein aata hai.
4. Template mein `{query}` ki jagah question insert hota hai.
5. Complete prompt AI model ko bheja jata hai.
6. AI response return karta hai.

Isliye template ko complete prompt mein convert karne ke process ko rendering kehte hain.

## MODULE 4: Spring AI mein Prompt Template use karna

Q7. Teacher: Spring AI ke ChatClient ke saath dynamic prompt kaise banayenge?

Answer: Video mein fluent API ke saath `.user()` aur `.param()` ka use dikhaya gaya hai.

Example:

```
String template = """
        You are an expert in coding and programming.
        Always write the program in Java.
        Answer this question: {query}
        """;

String response = chatClient.prompt()
        .user(user -> user
                .text(template)
                .param("query", query))
        .call()
        .content();
```

Yahan `query` woh variable hai jismein user ka actual question aata hai.

Example:

```
String query = "Write a program to check prime number.";
```

Ab `.param("query", query)` ka meaning hai:

- Pehla `"query"` — template mein placeholder ka naam.
- Doosra `query` — Java variable, jismein actual question stored hai.

Q8. Teacher: `.param()` kyun lagaya? Sirf `.text(template)` kyun nahi?

Answer: Kyunki `.text(template)` template ka format provide karta hai, lekin humein placeholder ki actual value bhi deni hai.

Agar template hai:

```
Explain {topic}
```

Aur hum sirf template bhejte hain, to `{topic}` ki actual value provide nahi hui.

Isliye:

```
.user(user -> user
        .text("Explain {topic}")
        .param("topic", "HashMap"))
```

Yahan `topic` ki value `HashMap` hai. Rendering ke baad intended prompt hoga:

```
Explain HashMap
```

Yaad rakhna: Placeholder ka naam aur `.param()` mein diya gaya naam match hona chahiye.

Q9. Teacher: Agar `.param()` remove kar dein to kya problem hogi?

Answer: Agar template mein `{query}` hai, lekin uski value provide nahi ki, to prompt incomplete reh sakta hai ya rendering ke time error aa sakta hai. Exact behaviour template renderer aur Spring AI version par depend karta hai.

Example:

```
.user(user -> user
        .text("Answer this question: {query}"))
```

Yahan actual query pass nahi ki gayi.

Isliye correct pattern:

```
.user(user -> user
        .text("Answer this question: {query}")
        .param("query", query))
```

## MODULE 5: Multiple Placeholders ka use

Q10. Teacher: Kya ek template mein sirf ek placeholder ho sakta hai?

Answer: Nahi. Ek template mein multiple placeholders ho sakte hain.

Example: AI se personalized message generate karna hai.

```
My name is {name}.
My role is {role}.
Write a short introduction for me.
```

Java code:

```
String template = """
        My name is {name}.
        My role is {role}.
        Write a short introduction for me.
        """;

String response = chatClient.prompt()
        .user(user -> user
                .text(template)
                .param("name", "Rahul")
                .param("role", "Java Developer"))
        .call()
        .content();
```

Ab template mein do values insert hongi:

```
My name is Rahul.
My role is Java Developer.
Write a short introduction for me.
```

Q11. Teacher: Agar bahut saare placeholders hon to kya karenge?

Answer: Video mein `param()` ke through multiple values pass karne ke saath Map use karne ka option bhi explain kiya gaya hai.

Conceptual example:

```
Map<String, Object> values = Map.of(
        "name", "Rahul",
        "role", "Java Developer",
        "topic", "Spring Boot"
);
```

Is Map mein har key placeholder ka naam hai aur uski value actual data hai.

Template:

```
My name is {name}.
I work as a {role}.
Explain {topic} in simple language.
```

Map se values provide karne ka exact method aapke Spring AI version ke according check karna chahiye. Main point ye hai ki multiple placeholder values ko key-value pairs ke roop mein manage kar sakte hain.

## MODULE 6: PromptTemplate Class kya hai?

Q12. Teacher: Ab tak hum ChatClient ke andar template likh rahe the. Kya template ka separate object bhi bana sakte hain?

Answer: Haan. Video mein `PromptTemplate` class explain ki gayi hai. Iska use template ko separately represent karne aur values ke saath render karne ke liye kiya ja sakta hai.

Example:

```
String templateText =
        "You are a Java expert. Explain {topic}.";

PromptTemplate promptTemplate =
        new PromptTemplate(templateText);
```

Yahan humne template ko ek separate object mein store kiya.

Ab ismein `topic` ki value provide karke complete prompt generate kar sakte hain.

Q13. Teacher: `PromptTemplate` aur `Prompt` mein kya difference hai?

Answer:

| `PromptTemplate`                                       | `Prompt`                                                     |
| ------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Reusable format ya template represent karta hai.       | Model ko bhejne ke liye prepared prompt represent karta hai. |
| Ismein placeholders ho sakte hain.                     | Ismein actual messages aur supported options hote hain.      |
| Runtime par values insert karke render kar sakte hain. | Rendered prompt ko model call mein use kiya ja sakta hai.    |

Simple example:

```
PromptTemplate:
"Explain {topic}"

Value:
topic = "Spring AI"

Rendered prompt:
"Explain Spring AI"
```

Rendered prompt ko aage model request mein use kiya ja sakta hai.

Q14. Teacher: `PromptTemplate` ka main benefit kya hai?

Answer: Template aur Java code ko alag manage kar sakte hain. Jab same format ko baar-baar use karna ho, to template ko separately maintain karna useful hai.

Video mein `PromptTemplate.from(...)`, variable Map aur custom renderer jaise options bhi discuss kiye gaye hain. Exact constructor aur method signatures installed Spring AI version par depend kar sakte hain.

## MODULE 7: Template Rendering aur Renderer

Q15. Teacher: Rendering kya hoti hai?

Answer: Rendering woh process hai jismein template ke placeholders ki jagah actual values insert hoti hain aur complete prompt generate hota hai.

Example:

```
Template:
Hello {name}, welcome to {course}.

Values:
name = Rahul
course = Spring AI

Rendered result:
Hello Rahul, welcome to Spring AI.
```

Q16. Teacher: Template Renderer ka kya role hai?

Answer: Video mein explain kiya gaya hai ki `PromptTemplate` rendering ke liye renderer ka use karta hai.

Renderer template ke placeholders ko samajhta hai aur provided values ke according rendered text banata hai.

Video mein `StTemplateRenderer` ka example diya gaya hai, jo StringTemplate-based rendering use karta hai.

Q17. Teacher: Kya placeholders ke liye sirf curly braces hi use kar sakte hain?

Answer: Video ke according placeholder syntax renderer par depend kar sakta hai. Different renderer ya custom configuration use karne se template ka format bhi change ho sakta hai.

Lekin jo syntax aapke project mein configured hai, usi ko consistently use karna chahiye.

Important: Template syntax ko bina verify kiye change mat karna. Agar renderer `{query}` expect karta hai, to koi doosra placeholder format automatically work kare, ye guaranteed nahi hai.

## MODULE 8: Real Project Example – Java Coding Assistant

Ab saare concepts ko ek example mein combine karte hain.

Q18. Teacher: Maan lo humein Java Coding Assistant banana hai. Complete logic kya hoga?

Answer: User ka question receive karenge, fixed instructions ke saath combine karenge, aur final prompt AI ko bhejenge.

```
@Service
public class CodingService {

    private final ChatClient chatClient;

    public CodingService(ChatClient.Builder builder) {
        this.chatClient = builder.build();
    }

    public String ask(String query) {

        String template = """
                You are an expert Java developer.
                Always write code in Java.
                Explain the solution in simple language.
                User question: {query}
                """;

        return chatClient.prompt()
                .user(user -> user
                        .text(template)
                        .param("query", query))
                .call()
                .content();
    }
}
```

Example input:

```
Write a program to check whether a number is prime.
```

Application ka flow:

1. `ask(query)` method user ka question receive karta hai.
2. Template fixed instructions provide karta hai.
3. `.param("query", query)` actual question insert karta hai.
4. `.call()` AI request execute karta hai.
5. `.content()` response ka text return karta hai.

Note: Ye example concept samjhane ke liye hai. Exact API methods aur Java text-block support aapke Java/Spring AI version ke saath compatible hone chahiye.

Q19. Teacher: Prompt Template use karne se kya AI hamesha correct answer dega?

Answer: Nahi. Prompt Template instructions ko consistently provide karne mein help karta hai, lekin AI ke answer ki correctness guarantee nahi karta.

Example: Template mein Java code maanga gaya hai, phir bhi generated code ko compile aur test karna chahiye.

# MODULE 9: Interview Questions with Answers

Q20. What is a Prompt in Spring AI?

Answer: Prompt woh input hai jo AI model ko bheja jata hai. Ismein messages aur supported request options ho sakte hain.

Q21. What is a Prompt Template?

Answer: Prompt Template ek reusable prompt format hai jismein placeholders hote hain. Runtime par actual values insert karke complete prompt banaya ja sakta hai.

Q22. What is a dynamic prompt?

Answer: Dynamic prompt aisa prompt hai jiska kuch content runtime par user input ya application data ke according change hota hai.

Q23. Why use Prompt Templates instead of String concatenation?

Answer: Prompt Templates fixed instructions aur dynamic values ko separate rakhte hain, jisse prompt ko read, reuse aur maintain karna easier hota hai.

Q24. What does `.param()` do?

Answer: Ye template ke named placeholder ke liye actual value provide karta hai.

Q25. What is rendering?

Answer: Rendering template ke placeholders ko provided values se replace karke final text generate karne ka process hai.

Q26. Can a Prompt Template have multiple placeholders?

Answer: Haan. Multiple placeholders ke liye multiple key-value pairs provide kiye ja sakte hain.

Q27. What is the difference between Prompt and PromptTemplate?

Answer: `PromptTemplate` reusable format represent karta hai, jabki `Prompt` prepared messages aur supported options ke through model request ko represent karta hai.

Q28. What is `StTemplateRenderer`?

Answer: Video mein ise StringTemplate-based template renderer ke roop mein explain kiya gaya hai. Ye template ko configured syntax aur values ke according render karta hai.

Q29. Does Prompt Template guarantee correct AI output?

Answer: Nahi. Ye prompt structure aur instructions ko manage karta hai, lekin generated answer ko validate karna phir bhi zaroori hai.

# Quick Revision Notes – Part 11

```
SPRING AI PART 11: DYNAMIC TEMPLATES

1. Prompt:
   Input sent to the AI model.

2. PromptTemplate:
   Reusable prompt format with placeholders.

3. Dynamic Prompt:
   Prompt whose values change at runtime.

4. Placeholder:
   Template mein dynamic value ki jagah.

5. Rendering:
   Placeholder values insert karke final prompt banana.

6. .text(template):
   ChatClient fluent API mein template text provide karna.

7. .param("query", query):
   Template ke query placeholder ko actual value dena.

8. Multiple Placeholders:
   Multiple values ko template mein insert kar sakte hain.

9. PromptTemplate Class:
   Template ko separately manage aur render karne mein useful.

10. Template Renderer:
    Template ko configured syntax ke according render karta hai.

11. Main Benefit:
    Fixed instructions repeat nahi karni padti.
    Dynamic values ko easily manage kar sakte hain.

12. Important:
    Placeholder names aur provided parameters match hone chahiye.
    AI response ki correctness guaranteed nahi hoti.
    Exact API syntax Spring AI version par depend kar sakti hai.
```

Part 11 ka main takeaway: Prompt Template ka purpose sirf text ko chhote code mein likhna nahi hai. Iska main benefit hai fixed instructions ko reusable rakhna aur user/application ki dynamic values ko runtime par insert karke complete prompt banana. Isse AI application ka prompt logic maintain karna easier hota hai.
========================================================================================================================
# Spring AI Tutorial – Part 12: Master Prompt Templating in Spring AI

Is part mein hum Prompt Templating, System Message, User Message, PromptTemplate, Template Rendering, Fluent API aur file se prompts read karna detail mein seekhenge.

Notes simple Hinglish mein hain, taaki aap inhe apni `.txt` file mein save karke interview aur revision ke liye use kar sako.

## Module 1: Prompt aur Message Roles

### Q1. Prompt kya hota hai?

Teacher: Spring AI mein Prompt kya hota hai?

Student: Prompt woh input hota hai jo hum AI model ko bhejte hain, taaki model uske according response generate kare.

Prompt sirf ek simple question nahi hota. Ismein multiple messages ho sakte hain, jaise:

- System Message – AI ko rules aur behaviour batata hai.
- User Message – User ka actual question ya input hota hai.
- Assistant Message – AI ka response hota hai.
- Tool/Function Message – Tools ke saath interaction mein use hota hai; exact support model aur API par depend karta hai.

Example:

User poochta hai: `What is Java Stream API?`

Hum AI ko sirf question nahi bhejna chahte. Hum chahte hain ki AI simple language mein explain kare aur Java example bhi de.

Toh hum system instructions aur user question dono bhej sakte hain.

### Q2. LLM ko prompt bhejne par kya hota hai?

Teacher: Jab Spring Boot application AI model ko prompt bhejti hai, tab kya hota hai?

Student:

1. User hamare Spring Boot application ko request bhejta hai.
2. Application prompt ya messages prepare karti hai.
3. Spring AI ke through request AI model/provider tak jaati hai.
4. Model prompt ko process karke response generate karta hai.
5. Response hamare Spring Boot application ko milta hai.
6. Application response ko user tak return karti hai.

Example flow:

```
User
  |
  v
Spring Boot Controller
  |
  v
Chat Service
  |
  v
Spring AI ChatClient
  |
  v
LLM / AI Model
  |
  v
AI Response
  |
  v
Spring Boot -> User
```

Important: Spring AI prompt aur model ke saath kaam karne ke liye abstraction/API provide karta hai. Actual response generate karne ka kaam AI model karta hai.

### Q3. User Message kya hota hai?

Teacher: User Message ka use kab hota hai?

Student: Jab user apna actual question, command ya input deta hai, toh use User Message ke through bhej sakte hain.

Example:

```
Explain Java Stream API with an example.
```

Ya:

```
What is a Spring Boot REST Controller?
```

Java mein Fluent API se:

```
String response = chatClient.prompt()
        .user("Explain Java Stream API with an example.")
        .call()
        .content();
```

Line-by-line samjho:

- `chatClient.prompt()` – Naya prompt prepare karna start karta hai.
- `.user(...)` – User ka message set karta hai.
- `.call()` – Model ko request execute karne ke liye call karta hai.
- `.content()` – Response ka text nikalta hai.

### Q4. System Message kya hota hai?

Teacher: Agar humein AI ka behaviour control karna ho, toh kya karenge?

Student: Hum System Message use karenge. Ismein hum AI ko batate hain ki usne kis tarah behave karna hai, kis role mein answer dena hai aur response kis style mein dena hai.

Example system message:

```
You are an expert Java developer.
Explain every concept in simple language.
Always provide a Java example.
```

User message:

```
Explain HashMap.
```

Ab model ko dono cheezein milengi:

- System message: Java expert ki tarah simple language aur example ke saath answer dena.
- User message: HashMap explain karna.

Isse hum AI ke response ko apni application ki requirement ke according guide kar sakte hain.

Real-world example: Aap ek Java interview preparation application bana rahe ho. Aap chahte ho ki har answer mein definition, example aur interview point ho. Ye instructions system message mein rakh sakte ho.

Important: System message model ke behaviour ko guide karta hai, lekin har baar perfect ya guaranteed response ki guarantee nahi deta.

### Q5. Assistant Message kya hota hai?

Teacher: AI jo answer generate karta hai, us message ko kya bolte hain?

Student: Use Assistant Message kehte hain.

Example:

```
User:
What is HashMap?

Assistant:
HashMap is a Java collection that stores data in key-value pairs.
```

Yahan `What is HashMap?` user message hai aur AI ka answer assistant message hai.

Conversation history maintain karte waqt purane assistant messages ko bhi context mein include kiya ja sakta hai.

### Q6. Tool/Function Calling kya hota hai?

Teacher: Kya AI sirf text answer hi de sakta hai?

Student: Nahi. Supported models aur configurations mein AI tool/function calling ke through kisi defined function ya external capability ko call karne ka request de sakta hai.

Example: Ek weather application mein AI weather tool use karne ka request de sakta hai. Tool actual weather data return karega aur phir AI us data ko explain kar sakta hai.

Basic flow:

```
User Question
    |
    v
AI Model decides a tool is needed
    |
    v
Application executes the tool
    |
    v
Tool Result
    |
    v
AI generates the final answer
```

Har model/provider tool calling support nahi karta, isliye implementation se pehle uski capability check karni chahiye.

### Q7. Sabse important message roles kaun se hain?

Teacher: Video ke according, humein kaun se roles sabse pehle achhe se samajhne chahiye?

Student:

1. User – User ka input.
2. System – Instructions aur behaviour.
3. Assistant – AI ka generated response.
4. Tool/Function – Tool interaction, jab supported ho.

Practical Spring AI applications mein System aur User messages ko sahi tarah define karna bahut useful hai.

## Module 2: Prompt Templating ka Concept

### Q8. Prompt Templating kya hoti hai?

Teacher: Prompt Templating ka simple meaning kya hai?

Student: Jab hum ek reusable prompt banate hain aur usmein kuch values runtime par dynamically insert karte hain, toh ise Prompt Templating kehte hain.

Matlab prompt ka fixed structure same rehta hai, lekin uske andar ki values change hoti rehti hain.

Example template:

```
Explain {technology} with an example of {example}.
```

Agar hum values dete hain:

```
technology = Spring
example = Spring Boot
```

Toh final prompt banega:

```
Explain Spring with an example of Spring Boot.
```

Yahan `{technology}` aur `{example}` ko placeholders kehte hain.

### Q9. Prompt Templating ki zaroorat kyun padti hai?

Teacher: Har baar poora prompt manually kyun nahi likh dete?

Student: Chhote applications mein manually likhna possible hai. Lekin jab application mein multiple users, multiple questions aur bade prompts hote hain, toh same prompt ko baar-baar likhna difficult ho jata hai.

Prompt templating ke benefits:

- Reusability: Ek template ko multiple requests mein use kar sakte hain.
- Dynamic values: User ke input ke according values change kar sakte hain.
- Maintainability: Prompt ke fixed instructions ko ek jagah maintain kar sakte hain.
- Consistency: Har request mein similar structure aur instructions rakh sakte hain.
- Separation: Bade prompts ko Java code se alag file mein rakh sakte hain.

### Q10. Simple string concatenation se kya problem hoti hai?

Teacher: Agar prompt ko `+` operator se bana sakte hain, toh template kyun use karein?

Student: Simple case mein concatenation kaam karti hai, lekin prompt bada hone par code difficult to read aur maintain ho jata hai.

Concatenation example:

```
String technology = "Spring";
String example = "Spring Boot";

String prompt = "Explain "
        + technology
        + " with an example of "
        + example;
```

Ismein koi problem nahi hai agar prompt chhota hai.

Lekin agar prompt mein system instructions, output format, examples aur multiple dynamic values hain, toh concatenation complicated ho sakti hai.

Template approach:

```
You are an expert Java developer.

Explain {technology} with an example of {example}.
Use simple language.
```

Ab template ka structure ek hi jagah rahega aur runtime par values insert hongi.

### Q11. Template aur final prompt mein kya difference hai?

Teacher: Kya template aur final prompt same hote hain?

Student: Nahi.

Template mein placeholders ho sakte hain. Jab tak values insert nahi hoti, woh incomplete ho sakta hai.

Final rendered prompt mein placeholders ki jagah actual values aa jaati hain.

Example:

Template:

```
Explain {technology} with an example of {example}.
```

Values:

```
technology = Spring
example = Spring Boot
```

Rendered prompt:

```
Explain Spring with an example of Spring Boot.
```

Is process ko Rendering kehte hain.

## Module 3: PromptTemplate se Prompt banana

### Q12. Spring AI mein `PromptTemplate` class kya karti hai?

Teacher: Hum Java mein PromptTemplate kaise create kar sakte hain?

Student: Spring AI mein `PromptTemplate` class ka use template ko define karne aur dynamic values insert karne ke liye kiya ja sakta hai.

Video mein process ko step-by-step dikhaya gaya hai:

1. Template string define karo.
2. `PromptTemplate` banao.
3. Dynamic values ka `Map` pass karke template render karo.
4. Rendered content se message/prompt prepare karo.
5. Prompt ko `ChatClient` ke through model tak bhejo.
6. Response return karo.

### Q13. Ek complete example samjhao.

Teacher: Maan lo user technology aur example dono specify karta hai. Humein kya karna chahiye?

Student: Hum ek template bana sakte hain jismein do placeholders honge.

```
String template = """
        Explain {techName}.
        Also provide an example of {techExample}.
        Explain in simple language.
        """;
```

Yahan:

- `{techName}` – Technology ka naam.
- `{techExample}` – Example ke liye technology ka naam.

Ab inki values dynamically pass karenge.

```
Map<String, Object> values = Map.of(
        "techName", "Spring",
        "techExample", "Spring Boot"
);
```

Ab template ko render karenge:

```
PromptTemplate promptTemplate =
        PromptTemplate.builder()
                .template(template)
                .build();

String renderedText = promptTemplate.render(values);

System.out.println(renderedText);
```

Expected rendered text:

```
Explain Spring.
Also provide an example of Spring Boot.
Explain in simple language.
```

Is code ka main point: Template ek baar likha, lekin values change karke alag-alag prompts generate kar sakte hain.

Note: `PromptTemplate` ke builder aur rendering methods Spring AI version ke according thode different ho sakte hain. Upar ka example commonly used API style ko illustrate karta hai.

### Q14. `Map` ka use kyun kiya?

Teacher: Hum `Map` mein data kyun pass kar rahe hain?

Student: Kyunki template mein multiple placeholders ho sakte hain. `Map` har placeholder ka naam aur uski value store karta hai.

Example:

```
Map<String, Object> values = Map.of(
        "techName", "Java Collections",
        "techExample", "ArrayList"
);
```

Ismein:

- Key `techName` ki value `Java Collections` hai.
- Key `techExample` ki value `ArrayList` hai.

Template renderer matching placeholder ki jagah value insert karta hai.

Yaad rakho: Placeholder ka naam aur `Map` ki key match honi chahiye.

Example: `{techName}` ke liye key `"techName"` honi chahiye, `"technology"` nahi.

### Q15. Agar placeholder ki value pass nahi ki, toh kya hoga?

Teacher: Agar `{techName}` ki value missing ho, toh kya prompt sahi banega?

Student: Zaroori nahi. Template renderer ya API configuration ke according rendering error aa sakta hai, placeholder unresolved reh sakta hai, ya problem request execute karte waqt saamne aa sakti hai.

Isliye request bhejne se pehle ensure karna chahiye ki saare required variables ki values available hain.

Example:

```
Template:
Explain {techName}.

Value:
techName = Spring
```

Final text:

```
Explain Spring.
```

Agar required value missing hai, toh application ko us case ko properly handle karna chahiye.

## Module 4: Render aur Message mein difference

### Q16. `render()` method kya karta hai?

Teacher: `render()` ka main purpose kya hai?

Student: `render()` template mein dynamic values insert karke rendered text return karta hai.

Example:

```
String template = "Explain {topic}.";

PromptTemplate promptTemplate =
        PromptTemplate.builder()
                .template(template)
                .build();

String result = promptTemplate.render(
        Map.of("topic", "Java Streams")
);
```

Output:

```
Explain Java Streams.
```

Video mein `render()` ko un templates ke liye use kiya gaya hai jahan final output ko string ke form mein chahiye.

### Q17. `createMessage()` method ka use kab hota hai?

Teacher: Agar humein rendered string ke bajay message object chahiye, toh kya kar sakte hain?

Student: Video mein `createMessage()` ka use dikhaya gaya hai. Iska purpose template se Message object banana hai, jise `Prompt` ke andar doosre messages ke saath use kiya ja sakta hai.

Conceptually:

```
Template
   |
   v
Dynamic Values
   |
   v
createMessage(...)
   |
   v
Message Object
```

Yahan important difference:

- `render()` – Rendered text return karta hai.
- `createMessage()` – Message object create karne ke liye use hota hai.

Exact return types aur method signatures ke liye apne Spring AI version ki API check karna chahiye.

### Q18. Kya ek Prompt mein multiple messages ho sakte hain?

Teacher: Kya Prompt sirf ek message contain karta hai?

Student: Nahi. Video ke according, ek Prompt multiple messages contain kar sakta hai.

Example:

```
System Message:
You are a helpful Java coding assistant.

User Message:
Explain Spring Boot with an example.
```

Dono messages ko combine karke model ko bheja ja sakta hai.

Conceptual Java example:

```
Message systemMessage = /* system message object */;
Message userMessage = /* user message object */;

Prompt prompt = new Prompt(
        List.of(systemMessage, userMessage)
);
```

Iske baad:

```
String response = chatClient.prompt(prompt)
        .call()
        .content();
```

Samjho: System message AI ko behaviour batata hai, jabki user message actual question batata hai.

Yeh conceptual example hai; `Message` banane aur `ChatClient` mein `Prompt` pass karne ke exact APIs Spring AI version par depend kar sakte hain.

### Q19. `SystemPromptTemplate` kya hota hai?

Teacher: Agar humein system instructions ke liye alag template banana ho, toh kya use karenge?

Student: Video mein `SystemPromptTemplate` dikhaya gaya hai. Iska use system-role message ke liye template prepare karne mein hota hai.

Example system instructions:

```
You are a helpful coding assistant.
Explain {concept} in simple language.
Always provide an example.
```

Agar `concept = Spring Validation` pass karte hain, toh system instructions mein dynamic value insert ho jayegi.

Isse system instructions ko bhi reusable aur dynamic banaya ja sakta hai.

### Q20. Kya User Message ke liye bhi `UserPromptTemplate` class hoti hai?

Teacher: Video mein kya separate `UserPromptTemplate` class use ki gayi?

Student: Video mein bataya gaya ki us example mein separate `UserPromptTemplate` use nahi ki gayi. Normal `PromptTemplate` se user message ka template banaya gaya, jabki system message ke liye `SystemPromptTemplate` use hua.

Main concept: Template ka selection message ke role aur required API ke according karo. Exact class availability aur methods apne Spring AI version mein verify karo.

## Module 5: Fluent ChatClient API se Prompt Templating

### Q21. Fluent API kya hoti hai?

Teacher: Fluent API ka matlab simple language mein kya hai?

Student: Fluent API mein multiple methods ko chain karke code ko readable banaya jata hai.

Spring AI mein example:

```
String response = chatClient.prompt()
        .system("You are a helpful Java assistant.")
        .user("Explain Spring Boot.")
        .call()
        .content();
```

Yahan ek ke baad ek methods call ho rahe hain:

- `.prompt()` – Prompt preparation start.
- `.system(...)` – System instructions.
- `.user(...)` – User question.
