Student: Video mein response chain ka concept ye hai:

```
Response
   -> ChatResponse
       -> Result
           -> Output
               -> Text
```

Example:

```
String answer = response.chatResponse()
    .getResult()
    .getOutput()
    .getText();
```

Isse generated answer ka text milta hai, jab response aur output available hon. Token usage nikalna aur response text nikalna do alag operations hain.

- `getText()` → AI ka generated answer.
- Usage metadata → input/output token counts.

## Module 8: Complete Custom Advisor — Practical Java Example

Q20. Teacher: Ab `TokenPrintAdvisor` ka main implementation kaise likhenge?

Student: Neeche normal, non-streaming call ke liye example hai. Ye video ke central logic ko show karta hai: request log karo, chain ko call karo, response aur usage log karo, phir response return karo.

```
import org.slf4j.Logger;
import org.slf4j.LoggerFactory;
import org.springframework.ai.chat.client.advisor.api.CallAdvisor;
import org.springframework.ai.chat.client.advisor.api.CallAdvisorChain;
import org.springframework.ai.chat.client.advisor.api.ChatClientRequest;
import org.springframework.ai.chat.client.advisor.api.ChatClientResponse;

public class TokenPrintAdvisor implements CallAdvisor {

    private static final Logger log =
            LoggerFactory.getLogger(TokenPrintAdvisor.class);

    @Override
    public ChatClientResponse adviseCall(
            ChatClientRequest request,
            CallAdvisorChain chain) {

        // 1. Request model tak jaane se pehle
        log.info("TokenPrintAdvisor is called");

        // 2. Request ko next Advisor / model tak forward karo
        ChatClientResponse response = chain.nextCall(request);

        // 3. Model ka response receive hua
        log.info("Response received from model");

        // 4. Token usage read karo
        if (response.chatResponse() != null
                && response.chatResponse().getMetadata() != null
                && response.chatResponse().getMetadata().getUsage() != null) {

            var usage = response.chatResponse()
                    .getMetadata()
                    .getUsage();

            log.info("Prompt tokens: {}", usage.getPromptTokens());
            log.info("Completion tokens: {}", usage.getCompletionTokens());
            log.info("Total tokens: {}", usage.getTotalTokens());

        } else {
            log.info("Token usage metadata is not available");
        }

        // 5. Response ko aage return karo
        return response;
    }

    @Override
    public String getName() {
        return getClass().getSimpleName();
    }

    @Override
    public int getOrder() {
        return 0;
    }
}
```

Version note: Yeh implementation Spring AI ke Advisor API ke ek common shape par based hai. Method names, package names, aur metadata getters aapke installed version mein thode different ho sakte hain. Is example mein `CallAdvisor` hai; streaming ke liye `StreamAdvisor` ka separate method bhi implement karna hoga.

Q21. Teacher: Code ko step-by-step explain karo.

Student:

Step 1 — Logger create karna

```
private static final Logger log =
        LoggerFactory.getLogger(TokenPrintAdvisor.class);
```

Isse hum application ke logs mein messages print kar sakte hain.

Step 2 — Advisor method call hota hai

```
public ChatClientResponse adviseCall(
        ChatClientRequest request,
        CallAdvisorChain chain)
```

- `request` mein current ChatClient request hoti hai.
- `chain` request ko next Advisor ya model tak forward karne ke liye hota hai.

Step 3 — Model call se pehle log

```
log.info("TokenPrintAdvisor is called");
```

Isse pata chalta hai ki Advisor execute hua.

Step 4 — Request ko aage bhejna

```
ChatClientResponse response = chain.nextCall(request);
```

Ye main line hai. Is call ke andar chain aage execute hoti hai. Model ka response aane ke baad control current Advisor par wapas aa sakta hai.

Step 5 — Usage metadata nikalna

```
var usage = response.chatResponse()
        .getMetadata()
        .getUsage();
```

Yahan response se usage object nikala ja raha hai.

Step 6 — Token counts print karna

```
log.info("Prompt tokens: {}", usage.getPromptTokens());
log.info("Completion tokens: {}", usage.getCompletionTokens());
log.info("Total tokens: {}", usage.getTotalTokens());
```

Ye input, output aur combined token counts print karne ke liye hai.

Step 7 — Response return karna

```
return response;
```

Response ko return karna zaroori hai, taaki calling flow ko model ka response mil sake.

## Module 9: Custom Advisor ko ChatClient mein register karna

Q22. Teacher: Advisor class bana li. Ab ise use kaise karenge?

Student: Humein apne `ChatClient` mein custom Advisor register karna hoga.

Video mein existing default Advisors ke saath custom Advisor add kiya gaya tha.

Example:

```
ChatClient chatClient = builder
    .defaultAdvisors(
        new TokenPrintAdvisor()
    )
    .build();
```

Agar application mein multiple Advisors hain, toh unhe ek saath configure kar sakte hain:

```
ChatClient chatClient = builder
    .defaultAdvisors(
        new TokenPrintAdvisor(),
        new SimpleLoggerAdvisor()
    )
    .build();
```

`defaultAdvisors()` mein register kiya gaya Advisor us ChatClient ke applicable calls par run karega. Exact execution order Advisor ke order configuration aur Spring AI version par depend karega.

Q23. Teacher: `defaultAdvisors()` aur `advisors()` mein kya difference hai?

Student:

- `defaultAdvisors()` — ChatClient ke liye default Advisors configure karta hai.
- `advisors()` — particular request ke liye Advisors configure karne ke kaam aa sakta hai.

Example:

```
// Client-level configuration
ChatClient chatClient = builder
    .defaultAdvisors(new TokenPrintAdvisor())
    .build();
```

Request-level configuration ka conceptual example:

```
String answer = chatClient.prompt()
    .user("Explain Java Streams")
    .advisors(new TokenPrintAdvisor())
    .call()
    .content();
```

Request-level API aur default Advisors ke saath interaction ko apne Spring AI version mein verify karein. Agar har request ka token usage track karna hai, toh client-level registration practical ho sakta hai.

## Module 10: Application ka end-to-end flow

Q24. Teacher: Jab user question bhejta hai, tab complete flow kya hota hai?

Student: Maan lo user question bhejta hai: `Explain Java 24 features`.

1\. User / Frontend

Question bhejta hai

2\. Spring Boot Controller / Service

ChatClient call karta hai

3\. TokenPrintAdvisor

Request log karta hai

4\. LLM / AI Model

Request process karke response deta hai

5\. TokenPrintAdvisor

Response aur token usage log karta hai

6\. Frontend

AI answer receive karta hai

Example log output:

```
TokenPrintAdvisor is called
Prompt tokens: 43
Completion tokens: 200
Total tokens: 243
Response received from model
```

Ye sample output hai. Real numbers har request mein different ho sakte hain. Code mein log statements ka exact order bhi is baat par depend karega ki aap unhe request aur response ke relative kahan likhte hain.

Q25. Teacher: Kya token count directly frontend par display kar sakte hain?

Student: Haan. Lekin Advisor mein log print karne se token count automatically frontend tak nahi pahunchta.

Agar frontend par display karna hai, toh service/controller response mein token usage ko expose karna hoga. Example ke liye ek DTO bana sakte hain.

```
public class AiResponseDto {

    private String answer;
    private Integer promptTokens;
    private Integer completionTokens;
    private Integer totalTokens;

    // Constructors, getters and setters
}
```

Phir service mein answer aur usage metadata se DTO populate karke controller se return kar sakte hain.

Example JSON response:

```
{
  "answer": "Java provides several useful features...",
  "promptTokens": 43,
  "completionTokens": 200,
  "totalTokens": 243
}
```

Yeh JSON sirf example hai. Video mein token usage console par print kiya gaya hai; frontend DTO ka complete implementation video mein nahi dikhaya gaya.

## Module 11: Token usage aur billing

Q26. Teacher: Kya har model ka input aur output token price same hota hai?

Student: Nahi. Different models ke input aur output token rates alag ho sakte hain.

Conceptual formula:

\\[ \text{Estimated Cost} = \frac{\text{Input Tokens}}{1{,}000{,}000} \times \text{Input Rate} + \frac{\text{Output Tokens}}{1{,}000{,}000} \times \text{Output Rate} \\]

Yahan rates ko per-million-token pricing maana gaya hai. Agar provider kisi aur billing unit ka use karta hai, toh formula accordingly adjust karna hoga.

Example: Maan lo kisi hypothetical model ka input rate ₹100 per million tokens aur output rate ₹300 per million tokens hai. Request mein 1,000 input tokens aur 2,000 output tokens use hue.

- Input cost = \\(1000 / 1{,}000{,}000 \times ₹100 = ₹0.10\\)
- Output cost = \\(2000 / 1{,}000{,}000 \times ₹300 = ₹0.60\\)
- Estimated total = ₹0.70

Ye sirf calculation samjhane ke liye hypothetical rates hain, real model pricing nahi.

Q27. Teacher: Token usage ko database mein kyun save karenge?

Student: Agar application multiple users ko AI service provide karti hai, toh usage tracking important ho sakta hai.

Example database fields:

```
id
user_id
model_name
prompt_tokens
completion_tokens
total_tokens
created_at
```

Is data ki help se hum:

- User-wise token consumption track kar sakte hain.
- Monthly reports bana sakte hain.
- Usage limits implement kar sakte hain.
- Cost estimates calculate kar sakte hain.
- Unusually high usage detect kar sakte hain.

Important production point: Agar users ke balance se charges deduct karne hain, toh provider ke actual billing rules, missing usage metadata, retries aur duplicate requests ko consider karein. Sirf console logs ke basis par financial deductions mat karein.

## Module 12: Important code concepts — quick revision

Q28. Teacher: Is video ke important methods ka revision karo.

| Method / Concept          | Simple meaning                                      |
| ------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| `CallAdvisor`             | Normal call ke liye Advisor contract                |
| `StreamAdvisor`           | Streaming call ke liye Advisor contract             |
| `adviseCall()`            | Normal call ke around custom logic                  |
| `adviseStream()`          | Streaming flow ke around custom logic               |
| `chain.nextCall(request)` | Request ko next Advisor/model tak forward karta hai |
| `getName()`               | Advisor ka naam deta hai                            |
| `getOrder()`              | Advisor ka order specify karta hai                  |
| `getMetadata()`           | Response metadata access karta hai                  |
| `getUsage()`              | Token usage details access karta hai                |
| `getPromptTokens()`       | Input/prompt token count                            |
| `getCompletionTokens()`   | Generated output token count                        |
| `getTotalTokens()`        | Combined token count, agar provider report kare     |
| `defaultAdvisors()`       | ChatClient ke default Advisors configure karta hai  |

## Module 13: Interview Questions and Answers

Q29. What is a custom Advisor in Spring AI?

Answer: A custom Advisor is a component that allows us to add application-specific logic around an AI model call. For example, we can log requests, inspect responses, or track token usage.

Q30. What is the difference between prompt tokens and completion tokens?

Answer: Prompt tokens represent the tokens used for the model input, while completion tokens represent the tokens generated in the model output.

Q31. How do you get token usage in Spring AI?

Answer: We can read usage information from the model response metadata. The response's usage object may provide prompt-token, completion-token, and total-token counts, depending on the provider and its support.

Q32. Why do we use `chain.nextCall(request)`?

Answer: It forwards the request to the next Advisor in the chain or to the model when no further Advisor remains. The current Advisor can then inspect the response returned by the chain.

Q33. What is the difference between `CallAdvisor` and `StreamAdvisor`?

Answer: `CallAdvisor` handles normal, non-streaming calls, whereas `StreamAdvisor` is designed for streaming responses.

Q34. Why is `getOrder()` important?

Answer: It defines an Advisor's execution order. Advisor ordering matters when multiple Advisors are registered because each one may modify or inspect the request and response.

Q35. Can we calculate the exact API cost using total tokens alone?

Answer: Not always. We generally need the model's input and output token counts, applicable pricing rates, and any other provider-specific billing rules.

Q36. Can all AI providers return token usage?

Answer: Not necessarily. Usage metadata depends on the provider, model and response. Applications should handle cases where the usage information is missing or incomplete.

Q37. How can token usage be displayed in the frontend?

Answer: We can extract usage metadata in the backend and return it as part of a response DTO, along with the AI-generated answer.

## Final revision — Part 15

- Advisor = AI request/response flow ke beech additional logic.
- Custom Advisor = apni application ki requirement ke according banaya gaya Advisor.
- CallAdvisor = normal, non-streaming requests.
- StreamAdvisor = streaming requests.
- `chain.nextCall(request)` = request ko aage forward karta hai.
- Prompt Tokens = model input ka token usage.
- Completion Tokens = model output ka token usage.
- Total Tokens = prompt aur completion tokens ka combined count, jab usage metadata available ho.
- `getMetadata().getUsage()` = token usage details access karne ka conceptual flow.
- `defaultAdvisors()` = ChatClient ke default Advisors configure karne ke liye.
- Real-world use cases = usage reports, quotas, cost estimates aur billing support.

Interview mein yaad rakhne wali line:

“Spring AI mein custom Advisor implement karke hum AI model call ke around custom logic add kar sakte hain. For token tracking, we inspect the response usage metadata to retrieve prompt, completion, and total token counts.”
========================================================================================================================
# Spring AI Tutorial – Part 16: Streaming Responses in Spring AI

Topic: Blocking vs Non-Blocking, `Flux<String>`, `ChatClient.stream()`, and Custom `StreamAdvisor`

Language: Simple Hinglish (Hindi wali English)\
Format: Teacher asks → Student answers, with practical examples and code for your `.txt` revision notes.

## Module 1: Streaming Response kya hota hai?

Q1. Teacher: Spring AI mein Streaming Response kya hota hai?

Answer: Streaming Response ka matlab hai AI model se response ko ek baar mein poora receive karne ke bajay, chhote-chhote chunks mein receive karna.

Example: Aap AI se poochte ho:

`Explain Java Interfaces in detail with examples.`

AI ko 1,000 words generate karne hain.

- Normal response: Pehle AI poora answer generate karega, phir application user ko answer degi.
- Streaming response: AI jaise-jaise answer generate karega, application user ko chhote-chhote chunks bhejti rahegi.

Example of streaming:

```
Chunk 1: A Java interface
Chunk 2: is a blueprint
Chunk 3: that defines
Chunk 4: a contract for classes...
```

Actual chunks words ke hisaab se hi aayenge, ye zaroori nahi. Ek chunk mein multiple words ya text ka koi aur portion ho sakta hai.

Remember: Streaming ka main purpose hai user ko response jaldi dikhana, bina poore answer ka wait karwaye.

Q2. Teacher: Blocking response kya hota hai?

Answer: Blocking approach mein application complete AI response ka wait karti hai. Jab tak response ready nahi hota, client ko final response nahi milta.

Example flow:

User — question send karta hai

Spring Boot + Spring AI

LLM — poora answer generate karta hai

Complete response aane tak wait

User ko complete answer milta hai

Example code:

```
String response = chatClient.prompt()
        .user("Explain Java interfaces")
        .call()
        .content();

return response;
```

Yahan:

- `prompt()` — prompt create karta hai.
- `user(...)` — user ka question set karta hai.
- `call()` — normal synchronous call execute karta hai.
- `content()` — generated text return karta hai.
- `return response` — complete response return karta hai.

Important: Synchronous call mein current request-processing flow complete response ka wait karta hai. Iska matlab ye nahi ki poora Spring Boot server ya har doosri request bhi zaroor block hogi.

Q3. Teacher: Blocking response ki problem kya hai?

Answer: Agar AI ko response generate karne mein 30 seconds lagte hain, to user ko poora answer milne mein bhi wait karna pad sakta hai.

Problems:

1. User ko turant useful output nahi dikhta.
2. Chatbot slow feel ho sakta hai.
3. Long responses mein waiting experience kharab ho sakta hai.
4. Server-side resources request complete hone tak occupied reh sakte hain, depending on the application stack.

Real-world example: ChatGPT-type application mein user question poochta hai. Agar poora answer generate hone ke baad hi display ho, to user ko lagta hai application kuch nahi kar rahi.

Q4. Teacher: Non-blocking streaming response kya hota hai?

Answer: Is approach mein application response ko chunks mein receive aur forward kar sakti hai. Client ko answer ka pehla portion milne ke liye complete answer ka wait nahi karna padta.

User — question send karta hai

Spring Boot + Spring AI

LLM — chunks generate karta hai

Chunk 1 → User

Chunk 2 → User

Chunk 3 → User

Answer aate hi display hota hai

Yaad rakho: Streaming ka matlab data ko gradually send karna hai. Non-blocking processing ka matlab thread ko wait mein rokne se bachana hai. Dono related hain, lekin technically same concept nahi hain.

## Module 2: `Flux<String>` kya hai?

Q5. Teacher: Spring AI streaming ke liye `Flux` kyun use karte hain?

Answer: `Flux` Project Reactor ka reactive type hai. Ye zero, one, ya multiple items ko asynchronously emit kar sakta hai.

Normal `String`:

```
String response = "Hello from AI";
```

Ismein ek complete String hai.

Streaming:

```
Flux<String> response = ...;
```

Ismein String ke multiple chunks emit ho sakte hain, jaise:

```
"Hello"
" from"
" Spring"
" AI"
```

Client in chunks ko receive karke display kar sakta hai.

Important difference:

| Type           | Meaning                                |
| -------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| `String`       | Ek text value                          |
| `Mono<String>` | Zero ya one text value                 |
| `Flux<String>` | Zero ya multiple text values over time |

Note: `Flux` ka use tabhi end-to-end streaming experience deta hai jab server, transport, aur client streaming ko properly support karte hon.

Q6. Teacher: Kya `Flux` sirf Spring AI ke liye hai?

Answer: Nahi. `Flux` Project Reactor ka part hai aur Spring WebFlux jaise reactive applications mein bhi use hota hai.

Example:

```
Flux<String> messages = Flux.just(
        "Hello",
        "from",
        "Spring AI"
);
```

Is example mein `Flux` teen String items emit karta hai.

AI streaming mein model se aane wale text chunks ko isi tarah represent kiya ja sakta hai.

## Module 3: Streaming Controller banana

Q7. Teacher: Video mein streaming ke liye kaunsa endpoint banaya gaya?

Answer: Video mein existing blocking endpoint `/chat` ke saath ek naya endpoint `/stream-chat` banaya gaya.

Example controller:

```
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api")
public class ChatController {

    private final ChatService chatService;

    public ChatController(ChatService chatService) {
        this.chatService = chatService;
    }

    @GetMapping(
        value = "/stream-chat",
        produces = MediaType.TEXT_PLAIN_VALUE
    )
    public Flux<String> streamChat(
            @RequestParam("q") String query) {

        return chatService.streamChat(query);
    }
}
```

Line-by-line explanation:

- `@RestController` — class ko REST controller banata hai.
- `@RequestMapping("/api")` — common URL prefix set karta hai.
- `ChatService` — AI interaction ki business logic handle karta hai.
- `@GetMapping(...)` — GET endpoint define karta hai.
- `produces = MediaType.TEXT_PLAIN_VALUE` — response ko plain text ke roop mein serve karta hai.
- `@RequestParam("q")` — URL se `q` parameter leta hai.
- `Flux<String>` — multiple text chunks return karta hai.
- `chatService.streamChat(query)` — query ko service tak forward karta hai.

Example request:

```
GET http://localhost:8082/api/stream-chat?q=Explain%20Java%20Interfaces
```

Version/configuration note: Actual endpoint path video ke project se match karna chahiye. Streaming ke liye `text/event-stream` bhi use kiya ja sakta hai, lekin us format ke liye client aur response serialization ko SSE-compatible banana zaroori hai.

Q8. Teacher: Controller mein `ResponseEntity<Flux<String>>` use kar sakte hain?

Answer: Haan. Video mein controller ke liye `ResponseEntity` aur `Flux<String>` ka combination use kiya gaya.

Example:

```
@GetMapping(
    value = "/stream-chat",
    produces = MediaType.TEXT_PLAIN_VALUE
)
public ResponseEntity<Flux<String>> streamChat(
        @RequestParam("q") String query) {

    Flux<String> response = chatService.streamChat(query);

    return ResponseEntity.ok(response);
}
```

Ismein:

- `ResponseEntity` — HTTP status aur headers control karne deta hai.
- `Flux<String>` — streaming body represent karta hai.
- `ResponseEntity.ok(...)` — HTTP 200 status ke saath response return karta hai.

Agar extra headers ya status control ki zaroorat nahi hai, to simple `Flux<String>` bhi kaafi ho sakta hai.

## Module 4: ChatService mein streaming implement karna

Q9. Teacher: Service class mein streaming kaise implement karenge?

Answer: Video mein existing `ChatClient` use karke system prompt aur dynamic user prompt set kiya gaya. Normal `.call()` ke badle `.stream()` use kiya gaya.

Example:

```
@Service
public class ChatService {

    private final ChatClient chatClient;

    public ChatService(ChatClient.Builder chatClientBuilder) {
        this.chatClient = chatClientBuilder.build();
    }

    public Flux<String> streamChat(String query) {

        return chatClient.prompt()
                .system(s -> s.text("""
                    You are a helpful coding assistant.
                    Explain concepts in detail with examples.
                    """))
                .user(u -> u.text("""
                    Explain this coding concept: {concept}
                    """)
                    .param("concept", query))
                .stream()
                .content();
    }
}
```

Step-by-step samjho:

Step 1 — `chatClient.prompt()`

Naya prompt request create karta hai.

Step 2 — `.system(...)`

AI ke behavior ke liye instructions set karta hai.

Example:

```
You are a helpful coding assistant.
Explain concepts in detail with examples.
```

Step 3 — `.user(...)`

User ka actual question prompt mein add karta hai.

Step 4 — `.param("concept", query)`

`{concept}` placeholder ki value set karta hai. Agar user `Java Interfaces` bhejta hai, to template mein wahi value use hogi.

Step 5 — `.stream()`

Streaming execution path select karta hai.

Step 6 — `.content()`

Response stream se text content ke chunks deta hai, jisse return type `Flux<String>` milta hai.

Remember this chain:

```
chatClient.prompt()
    .system(...)
    .user(...)
    .stream()
    .content();
```

Video ke code ka main difference hai: `.call().content()` ki jagah `.stream().content()` use karna.

Version note: Spring AI versions ke hisaab se builder methods aur overloads mein differences ho sakte hain. Apne project ki Spring AI version ke API ke according compile check karein.

Q10. Teacher: Prompt template file ka kya use hai?

Answer: Video mein system message aur user message ko separate resource files mein rakhne ka pattern use kiya gaya.

Example project structure:

```
src/main/resources/
    prompts/
        system-message.st
        user-message.st
```

`system-message.st`:

```
You are a helpful coding assistant.
Explain the concept in detail with examples.
```

`user-message.st`:

```
Explain the coding concept: {concept}
```

Service mein resource ko load karke template use kar sakte hain. Example:

```
.user(u -> u.text(userMessage)
        .param("concept", query))
```

Yahan `userMessage` resource/template hai aur `query` dynamic value hai.

Benefits:

1. Prompt Java code se separate rehta hai.
2. Prompt ko maintain karna easier hota hai.
3. System aur user instructions clearly separate rehte hain.
4. Same template mein different concepts pass kar sakte hain.

Important: `.st` video mein use ki gayi file extension hai. Prompt files ko load karne ka exact mechanism aapke Spring AI setup par depend karega.

## Module 5: `stream()` aur `call()` mein difference

Q11. Teacher: `.call()` aur `.stream()` mein kya difference hai?

Answer: Dono AI model se response lene ke liye use hote hain, lekin response handling alag hai.

| Feature            | `.call()`               | `.stream()`                           |
| ------------------ | ----------------------- | ------------------------------------- |
| Response           | Complete response       | Response chunks                       |
| Common text result | `String`                | `Flux<String>`                        |
| Typical use        | Normal chatbot API      | Live typing chatbot                   |
| Client experience  | Complete answer ka wait | Answer gradually display ho sakta hai |
| Advisor support    | `CallAdvisor`           | `StreamAdvisor`                       |

Blocking example:

```
String answer = chatClient.prompt()
        .user("Explain Spring Boot")
        .call()
        .content();
```

Streaming example:

```
Flux<String> answer = chatClient.prompt()
        .user("Explain Spring Boot")
        .stream()
        .content();
```

Interview answer: `.call()` synchronous response handling ke liye use hota hai, jabki `.stream()` reactive streaming response provide karta hai. Streaming se client ko complete answer aane se pehle chunks mil sakte hain.

## Module 6: Custom Advisor ko streaming ke saath use karna

Q12. Teacher: Video mein streaming endpoint par exception kyun aa raha tha?

Answer: Video mein pehle custom `TokenPrintAdvisor` banaya gaya tha, jo `CallAdvisor` implement karta tha. Is advisor mein streaming method ki implementation properly nahi thi.

Jab streaming request execute hui, tab `StreamAdvisor` wale flow mein problem aayi, kyunki video ke custom advisor mein streaming chain ko forward karne wala code missing tha.

Main concept:

- `CallAdvisor` — normal call flow ke liye.
- `StreamAdvisor` — streaming flow ke liye.

Agar aap custom advisor likh rahe ho, to use jis execution path ke saath register karte ho, us path ko correctly support karna zaroori hai.

Q13. Teacher: `StreamAdvisorChain` ka kya role hai?

Answer: Advisor chain request ko next advisor ya aage ke execution flow tak forward karne mein help karti hai.

Video mein streaming request ko aage forward karne ke liye conceptually ye call use ki gayi:

```
return chain.nextStream(request);
```

Iska purpose hai incoming streaming request ko chain ke next step tak bhejna aur usse aane wala response stream return karna.

Simplified example:

```
ChatClient
    |
    v
Custom StreamAdvisor
    |
    v
Next Advisor / Model Execution
    |
    v
Flux of ChatClientResponse
```

Agar advisor ko response process karna hai, to woh stream ko aage forward karne ke baad us par supported reactive operations bhi perform kar sakta hai.

Important: Exact interface, method signatures aur registration behavior Spring AI version ke hisaab se check karein. Sirf `CallAdvisor` implement karne se streaming automatically support nahi hoti.

Q14. Teacher: Streaming response aane se pehle aur baad mein processing kar sakte hain?

Answer: Haan. Advisor ke andar request forward karne se pehle logging aur stream receive hone ke baad processing ki ja sakti hai.

Conceptual example:

```
public Flux<ChatClientResponse> adviseStream(
        ChatClientRequest request,
        StreamAdvisorChain chain) {

    System.out.println("Streaming request started");

    return chain.nextStream(request)
            .doOnNext(response ->
                    System.out.println("Received a response chunk"))
            .doOnError(error ->
                    System.err.println("Streaming failed"))
            .doOnComplete(() ->
                    System.out.println("Streaming completed"));
}
```

Ye conceptual illustration hai; ise exact compile-ready Spring AI advisor implementation na samjhein. Actual method signature aapke Spring AI version par depend karegi.

- `doOnNext(...)` — har emitted item ke liye side effect.
- `doOnError(...)` — error aane par action.
- `doOnComplete(...)` — stream normally complete hone par action.

Dhyan rahe: ek `ChatClientResponse` emission zaroori nahi ki exactly ek word ho. Isliye logging mein “response chunk” kehna zyada sahi hai.

## Module 7: Testing — Blocking vs Streaming

Q15. Teacher: Video mein streaming ko kaise test kiya gaya?

Answer: Video mein application ko alag port `8082` par run karke normal aur streaming endpoints compare kiye gaye.

Example:

```
Normal:
http://localhost:8082/api/chat?q=Explain%20Java

Streaming:
http://localhost:8082/api/stream-chat?q=Explain%20Java
```

Ye URLs example hain; apne controller ke actual mappings ke according adjust karein.

Expected behavior:

- Normal endpoint complete response return karta hai.
- Streaming endpoint response ko chunks mein emit karta hai.
- Streaming ko browser ya client mein visible dekhne ke liye client aur HTTP transport ko streaming properly handle karna chahiye.

Postman note: Postman version aur response mode ke hisaab se streaming chunks display hone ka behavior different ho sakta hai. Agar response ek saath dikh raha hai, iska matlab automatically ye nahi ki backend streaming nahi kar raha. Client-side buffering aur server/proxy buffering bhi check karein.

Q16. Teacher: Streaming endpoint ke liye `produces` mein kya use karna chahiye?

Answer: Ye client aur streaming format par depend karta hai.

Plain text stream ke liye:

```
produces = MediaType.TEXT_PLAIN_VALUE
```

Server-Sent Events (SSE) ke liye:

```
produces = MediaType.TEXT_EVENT_STREAM_VALUE
```

Difference:

- `text/plain` — plain text response.
- `text/event-stream` — SSE format, jisme events stream kiye ja sakte hain.

Agar frontend `EventSource` use kar raha hai, to SSE format aur uski event framing ko sahi tarah configure karna important hai. Plain `Flux<String>` ko SSE-compatible JSON/event format samajhna galat hoga.

## Module 8: Real-world use cases

Q17. Teacher: Streaming response kahan use karna chahiye?

Answer: Jab response lengthy ho aur user ko jaldi se partial output dikhana useful ho, tab streaming kaafi helpful hai.

Examples:

1. AI chatbot — answer gradually display karna.
2. Coding assistant — code explanation ya long code response show karna.
3. Document assistant — lengthy summaries generate karna.
4. Customer support bot — response ke initial parts jaldi dikhana.
5. Content generation — articles ya descriptions generate karna.

Normal response bhi useful hai, jaise jab aapko complete JSON object, validated DTO, ya ek hi final result chahiye.

Important: Streaming har situation mein best nahi hoti. Agar application ko response ka complete hona, parse hona, aur validate hona zaroori hai, to full response approach simpler ho sakti hai.

## Module 9: Common mistakes

Q18. Teacher: Streaming implement karte waqt kaun-si mistakes avoid karni chahiye?

Answer:

1. `.call()` use karna: Agar streaming chahiye, to appropriate `.stream()` API use karein.
2. Wrong return type: Streaming method ko `Flux<String>` ya required reactive type return karna chahiye.
3. Advisor implementation incomplete: Streaming path ke liye advisor chain ko correctly forward karein.
4. Template placeholder mismatch: `{concept}` aur `.param("concept", query)` ka naam match hona chahiye.
5. Client buffering: Client ya proxy buffering ki wajah se chunks late dikh sakte hain.
6. Missing reactive dependency/configuration: Project mein compatible reactive web support hona chahiye.
7. Unbounded response: Long responses token limits, timeouts aur resource usage ko affect kar sakte hain.
8. Error handling missing: Stream ke beech model ya network error aaye to client ko handle karna chahiye.

## Module 10: Interview Questions and Answers

Q19. What is streaming in Spring AI?

Answer: Streaming in Spring AI allows the application to receive and forward an LLM response incrementally instead of waiting for the complete response. It is useful for responsive chatbots and long AI-generated answers.

Q20. What is the difference between `Mono` and `Flux`?

Answer: `Mono` represents zero or one item, whereas `Flux` represents zero to many items emitted over time. `Flux<String>` is commonly used to represent a stream of text chunks.

Q21. Why do we use `.stream().content()`?

Answer: `.stream()` selects the streaming execution path, and `.content()` exposes the response text as a reactive stream, commonly `Flux<String>`.

Q22. Does streaming mean the server is completely non-blocking?

Answer: No. Streaming allows incremental response delivery, but complete non-blocking behavior depends on the application stack, model client, transport, and any blocking operations in the processing chain.

Q23. What is the difference between `CallAdvisor` and `StreamAdvisor`?

Answer: `CallAdvisor` is intended for the normal call execution path, while `StreamAdvisor` is intended for streaming execution. A custom advisor must correctly support the path in which it is used.

Q24. Can streaming reduce the time needed to generate the complete answer?

Answer: Not necessarily. Streaming mainly reduces the time until the user sees the first part of the response. Total generation time depends on the model, prompt, network, and response length.

## Final Quick Revision

- Blocking: Complete response aane ka wait.
- Streaming: Response chunks mein receive aur forward hota hai.
- `Flux<String>`: Multiple text chunks represent kar sakta hai.
- `.call().content()`: Normal complete-text response.
- `.stream().content()`: Streaming text response.
- System prompt: AI ko instructions deta hai.
- User prompt: User ka actual question contain karta hai.
- Prompt template: `{concept}` jaise placeholders ke through dynamic input leta hai.
- `CallAdvisor`: Normal call execution path.
- `StreamAdvisor`: Streaming execution path.
- `chain.nextStream(request)`: Streaming request ko aage forward karne ka video mein dikhaya gaya concept.
- Real-world benefit: User ko answer ka initial part jaldi dikh sakta hai.

One-line interview summary: Spring AI streaming uses the reactive `Flux` API to deliver LLM-generated content incrementally, improving perceived responsiveness, while advisor implementations must support the correct streaming execution path.
========================================================================================================================
# Spring AI Tutorial – Part 17: Chat Memory in Spring AI

Teacher–Student Q&A Notes | Simple Hinglish | Text-file friendly

Is part mein hum samjhenge ki Spring AI application ko previous conversation ka context kaise diya jata hai, ChatMemory aur ChatMemoryRepository mein kya difference hai, conversation ID kyon zaroori hai, aur kaunse Advisors memory ko prompt mein add karte hain.

## Module 1: LLM ko memory ki zaroorat kyon hoti hai?

Q1. Chat Memory kya hai?

Teacher: Maan lo user AI se baat kar raha hai. Pehle woh apna naam batata hai, phir poochta hai ki mera naam kya hai. AI ko pehli baat kaise yaad rahegi?

Student: Iske liye Chat Memory use karte hain. Ye previous conversation ke messages ko manage karne mein help karti hai, taaki agle request ke saath relevant history model ko di ja sake.

Example:

Request 1:

```
User: My name is Rahul.
AI: Nice to meet you, Rahul.
```

Request 2:

```
User: What is my name?
```

Agar previous conversation ka context nahi diya, toh LLM ko naam pata nahi ho sakta.

Lekin agar memory se previous message context ke roop mein bheja jaye, toh AI answer kar sakta hai:

```
AI: Your name is Rahul.
```

Important: LLM generally khud se previous API request yaad nahi rakhta. Application ko relevant conversation history dobara provide karni padti hai.

Q2. Kya ChatGPT aur LLM same cheez hain?

Student: Nahi, dono ko alag samajhna chahiye.

- LLM: Language model jo input process karke response generate karta hai.
- ChatGPT jaisi application: Model ke saath conversation management, instructions, tools aur doosri application logic bhi handle kar sakti hai.
- Chat Memory: Application ke previous messages ko manage karne ka mechanism.

Example flow:

```
User
  |
  v
Chat Application / Spring Boot App
  |
  |-- Previous conversation history
  |-- Current user message
  |-- System instructions
  |
  v
LLM
  |
  v
Generated response
  |
  v
Application / User
```

Q3. Kya har new request mein poori conversation bhejni chahiye?

Student: Zaroori nahi. Application apni strategy ke hisaab se recent messages, selected messages ya limited context bhej sakti hai.

Agar bahut saare messages bhejenge, toh:

- Prompt mein tokens badhenge.
- Cost badh sakti hai.
- Context window ki limit hit ho sakti hai.
- Response processing mein zyada time lag sakta hai.

Isliye memory mein kya rakhna hai aur model ko kya bhejna hai, dono important decisions hain.

## Module 2: ChatMemory aur ChatMemoryRepository

Q4. Spring AI mein conversation memory ke liye video mein kaunse do main interfaces samjhaye gaye hain?

Student:

1. ChatMemory
2. ChatMemoryRepository

Dono ka purpose alag hai.

### 1. ChatMemory

Ye define karta hai ki conversation messages ko kaise manage karna hai—jaise messages add karna, retrieve karna aur memory ki strategy apply karna.

### 2. ChatMemoryRepository

Ye messages ko store aur retrieve karne ke liye storage layer ka contract provide karta hai.

Simple example:

```
ChatMemory
    |
    | Decides what messages to keep
    | Applies memory strategy
    v
ChatMemoryRepository
    |
    | Saves messages
    | Reads messages
    v
Storage
```

Q5. Kya ChatMemory khud database mein data save karti hai?

Student: Conceptually, `ChatMemory` memory management ko handle karti hai aur repository storage ko. Actual saving aur retrieval repository ki implementation ke through hota hai.

Example:

```
ChatMemory:
"Har conversation ke last 10 messages rakho."

ChatMemoryRepository:
"Ye messages conversation ID ke against save kar do."
```

Yahan 10 messages sirf example hain, Spring AI ki universal default limit nahi.

Q6. Ye dono interfaces kyon banaye gaye hain?

Student: Taaki memory strategy aur storage implementation ko independently change kar sakein.

Example:

- Aaj application in-memory storage use kar rahi hai.
- Kal database storage chahiye.
- Baad mein custom repository chahiye.

Agar interfaces ke through design kiya hai, toh suitable implementation replace karna easier hota hai.

Interview Answer:

`ChatMemory` conversation history ko manage karne ka abstraction hai, jabki `ChatMemoryRepository` messages ko persist aur retrieve karne ke liye storage abstraction hai.

## Module 3: MessageWindowChatMemory

Q7. MessageWindowChatMemory kya hai?

Student: Ye Spring AI mein conversation memory manage karne ki ek implementation hai. Iska main concept hai ki memory mein messages ki ek limited window rakhi jaye.

Example:

Maan lo memory ki limit 4 messages hai.

### Conversation history example

Message 1

Purana

Message 2

Purana

Message 3

Keep

Message 4

Keep

Ye conceptual example hai: actual window behavior configured limit aur Spring AI version par depend karega.

Jab naye messages aate hain, toh configured window ke bahar ke purane messages memory context se remove ho sakte hain.

Q8. Kya video mein MessageWindowChatMemory ki default limit 20 batayi gayi hai?

Student: Haan, video mein implementation inspect karte waqt default maximum 20 messages ka zikr hai.

Lekin dhyan rakho: ise har Spring AI version ka guaranteed default mat samjho. Version ke hisaab se implementation aur configuration check karni chahiye.

Q9. Message limit bahut zyada rakhna achha hai kya?

Student: Har baar nahi.

Agar bahut saari history model ko bhejoge, toh prompt tokens badh sakte hain. Isse model ki context window aur usage cost par impact pad sakta hai.

Isliye memory window ko application ki need ke hisaab se configure karna chahiye.

Q10. Kya memory mein message store karna aur model ko message bhejna same baat hai?

Student: Nahi.

- Store karna: Message ko memory/repository mein save karna.
- Retrieve karna: Stored conversation messages ko read karna.
- Prompt mein add karna: Retrieved history ko current model request ka part banana.

Sirf messages store hone ka matlab ye nahi ki har request mein woh automatically LLM tak pahunch jayenge. Iske liye memory ko request flow ke saath integrate karna padta hai, jaise suitable Advisor ke through.

## Module 4: Conversation ID kyon zaroori hai?

Q11. Conversation ID kya hoti hai?

Student: Conversation ID ek unique identifier hoti hai jo kisi particular conversation ki memory ko identify karne mein help karti hai.

Example:

```
User A:
conversationId = user-A-chat
Message: My name is Rahul.

User B:
conversationId = user-B-chat
Message: My name is Priya.
```

Ab jab User A poochta hai:

```
What is my name?
```

Toh application ko `user-A-chat` ki history retrieve karni chahiye, `user-B-chat` ki nahi.

Q12. Agar hum Conversation ID use nahi karenge toh kya problem hogi?

Student: Agar application sabhi users ki history ko galat tarike se ek hi conversation mein manage karegi, toh messages mix ho sakte hain.

Example:

```
User A: My name is Rahul.
User B: My name is Priya.

User A: What is my name?
```

Galat memory management se AI ko User B ki history bhi mil sakti hai.

Isliye har independent conversation ke liye sahi ID use karna important hai.

Q13. Kya Conversation ID aur User ID same hain?

Student: Zaroori nahi.

- User ID: Kaun user hai.
- Conversation ID: Kaunsi conversation ya chat session hai.

Ek user ki multiple conversations ho sakti hain.

Example:

```
User ID: user-101

Conversation ID: chat-101  -> Java learning
Conversation ID: chat-102  -> Spring AI learning
```

Production application mein conversation ko correct user ke saath authorize aur associate karna bhi zaroori hai. Sirf client se aayi ID par blindly trust nahi karna chahiye.

## Module 5: ChatMemoryRepository ki implementations

Q14. ChatMemoryRepository kya-kya operations provide kar sakti hai?

Student: Video mein repository contract ke andar ye operations discuss kiye gaye hain:

1. `findConversationIds` – available conversation IDs find karna.
2. `findByConversationId` – particular conversation ke messages retrieve karna.
3. `saveAll` – messages save karna.
4. `deleteByConversationId` – particular conversation ke messages delete karna.

Exact method signatures Spring AI version ke hisaab se verify karni chahiye.

Q15. InMemoryChatMemoryRepository kya hai?

Student: Ye repository messages ko application ki memory mein rakhti hai.

Example:

```
Application starts
       |
       v
Conversation messages stored in memory
       |
       v
Application stops
       |
       v
In-memory data is lost
```

Video mein is implementation ke internal data structure ke roop mein `ConcurrentHashMap` ka example discuss kiya gaya hai.

Conceptually:

```
Key: Conversation ID
Value: List of messages
```

Example:

```
Map<String, List<Message>> conversations;
```

Ye sirf concept samjhane ke liye hai; exact internal implementation version ke saath vary kar sakti hai.

Q16. In-memory repository ki limitation kya hai?

Student: Iska data application restart ke baad normally persist nahi rehta.

Isliye ye useful hai:

- Learning aur practice.
- Local development.
- Simple demos.
- Aise use cases jahan permanent storage required nahi hai.

Lekin durable conversation history ke liye database-backed repository ki zaroorat ho sakti hai.

Q17. JDBC ChatMemoryRepository kya hai?

Student: Video mein JDBC-based repository ka bhi zikr hai. Is tarah ki implementation relational database ke through messages persist karne ke liye use ki ja sakti hai.

Example conceptual flow:

```
ChatClient
    |
    v
Chat Memory Advisor
    |
    v
ChatMemory
    |
    v
JDBC ChatMemoryRepository
    |
    v
Relational Database
```

Database-backed storage mein application restart hone ke baad bhi data available reh sakta hai, provided data successfully persist hua ho aur database accessible ho.

Q18. Kya hum apni custom ChatMemoryRepository bana sakte hain?

Student: Haan. Agar available implementations requirement fulfil nahi karti hain, toh `ChatMemoryRepository` interface implement karke custom storage logic likh sakte hain.

Example use cases:

- Custom database schema.
- Organization-specific storage.
- Special retention policy.
- Custom message retrieval rules.

Custom implementation mein required repository contract ke methods implement karne honge.

## Module 6: Chat Memory Advisors

Q19. Chat Memory Advisor kya karta hai?

Student: Advisor ChatClient ke request flow mein memory integrate karne mein help karta hai. Ye relevant previous messages retrieve karke current model request ke context mein add kar sakta hai.

Example:

```
User asks: What is my name?
        |
        v
Chat Memory Advisor
        |
        v
Previous history retrieved
        |
        v
Current request + relevant history
        |
        v
LLM
        |
        v
Response: Your name is Rahul.
```

Yahan Advisor application ke memory mechanism ko model call ke saath connect karta hai.

Q20. Video mein kaunse memory-related Advisors discuss kiye gaye hain?

Student: Video mein primarily ye names discuss hue hain:

- `MessageChatMemoryAdvisor`
- `PromptChatMemoryAdvisor`

Video mein vector-store-related memory Advisor ka bhi mention hai, lekin uski implementation aur configuration is lesson mein detail mein cover nahi ki gayi.

Q21. MessageChatMemoryAdvisor kya karta hai?

Student: Ye conversation history ko message/role structure ke saath prompt mein add karne ke liye use hota hai.

Example:

```
System: You are a Java coding assistant.
User: Explain HashMap.
Assistant: HashMap stores key-value pairs.
User: Give me an example.
```

Yahan previous messages apne respective roles ke saath represent kiye ja sakte hain.

Important: Role-aware messages model ko conversation structure samajhne mein help karte hain. Lekin supported message roles aur exact behavior selected model aur Spring AI version par depend kar sakte hain.

Q22. PromptChatMemoryAdvisor kya karta hai?

Student: Video ke explanation ke according, ye previous conversation ko prompt ke andar text-based context ke roop mein add karta hai.

Conceptual example:

```
Previous conversation:
User: Explain Java Streams.
Assistant: Java Streams help process collections.

Current question:
Give me one example.
```

Advisor history ko text ke form mein prompt mein include kar sakta hai, jisse model ko previous discussion ka context mil sake.

Q23. MessageChatMemoryAdvisor aur PromptChatMemoryAdvisor mein kya difference hai?

| Point                  | MessageChatMemoryAdvisor                                    | PromptChatMemoryAdvisor                                         |
| ---------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| History representation | Message/role structure                                      | Prompt mein text-based history                                  |
| Purpose                | Conversation history ko messages ke roop mein include karna | Conversation history ko text context ke roop mein include karna |
| Choice                 | Jab structured message history suitable ho                  | Jab text-based context suitable ho                              |

Video ka main point ye hai ki application aur model ke supported behavior ke hisaab se suitable Advisor choose karna chahiye. Exact implementation details Spring AI version ke saath verify karein.

## Module 7: Chat Memory ka complete flow

Q24. Jab user new question poochta hai, toh memory kaise kaam karti hai?

Student: Step-by-step flow samjho.

Step 1: User sends a message

Example: "What is my name?"

Step 2: ChatClient request starts

Current question is prepared.

Step 3: Memory Advisor retrieves history

Conversation ID is used to find the relevant messages.

Step 4: Memory and current message are combined

Relevant context is prepared for the model.

Step 5: LLM generates a response

The model answers using the supplied context.

Step 6: Conversation history is updated

The memory flow manages the messages according to its configuration.

Note: Actual retrieval, save timing, and Advisor execution order depend on the configured components and Spring AI version.

## Module 8: Practical example samjho

Q25. Java learning chatbot mein Chat Memory kaise useful hogi?

Student: Maan lo user Java concepts ke baare mein continuous questions pooch raha hai.

Conversation:

```
User: I am learning Java 8.
Assistant: Great! Java 8 introduced many useful features.

User: Explain Streams.
Assistant: Streams help process data from collections.

User: Give me an example.
Assistant: Here is a Java Stream example...
```

Last question, `"Give me an example"`, apne aap mein incomplete hai. Lekin previous history ke saath AI samajh sakta hai ki user Java Streams ka example maang raha hai.

Iske liye application ko previous conversation ka relevant context model ko dena hoga.

Q26. Kya Chat Memory aur RAG same hain?

Student: Nahi, dono ka purpose alag hai.

| Chat Memory                            | RAG                                                                |
| -------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Conversation history manage karti hai  | External documents/data se relevant information retrieve karta hai |
| Example: user ne pehle kya poochha tha | Example: company ke PDF mein leave policy kya hai                  |
| Conversation continuity ke liye useful | Knowledge retrieval ke liye useful                                 |

Dono ek hi application mein saath use kiye ja sakte hain.

## Module 9: Important interview questions

Q27. What is Chat Memory in Spring AI?

Student: Chat Memory is a mechanism for managing conversation history so that relevant previous messages can be included in subsequent model requests. This helps maintain conversational context across requests.

Q28. What is the difference between ChatMemory and ChatMemoryRepository?

Student: `ChatMemory` manages conversation memory, while `ChatMemoryRepository` provides the storage abstraction used to save and retrieve conversation messages.

Q29. Why is Conversation ID important?

Student: A Conversation ID identifies a particular conversation. It helps retrieve the correct history and prevents different conversations from being mixed together when the application manages IDs properly.

Q30. What is MessageWindowChatMemory?

Student: It is a ChatMemory implementation that maintains a bounded window of conversation messages, helping control how much history is retained for conversational context.

Q31. What is InMemoryChatMemoryRepository?

Student: It is a repository implementation that stores conversation messages in application memory. Its data is generally lost when the application stops, so it is not suitable when durable persistence is required.

Q32. Why should we limit the number of messages sent to an LLM?

Student: More messages can increase token usage, cost and context-window consumption. A suitable memory limit helps balance conversational continuity and resource usage.

Q33. What is the role of a Chat Memory Advisor?

Student: A Chat Memory Advisor integrates conversation history with the ChatClient request flow so that relevant previous messages can be supplied to the model.

Q34. Can we implement a custom ChatMemoryRepository?

Student: Yes. We can implement the repository interface and provide our own message storage and retrieval logic when the available implementations do not meet the application's requirements.

Q35. Does an LLM automatically remember previous API requests?

Student: Generally, no. The application must provide relevant previous context to the model, for example through a chat memory mechanism.

## Module 10: Quick revision notes

- LLM Statelessness: Generally, the model does not automatically remember earlier independent requests.
- ChatMemory: Manages conversation history and memory strategy.
- ChatMemoryRepository: Provides storage and retrieval functionality.
- MessageWindowChatMemory: Keeps a bounded window of messages.
- Conversation ID: Identifies a conversation's history.
- InMemoryChatMemoryRepository: Stores messages in application memory; generally not durable across restarts.
- JDBC repository: Can provide database-backed persistence.
- MessageChatMemoryAdvisor: Adds history as structured messages.
- PromptChatMemoryAdvisor: Adds history as text-based prompt context.
- Token usage: More conversation history can mean more prompt tokens and higher cost.
- Security: Keep each user's history isolated and authorize access to conversation IDs.

Final concept to remember:

```
ChatMemory = How conversation history is managed

ChatMemoryRepository = Where/how messages are stored and retrieved

Conversation ID = Which conversation's history to use

Chat Memory Advisor = How history is integrated into the model request
```

These notes cover the concepts explained in the supplied Part 17 transcript. The next lesson in the video series is expected to focus on implementation, so treat the names and behavior of specific classes as version-dependent until you verify them against the Spring AI version used in your project.

# Spring AI Tutorial – Part 18: Practical Implementation of Chat Memory

Teacher–Student Q&A Notes | Simple Hinglish | Practical Spring Boot Example

Is part mein hum previous lesson ki theory ko practical code se connect karenge. Hum dekhenge ki bina Chat Memory ke AI previous conversation kyon bhool jata hai, aur MessageWindowChatMemory + InMemoryChatMemoryRepository + MessageChatMemoryAdvisor use karke conversation history kaise maintain karte hain.

## Module 1: Practical project mein problem samjho

Q1. Video mein kaunsa project banaya gaya hai?

Student: Video mein pehle wale Advisor project ko copy karke ek naya project banaya gaya, jiska naam Memory App rakha gaya.

Application mein pehle se ye cheezein thi:

- Spring Boot application
- Spring AI `ChatClient`
- OpenAI model configuration
- System prompt
- Chat endpoint
- Pehle se banaye gaye Advisors, jaise token-printing aur safeguard-related Advisor

Video mein application ko port `8082` par run kiya gaya.

Q2. Memory implement karne se pehle application ka behavior kya tha?

Student: Application har request par AI se answer le rahi thi, lekin previous conversation ka context automatically maintain nahi ho raha tha.

Example:

```
Request 1
User: My name is John.
AI: Nice to meet you, John.

Request 2
User: What is my name?
AI: I don't know your name.
```

Kyunki second request ke saath pehli request ki conversation history nahi di ja rahi thi.

Q3. Kya System Prompt hone se AI previous conversation yaad rakhta hai?

Student: Nahi.

Example system prompt:

```
You are a helpful coding assistant.
Explain the concept in detail with examples.
```

Ye AI ko batata hai ki uska behavior kaisa hona chahiye. Lekin ye previous requests ko automatically store nahi karta.

Yaad rakho:

- System Prompt = AI ko kaise behave karna hai.
- Chat Memory = Previous conversation ka relevant context manage karna.
- Chat Memory Advisor = Memory ko model request ke saath integrate karna.

## Module 2: Video mein model configuration ka issue

Q4. Video mein model options change karne par kya problem aayi?

Student: Video mein OpenAI model `GPT-5 Nano` use karne ki koshish ki gayi. Model configuration mein `maxTokens` aur `temperature` options ke saath errors aaye.

Transcript ke according:

- Model ne configured `maxTokens` option ko support nahi kiya.
- `temperature = 0.3` bhi accept nahi hua.
- Error ke baad video mein unsupported options ko remove/change karke request dobara try ki gayi.

Important lesson: Har model har configuration option ko support nahi karta. Options ko selected model aur uske current API documentation ke hisaab se configure karna chahiye.

Example properties ka conceptual idea:

```
# Exact option names and supported values
# depend on the selected model and Spring AI version.
spring.ai.openai.chat.options.model=YOUR_MODEL_NAME
```

Ye sirf configuration ka example hai; apne model ka actual supported name aur options verify karo.

Q5. Kya `maxTokens` aur `temperature` har model mein same tarah kaam karte hain?

Student: Nahi. Model ke hisaab se supported options aur unke behavior mein difference ho sakta hai.

- `maxTokens` ya equivalent output-limit option response length control karne mein help karta hai, agar model support karta ho.
- `temperature` generation randomness ko influence karta hai, agar selected model is option ko support karta ho.

Video ka practical lesson hai: configuration error aaye toh pehle check karo ki selected model us option ko support karta hai ya nahi.

## Module 3: Chat Memory implement karne ke liye required components

Q6. Video mein kaunse three main components use kiye gaye hain?

Student: Video mein ye three components important hain:

### 1. MessageWindowChatMemory

Conversation history mein kitne messages retain karne hain, uski memory-window strategy manage karta hai.

### 2. InMemoryChatMemoryRepository

Messages ko application memory mein store aur retrieve karta hai. Application restart hone par data generally lost ho jata hai.

### 3. MessageChatMemoryAdvisor

Conversation history ko ChatClient ke model request flow ke saath integrate karta hai.

In teeno ka kaam alag hai. Inko ek hi cheez mat samajhna.

Q7. In teeno ka relationship kya hai?

Student: Is flow se samjho:

```
User sends a message
        |
        v
ChatClient
        |
        v
MessageChatMemoryAdvisor
        |
        v
MessageWindowChatMemory
        |
        v
InMemoryChatMemoryRepository
        |
        v
Conversation history retrieved
        |
        v
Current request + previous history
        |
        v
LLM generates response
```

Actual Advisor execution aur saving ka exact order Spring AI version aur configuration par depend kar sakta hai.

Q8. Kya video mein database use kiya gaya hai?

Student: Nahi. Is practical example mein in-memory repository use ki gayi hai.

Iska matlab messages application ki RAM mein maintain hote hain, permanent database mein nahi.

Video mein JDBC-backed repository ko future lesson ke liye mention kiya gaya hai.

## Module 4: Chat Memory ka configuration code

Q9. Sabse pehle ChatMemory ko Spring Bean ke roop mein kaise configure kar sakte hain?

Student: Video mein `MessageWindowChatMemory` ki implementation aur uski repository ko use kiya gaya hai. Concept samajhne ke liye ek representative configuration dekho.

```
@Configuration
public class MemoryConfig {

    @Bean
    public ChatMemoryRepository chatMemoryRepository() {
        return new InMemoryChatMemoryRepository();
    }

    @Bean
    public ChatMemory chatMemory(
            ChatMemoryRepository repository) {

        return MessageWindowChatMemory.builder()
                .chatMemoryRepository(repository)
                .maxMessages(20)
                .build();
    }
}
```

Line-by-line explanation:

1. `@Configuration` Spring ko batata hai ki class mein configuration/bean definitions hain.
2. `@Bean` se repository object Spring container mein register hota hai.
3. `InMemoryChatMemoryRepository` messages ko application memory mein store karta hai.
4. `ChatMemory` bean conversation memory manage karta hai.
5. `.chatMemoryRepository(repository)` memory ko storage implementation ke saath connect karta hai.
6. `.maxMessages(20)` memory window ki limit set karta hai.
7. `.build()` configured object create karta hai.

Version note: Ye representative code hai. Builder methods aur constructor APIs Spring AI version ke hisaab se differ kar sakte hain. Video mein configured `MessageWindowChatMemory` ki implementation class ko log karke verify bhi kiya gaya tha. Agar aapke version mein repository auto-configured hai, toh duplicate bean create karne ke bajaye existing configuration check karein.

Q10. `@Bean` aur dependency injection ka benefit kya hai?

Student: Object ko har jagah manually create karne ke bajaye Spring usko manage kar sakta hai.

Example:

```
public MemoryService(ChatMemory chatMemory) {
    this.chatMemory = chatMemory;
}
```

Yahan Spring suitable `ChatMemory` bean inject karta hai, provided ek unambiguous matching bean available ho.

Isse code maintain karna aur implementation change karna easier hota hai.

## Module 5: MessageChatMemoryAdvisor configure karna

Q11. Video mein Advisor kaise create kiya gaya?

Student: Video mein `MessageChatMemoryAdvisor.builder()` use karke `ChatMemory` pass ki gayi aur phir Advisor ko existing Advisors ki list mein add kiya gaya.

Conceptual configuration:

```
@Bean
public ChatClient chatClient(
        ChatClient.Builder builder,
        ChatMemory chatMemory) {

    MessageChatMemoryAdvisor memoryAdvisor =
            MessageChatMemoryAdvisor.builder(chatMemory)
                    .build();

    return builder
            .defaultAdvisors(memoryAdvisor)
            .build();
}
```

Explanation:

- `ChatClient.Builder` se ChatClient configure hota hai.
- `ChatMemory` memory strategy provide karta hai.
- `MessageChatMemoryAdvisor` history ko request ke saath integrate karta hai.
- `defaultAdvisors(...)` is Advisor ko is ChatClient ke default request flow mein register karta hai.

Agar application mein pehle se token-tracking aur safeguard Advisors hain, toh unko bhi configuration mein preserve karna hoga. Sirf memory Advisor add karne ke liye existing Advisors ko accidentally remove mat karna.

Q12. Video mein constructor directly use karne ke bajaye Builder kyon use kiya gaya?

Student: Video mein bataya gaya ki Advisor ka constructor directly accessible nahi tha, isliye `.builder(...)` approach use ki gayi.

Builder configuration ko readable banata hai aur implementation details ko hide kar sakta hai.

Important: Actual builder signature apne Spring AI version ke according check karna.

## Module 6: Conversation ID ke saath memory manage karna

Q13. Ek user ki conversation ko doosre user se alag kaise rakhenge?

Student: Har conversation ke liye stable aur appropriate conversation ID pass karni chahiye.

Conceptual request:

```
POST /chat?conversationId=chat-101&q=My name is John
POST /chat?conversationId=chat-101&q=What is my name?
```

Dono requests same conversation ko refer kar rahi hain.

Lekin:

```
POST /chat?conversationId=chat-202&q=What is my name?
```

Ye doosri conversation hai, isliye iski history alag honi chahiye.

Production warning: Video mein end-to-end implementation ke saare controller parameters transcript mein clearly specified nahi hain. Upar ka URL sirf conceptual example hai. Real application mein conversation ID ko authenticated user ke saath validate/associate karein; arbitrary IDs accept karne se history leak ho sakti hai.

## Module 7: Practical testing — AI ko naam yaad kaise rahega?

Q14. Memory implement karne ke baad pehla test kya kiya gaya?

Student: Video mein pehle user ne apna naam diya:

```
User: My name is John.
AI: Nice to meet you, John.
```

Phir same conversation mein user ne poochha:

```
User: What is my name?
AI: Your name is John.
```

Pehle memory nahi thi, toh AI previous request ka naam nahi jaanta tha. Memory Advisor add karne ke baad relevant history next request mein include ho sakti hai.

Q15. Kya AI ko sirf user ke messages yaad rehte hain?

Student: Zaroori nahi. Conversation history mein user messages aur assistant ke previous responses dono include ho sakte hain.

Example:

```
System: You are a helpful coding assistant.

User: My name is Amit.
Assistant: Nice to meet you, Amit.

User: What is my name?
```

Model ko current question ke saath previous messages milte hain, isliye woh answer de sakta hai.

Video mein logs inspect karke dikhaya gaya ki previous user message aur assistant response current prompt context mein include ho rahe the.

Q16. Kya Chat Memory sirf names yaad rakhne ke liye useful hai?

Student: Nahi. Ye multi-turn conversation mein previous topic aur instructions ka context maintain karne mein bhi useful hai.

Video ka coding example:

```
User: What is a prime number?
AI: A prime number is an integer greater than 1
    that has exactly two positive divisors.

User: Write the program also.
AI: Here is a program to check a prime number...
```

Second question `"Write the program also"` incomplete hai. Previous conversation ki wajah se model samajh sakta hai ki prime number ka program chahiye.

Aage user keh sakta hai:

```
User: Write it in Java.
```

Agar relevant history prompt mein available hai, toh AI previous prime-number task ko continue kar sakta hai.

Important: Memory history provide karti hai; model ka answer phir bhi galat ho sakta hai. Memory correctness ki guarantee nahi hai.

## Module 8: Logs mein behind-the-scenes kya dekha gaya?

Q17. Video mein SimpleLoggerAdvisor ka use kyon kiya gaya?

Student: Request aur response ka flow inspect karne ke liye.

Video mein pehle custom token-printing Advisor use hua, phir request details dekhne ke liye `SimpleLoggerAdvisor` ka example dikhaya gaya.

Isse developer ko samajhne mein help milti hai ki model ko actual request mein kya context bheja ja raha hai.

Q18. Logs mein kya-kya dekhne ko mila?

Student: Video ke according, request prompt mein ye cheezein thi:

1. System message — coding assistant ki instructions.
2. Previous user message — jaise `My name is Amit`.
3. Previous assistant response — jaise `Nice to meet you, Amit`.
4. Current user message — jaise `What is my name?`.
5. Model request options — selected model aur supported configuration.

Iska matlab model ko sirf current question nahi, relevant previous conversation bhi bheji ja rahi thi.

Q19. Agar memory enabled hai toh kya purani history har request mein model ko bheji jati hai?

Student: Memory Advisor relevant retained history ko request context mein include kar sakta hai. Lekin exactly kaunse messages aur kitne messages include honge, woh memory configuration, conversation ID aur implementation par depend karta hai.

Isliye logs inspect karna useful hai.

Security tip: Production mein full prompts log karne se personal data, confidential information aur API content expose ho sakta hai. Logging ko carefully configure karein.

## Module 9: Token usage aur Chat Memory ki limitation

Q20. Memory ki wajah se token usage kyon badhta hai?

Student: Kyunki previous conversation ka context bhi prompt ka part ban sakta hai.

Example:

```
Current question:
What is my name?

Previous history:
User: My name is Amit.
Assistant: Nice to meet you, Amit.
User: Explain Java variables.
Assistant: ...
```

Current question chhota hai, lekin uske saath previous history bhi send ho sakti hai.

Isliye model ko process karne ke liye zyada input tokens lag sakte hain.

Q21. Video mein token usage ke baare mein kya observe kiya gaya?

Student: Transcript mein ek request ke liye lagbhag 874 prompt tokens aur 1,300 total tokens ka example discuss hua.

Ye sirf video mein dikhaye gaye particular request ka observation hai; har request ka token count itna hi hoga, aisa nahi hai.

Token usage depend karta hai:

- Conversation history ki length.
- System prompt.
- Current user message.
- Model ki tokenization.
- Generated response aur model-specific accounting.

Q22. Kya memory window ko 20 se 100 kar dena best solution hai?

Student: Nahi. Badi window se zyada history available hogi, lekin prompt tokens bhi badh sakte hain.

Better strategy application ke use case par depend karti hai:

- Sirf recent context chahiye toh limited message window.
- Long-term facts ya large document knowledge chahiye toh appropriate retrieval/storage architecture.
- Very long conversation ho toh summarization aur selective retrieval consider kar sakte hain.

Video mein future improvement ke roop mein vector databases ka mention hai. Lekin Chat Memory aur vector-based retrieval alag concepts hain; vector database automatically har chat-memory problem solve nahi karta.

## Module 10: Interview Q&A

Q23. How do you implement Chat Memory in Spring AI?

Student: I configure a `ChatMemory` implementation, provide a suitable `ChatMemoryRepository`, and integrate the memory with `ChatClient` using a memory Advisor such as `MessageChatMemoryAdvisor`. The Advisor helps include relevant conversation history in subsequent model requests.

Q24. What is the purpose of MessageWindowChatMemory?

Student: It maintains a bounded window of conversation messages so that the application can retain relevant recent history without including unlimited conversation context in every request.

Q25. What is the difference between in-memory and database-backed chat memory?

Student: In-memory chat memory stores messages in application memory and generally loses them after a restart. Database-backed storage can persist messages across restarts, provided the data is saved successfully and the database remains accessible.

Q26. How does the AI remember a previous answer?

Student: The application retrieves previous conversation messages using the configured memory mechanism and supplies relevant history along with the current request. The model then generates a response using that context.

Q27. What are the limitations of Chat Memory?

Student: Chat Memory can increase token usage and cost, can be limited by the context window, and requires correct conversation isolation. It also does not guarantee that the model will always interpret previous messages correctly.

Q28. Why should we use a Conversation ID?

Student: It identifies the conversation whose history should be retrieved. It helps prevent unrelated conversations from being mixed, provided the application correctly associates and authorizes each ID.

## Final quick revision

```
MessageWindowChatMemory
    = Manages a bounded conversation window.

InMemoryChatMemoryRepository
    = Stores messages in application memory.

MessageChatMemoryAdvisor
    = Integrates history into ChatClient requests.

Conversation ID
    = Identifies the conversation to retrieve.

Previous history + current message
    = Context supplied to the LLM.

More history
    = Potentially more prompt tokens and cost.
```

Part 18 ka main takeaway: Memory implement karne ke liye sirf messages save karna enough nahi hai. Application ko correct conversation history retrieve karke current LLM request mein include bhi karni hoti hai. Video mein ye integration `MessageWindowChatMemory`, `InMemoryChatMemoryRepository` aur `MessageChatMemoryAdvisor` ke through demonstrate kiya gaya hai.
