Ye LLM ki help se multiple query variations generate karta hai.

Conceptual configuration:

```
QueryExpander queryExpander =
        MultiQueryExpander.builder()
                .chatClientBuilder(chatClientBuilder)
                .build();
```

Note: Transcript mein naam audio transcription ke kaaran inconsistent hai. Spring AI API mein exact class name aur builder methods apne project version ki documentation se verify karein.

Teacher: QueryExpander ko ChatClient builder kyun dena padta hai?

Student: Kyunki multiple query variations generate karne ke liye ye internally LLM ko call karta hai.

LLM ko instruction diya jaata hai ki given query ke different search-friendly versions generate karo.

### 12.3 Number of Queries

        RetrievalAugmentationAdvisor.builder()

## 14.1 Before Method

Teacher: Advisor ka `before` method kab execute hota hai?

Student: Jab ChatClient ki actual LLM request execute hone se pehle Advisor intercept karta hai, tab iska before processing hota hai.

Video mein instructor ne `RetrievalAugmentationAdvisor` ke `before` method mein breakpoint lagaya tha.

Is method mein RAG pipeline ke operations execute hote hain.

### Step 1 – Original Query Create Karna

Sabse pehle user ki query read hoti hai.

Example:

```
What is Java?
```

Advisor ChatClient prompt se query text read karta hai.

Agar conversation history available ho, toh relevant history bhi read ki ja sakti hai.

Video ke practical project mein Chat Memory disabled/commented thi.

### Step 2 – Query Transformation

Ab configured QueryTransformers execute hote hain.

Video mein RewriteQueryTransformer use kiya gaya.

Original query:

```
What is Java?
```

Transformed query:

```
What is Java programming language?
```

Teacher: Is transformation ka benefit kya hai?

Student: Query mein additional clarity aa jaati hai, jisse vector database mein relevant documents search karne mein help mil sakti hai.

### Step 3 – Query Expansion

Ab QueryExpander execute hota hai.

Suppose transformed query:

```
What is Java programming language?
```

Isse multiple queries generate hoti hain:

```
1. What is Java programming language?

2. What are the key features and uses of Java?

3. How does Java compare to other programming languages?

4. What is the history of Java programming language?
```

Ab humare paas ek query ki jagah multiple queries hain.

### Step 4 – Har Query ke Liye Documents Retrieve Karna

Teacher: Ab multiple queries generate ho gayi. Next kya hoga?

Student: Ab har query ko DocumentRetriever ke through process kiya jayega.

Example:

```
Query 1
   |
   v
Vector Database
   |
   v
Documents 1, 2, 3

Query 2
   |
   v
Vector Database
   |
   v
Documents 4, 5, 6

Query 3
   |
   v
Vector Database
   |
   v
Documents 7, 8, 9
```

Video mein debugger ke through instructor ne dikhaya ki har expanded query ke liye retrieval operation execute hota hai.

Har query ke against documents retrieve hote hain aur results collect kiye jaate hain.

### Step 5 – DocumentJoiner Execute Hona

Teacher: Ab har query se documents aa gaye. Next kya hoga?

Student: Ab DocumentJoiner execute hota hai.

Ye different queries se retrieve hue documents ko combine karta hai.

```
Query 1 Results: D1, D2, D3

Query 2 Results: D4, D5, D6

Query 3 Results: D7, D8, D9
             |
             v
       DocumentJoiner
             |

       Combined Documents

Video mein `ConcatenationDocumentJoiner` use kiya gaya.

### Step 6 – DocumentPostProcessor Execute Hona

Agar DocumentPostProcessor configure kiya gaya hai, toh retrieved documents par uska processing logic execute hota hai.

Video mein instructor ne bataya ki configured post processors ki list par loop chalakar unka processing method execute hota hai.

Note: Practical implementation mein instructor ne custom post processor configure nahi kiya tha.

### Step 7 – Context Create Karna

Ab final retrieved documents ko context mein store kiya jaata hai.

Example:

```

Java is a programming language.

Document 2:
Java is platform independent.

Document 3:
Java supports multithreading.
```

Ye information ab LLM ke liye context ban jaati hai.

### Step 8 – Query Augmentation

Teacher: Ab context ready ho gaya. Next kya hoga?

Student: Ab QueryAugmenter execute hota hai.

Isko do important inputs milte hain:

1. Original user query.
2. Retrieved documents.

Ye dono ko combine karke augmented query banata hai.

Example:

```
Context Information:

Java is a programming language.
Java is platform independent.
Java supports multithreading.

User Question:

What is Java?
```

### Step 9 – Final LLM Call

Ab augmented query ChatClient request mein set hoti hai.

```
Augmented Query
      |
      v
ChatClient
      |
      v
LLM
      |
      v
Final Answer
```

Ab LLM retrieved context ko use karke answer generate karta hai.

Important: Yahan actual RAG ka main benefit hai. LLM ko sirf user query nahi milti, balki vector database se retrieved relevant information bhi milti hai.

# 17. Query Expansion ki Count Configure Karna

```

      |
      v
Query Expander
      |
      v
3 Expanded Queries
```

Ya requirement ke according different count configure ki ja sakti hai.

Benefit:

- More queries = different search perspectives.
- Better coverage ke chances.
- More retrieval operations.
- Potentially more processing cost and latency.

Important: More queries hamesha better answer guarantee nahi karti. Unnecessary queries se irrelevant documents bhi retrieve ho sakte hain.

# 18. Custom DocumentRetriever – Internet se Data Retrieve Karna

Teacher: Ab tak hum documents kahan se retrieve kar rahe the?

Student: Hum `VectorStoreDocumentRetriever` use kar rahe the, jo VectorStore se documents retrieve karta hai.

Teacher: Agar humein Internet se documents retrieve karne hain toh kya karenge?

Student: Humein DocumentRetriever ki different implementation banani hogi.

Video mein instructor ne explain kiya:

1. `DocumentRetriever` interface implement karo.
2. Uske required retrieval method ko override karo.
3. Apne data source ka retrieval logic likho.
4. Retrieved documents return karo.
5. Apni custom implementation ko `RetrievalAugmentationAdvisor` mein provide karo.

Conceptual code:

```
public class WebDocumentRetriever
        implements DocumentRetriever {

    // Implement the required retrieve method
    // Fetch information from the Internet
    // Convert results into Spring AI Documents
    // Return retrieved documents
}
```

Teacher: Kya RetrievalAugmentationAdvisor ko pata hona chahiye ki documents Vector Database se aaye hain ya Internet se?

Student: Nahi. Advisor ko `DocumentRetriever` abstraction ke through documents milte hain.

Retrieval ka actual logic implementation ke andar hota hai.

Example:

```
RetrievalAugmentationAdvisor
            |
            v
      DocumentRetriever
            |
       +----+----+
       |         |
       v         v
  Vector Store  Web Search
  Retriever     Retriever
```

Iska benefit ye hai ki hum retrieval source change kar sakte hain bina poori RAG pipeline ko change kiye.

Important: Video mein Internet retriever ka complete practical code implement nahi kiya gaya. Sirf custom implementation ka approach explain kiya gaya hai.

# 19. Complete Advanced RAG Flow – Final Revision

Teacher: Ab mujhe complete Advanced RAG pipeline ek baar simple language mein samjhao.

Student: Advanced RAG mein user ki query ko directly vector database mein search nahi karte. Pehle query ko improve karte hain, phir multiple queries generate kar sakte hain, uske baad documents retrieve aur process karte hain.

Finally retrieved context ko original query ke saath combine karke LLM ko bhejte hain.

Advanced RAG – Complete Flow

User Query

Example: What is Java?

1\. Pre-Retrieval

- CompressionQueryTransformer
- RewriteQueryTransformer
- TranslationQueryTransformer
- QueryExpander

Query improve aur expand hoti hai.

2\. Retrieval

- DocumentRetriever
- VectorStoreDocumentRetriever

Relevant documents fetch hote hain.

3\. Post-Retrieval

- DocumentJoiner
- DocumentPostProcessor

Documents combine aur process hote hain.

4\. Query Augmentation

\- ContextualQueryAugmenter

Original query + retrieved context combine hota hai.

LLM

Context ke basis par answer generate karta hai.

Final Answer

## 20. Important Components – Quick Revision

| Component                    | Simple meaning                                    |
| ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- |
| RetrievalAugmentationAdvisor | Complete RAG workflow coordinate karta hai        |
| QueryTransformer             | Query ko transform karta hai                      |
| CompressionQueryTransformer  | Conversation history compress karta hai           |
| RewriteQueryTransformer      | Query ko rewrite karta hai                        |
| TranslationQueryTransformer  | Query ko target language mein translate karta hai |
| QueryExpander                | Ek query se multiple queries banata hai           |
| DocumentRetriever            | Data source se documents fetch karta hai          |
| VectorStoreDocumentRetriever | VectorStore se documents fetch karta hai          |
| DocumentJoiner               | Multiple document results combine karta hai       |
| ConcatenationDocumentJoiner  | Documents ko concatenate karta hai                |
| DocumentPostProcessor        | Retrieved documents process karta hai             |
| QueryAugmenter               | Query aur context combine karta hai               |
| ContextualQueryAugmenter     | Context ke saath query augment karta hai          |

## 21. Is Video Se Kya Samajhna Hai?

1. Simple RAG mein query ke basis par vector database se documents retrieve hote hain.
2. Advanced RAG mein retrieval se pehle query transformation aur expansion bhi ho sakti hai.
3. RewriteQueryTransformer query ko search-friendly banata hai.
4. TranslationQueryTransformer query ko target language mein convert karta hai.
5. QueryExpander multiple related queries generate karta hai.
6. DocumentRetriever data source se relevant documents fetch karta hai.
7. DocumentJoiner multiple retrieval results combine karta hai.
8. DocumentPostProcessor retrieved documents process karta hai.
9. QueryAugmenter original query ke saath retrieved context combine karta hai.
10. RetrievalAugmentationAdvisor in different components ko ek RAG workflow mein execute karne mein help karta hai.

Sabse important baat: Advanced RAG ka purpose sirf documents retrieve karna nahi hai. Purpose hai user query ko better understand karna, relevant documents ko effectively retrieve karna aur LLM ko useful context provide karna.

Jahan transcript mein exact API method names ya implementation incomplete thi, wahan conceptual code diya hai aur usse clearly label kiya hai. Internet-based DocumentRetriever aur custom PostProcessor ka complete implementation is video mein nahi dikhaya gaya.

# **Spring AI Tutorial – Part 28: ETL Pipeline in Detail**

**Extract, Transform and Load Data | Hinglish Teacher–Student Notes**

Ye notes tumhare uploaded YouTube transcript ke complete sequence par based hain. Is video mein ETL Pipeline ka concept, Spring AI ke `DocumentReader`, `DocumentTransformer` aur `DocumentWriter` components explain kiye gaye hain.

## **1. ETL Pipeline kya hoti hai?**

**Teacher:** Student, RAG implement karne se pehle humein kya karna hota hai?

**Student:** Sir, sabse pehle humein apne documents ko database mein store karna hota hai, taaki RAG un documents se relevant information retrieve kar sake.

**Teacher:** Bilkul correct! Lekin documents database mein directly kaise store honge?

**Student:** Sir, isi process ko samajhne ke liye ETL Pipeline use hoti hai.

**Teacher:** ETL ka full form kya hai?

**Student:** ETL ka full form hai:

- **E – Extract**
- **T – Transform**
- **L – Load**

ETL Pipeline ka main purpose raw data ko kisi source se read karna, usko required format mein transform karna aur database ya Vector Store mein load karna hai.

### **Simple example**

Suppose hamare paas ek PDF file hai:

```
spring-ai-notes.pdf
```

Is PDF ke andar Spring AI, ChatClient, Embeddings aur RAG ke concepts hain.

Humein kya karna hai?

1. PDF se text extract karna.
2. Extracted text ko Spring AI Documents mein convert karna.
3. Large documents ko small chunks mein divide karna.
4. Chunks ko embeddings mein convert karke Vector Store mein save karna.

**ETL Pipeline**

**E – Extract**

PDF / JSON / TXT / DOCX se data read karna

**T – Transform**

Content clean karna, metadata add karna, chunks banana

**L – Load**

Documents ko Vector Store mein save karna

**Real-world example:** Company ke paas 500 PDF files hain. Company chahti hai ki employees un PDFs ke content ke baare mein AI chatbot se questions pooch saken. Iske liye pehle ETL Pipeline se PDFs ka data extract, transform aur Vector Store mein load kiya jayega.

## **2. Extract – DocumentReader**

**Teacher:** ETL ka first step kya hai?

**Student:** Extract.

**Teacher:** Extract ka matlab kya hota hai?

**Student:** Extract ka matlab hai source se raw data read karna.

**Teacher:** Spring AI mein data read karne ke liye kaunsa component available hai?

**Student:** `DocumentReader`.

### **DocumentReader kya hai?**

`DocumentReader` Spring AI ka ek interface hai jo different sources se data read karke `List<Document>` return karta hai.

Example sources:

- PDF files
- JSON files
- Text files
- Other supported document sources

Iska basic flow:

```
Source File
    |
    v
DocumentReader
    |
    v
List<Document>
```

### **DocumentReader interface**

Transcript mein explain kiya gaya hai ki `DocumentReader` interface ke paas `read()` method hota hai.

Conceptually:

```
public interface DocumentReader {

    List<Document> read();
}
```

**Teacher:** Agar humein custom DocumentReader banana hai toh kya karenge?

**Student:** `DocumentReader` interface implement karke `read()` method provide karenge.

Example conceptual implementation:

```
public class MyDocumentReader implements DocumentReader {

    @Override
    public List<Document> read() {

        // Read data from your source

        // Convert source data into Documents

        // Return List<Document>

        return List.of();
    }
}
```

Yahan `return List.of()` sirf placeholder hai. Actual implementation mein source se content read karke documents return karne honge.

### **Important DocumentReader implementations**

Video mein kuch implementations ke naam discuss kiye gaye hain:

| Reader                       | Purpose                                             |
| ---------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| `JsonReader`                 | JSON source se data read karna                      |
| `PagePdfDocumentReader`      | PDF ko page-oriented documents mein read karna      |
| `ParagraphPdfDocumentReader` | PDF ko paragraph-oriented documents mein read karna |
| `TextReader`                 | Text source se data read karna                      |

**Teacher:** Kya humein har file ke liye same reader use karna chahiye?

**Student:** Nahi sir. Source format ke according appropriate reader choose karna chahiye.

Example:

```
PDF  -> PagePdfDocumentReader
JSON -> JsonReader
TXT  -> TextReader
```

*Note:* Ye video mein discuss kiye gaye reader examples hain. Har reader ki exact availability aur constructor Spring AI version aur dependency par depend karti hai.

## **3. PDF Reader ke liye dependencies**

**Teacher:** Video mein PDF reader use karne ke liye kaunsi library mention hui hai?

**Student:** Spring AI PDF Document Reader.

Video mein specifically do reader implementations discuss hui hain:

- `PagePdfDocumentReader`
- `ParagraphPdfDocumentReader`

Transcript mein inke liye `spring-ai-pdf-document-reader` library ka reference diya gaya hai aur PDFBox-related reader dependencies ka bhi mention hai.

Conceptually dependency ka purpose PDF file ko read karna aur uske content ko Spring AI `Document` objects mein convert karna hai.

**Important:** Transcript mein exact Maven coordinates aur version nahi diye gaye. Isliye yahan unverified dependency version invent nahi ki gayi hai.

## **4. Transform – DocumentTransformer**

**Teacher:** Ab humne DocumentReader se data read kar liya. Next step kya hai?

**Student:** Transform.

**Teacher:** Kya hum raw documents ko directly database mein store kar sakte hain?

**Student:** Store kar sakte hain, lekin RAG ke liye raw documents ko process karna important hai.

**Teacher:** Kyun?

**Student:** Kyunki documents bahut bade ho sakte hain. Agar hum poora document ek hi unit mein store karenge, toh retrieval ke time relevant information find karna difficult ho sakta hai.

Isliye hum documents ko transform karte hain.

### **DocumentTransformer kya hai?**

`DocumentTransformer` Spring AI ka interface hai jo documents ke batch ko transform karta hai.

Input:

```
List<Document>
```

Output:

```
List<Document>
```

Matlab original documents ko transform karke naye documents return hote hain.

Conceptual interface:

```
public interface DocumentTransformer {

    List<Document> transform(List<Document> documents);
}
```

**Teacher:** Agar hamare paas ek large PDF hai, toh transformer kya kar sakta hai?

**Student:** Us PDF ke content ko small chunks mein divide kar sakta hai.

Example:

Original document:

```
Spring AI is a framework for integrating
AI models into Spring Boot applications.

It supports ChatClient, ChatModel,
EmbeddingModel and VectorStore.

RAG stands for Retrieval Augmented Generation.
It retrieves relevant information before
sending context to an LLM.
```

Transformation ke baad:

```
Chunk 1:
Spring AI is a framework for integrating
AI models into Spring Boot applications.

Chunk 2:
It supports ChatClient, ChatModel,
EmbeddingModel and VectorStore.

Chunk 3:
RAG stands for Retrieval Augmented Generation.
It retrieves relevant information before
sending context to an LLM.
```

Ab ek large document ki jagah multiple smaller documents mil gaye.

### **DocumentTransformer ke implementations**

Video mein following components discuss kiye gaye hain:

| Component                  | Transcript mein bataya gaya purpose                      |
| -------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------- |
| `ContentFormatTransformer` | Document content ko required format mein transform karna |
| `KeywordMetadataEnricher`  | Document metadata mein keywords add karna                |
| `SummaryMetadataEnricher`  | Summary-related metadata enrich karna                    |
| `TextSplitter`             | Large text ko smaller chunks mein divide karna           |
| `TokenTextSplitter`        | Token-based splitting karna                              |

Inka exact configuration aur output specific implementation par depend karta hai.

## **5. TextSplitter – Large documents ko chunks mein divide karna**

**Teacher:** RAG mein TextSplitter itna important kyun hai?

**Student:** Kyunki RAG mein humein user ke question ke according relevant information retrieve karni hoti hai. Agar poora document bahut bada hai, toh usmein se relevant information ko accurately retrieve karna difficult ho sakta hai.

Isliye hum large documents ko small chunks mein divide karte hain.

### **Example**

Original content:

```
Spring AI supports ChatClient.
ChatClient is used to communicate
with AI models.

Spring AI also supports embeddings.
Embeddings convert text into vectors.

VectorStore stores vectors and
supports similarity search.
```

Suppose hum chunk size chhota rakhte hain.

Output:

```
Chunk 1:
Spring AI supports ChatClient.
ChatClient is used to communicate
with AI models.

Chunk 2:
Spring AI also supports embeddings.
Embeddings convert text into vectors.

Chunk 3:
VectorStore stores vectors and
supports similarity search.
```

### **Chunking ka benefit**

1. Relevant information easily retrieve hoti hai.
2. LLM ko unnecessary content bhejne ki zaroorat kam hoti hai.
3. Context window ka better utilization hota hai.
4. Retrieval results more focused ho sakte hain.

**Teacher:** Kya chunk size bahut chhota rakhna chahiye?

**Student:** Nahi sir. Agar chunk bahut chhota hua toh context lose ho sakta hai. Agar bahut bada hua toh irrelevant content bhi retrieve ho sakta hai.

Isliye chunk size aur overlap ko document ke type ke according tune karna chahiye.

**Chunk overlap** ka simple meaning hai: ek chunk ke end ka kuch text next chunk ke beginning mein repeat karna, taaki splitting ke time context completely lose na ho.

*Note:* Transcript mein TextSplitter aur TokenTextSplitter ka concept discuss hua hai, lekin chunk size aur overlap ki exact values nahi di gayi hain.

## **6. Load – DocumentWriter**

**Teacher:** Ab humne documents read bhi kar liye aur transform bhi kar liye. Next step kya hai?

**Student:** Load.

**Teacher:** Spring AI mein documents ko destination par write karne ke liye kya use hota hai?

**Student:** `DocumentWriter`.

### **DocumentWriter kya hai?**

`DocumentWriter` ek interface hai jo documents ko destination mein write karta hai.

Iska basic flow:

```
List<Document>
      |
      v
DocumentWriter
      |
      v
Destination
```

Conceptual interface:

```
public interface DocumentWriter {

    void write(List<Document> documents);
}
```

Video mein explain kiya gaya hai ki DocumentWriter ek sink ki tarah work karta hai.

**Sink ka meaning:** Aisi destination jahan processed documents consume ya store hote hain.

Example:

- MariaDB Vector Store
- File-based storage
- Other supported vector stores

## **7. MariaDB Vector Store as DocumentWriter**

**Teacher:** Agar humein documents MariaDB mein save karne hain toh kya use karenge?

**Student:** MariaDB Vector Store implementation.

Video mein bataya gaya hai ki `MariaDBVectorStore` ek DocumentWriter implementation ke roop mein kaam karta hai.

Conceptual flow:

```
List<Document>
      |
      v
MariaDB Vector Store
      |
      v
Embedding Generation
      |
      v
MariaDB Database
```

MariaDB Vector Store documents ke content, metadata aur vector embeddings ko persist kar sakta hai.

**Teacher:** Agar hum MariaDB ke bajaye koi doosra vector database use karein toh?

**Student:** Destination ke according doosri DocumentWriter implementation use kar sakte hain.

Video mein in writer implementations ka mention hai:

- `AbstractObservationVectorStore`
- `FileDocumentWriter`
- `MariaDBVectorStore`
- `SimpleVectorStore`

*Note:* Transcript mein in sabhi classes ki detailed implementation nahi dikhayi gayi hai. Ye video mein discuss kiye gaye examples hain.

## **8. Document kya hota hai?**

**Teacher:** Hum baar-baar `Document` ki baat kar rahe hain. Spring AI mein Document kya represent karta hai?

**Student:** Document ek object hai jo text content, metadata aur optional media references hold karta hai.

### **Document ke important parts**

**1. Text Content**

Document ke andar actual text information hoti hai.

Example:

```
Spring AI helps Java developers
integrate AI models into applications.
```

**2. Metadata**

Metadata document ke baare mein additional information provide karta hai.

Example:

```
{
  "topic": "Spring AI",
  "source": "spring-ai-notes.pdf",
  "page": 10
}
```

**3. Media References**

Agar document ke saath media-related references hain, toh woh bhi document mein represent ho sakte hain.

Example:

- Image reference
- Other media-related information

Conceptual structure:

```
Document
   |
   |---- Text Content
   |
   |---- Metadata
   |
   |---- Optional Media References
```

**Teacher:** Kya Document ka matlab sirf text hai?

**Student:** Nahi sir. Document mein text ke saath metadata aur optional media references bhi ho sakte hain.

### **Document important kyun hai?**

Spring AI ETL Pipeline mein har major stage par `Document` objects use hote hain.

```
Reader
  |
  v
List<Document>
  |
  v
Transformer
  |
  v
List<Document>
  |
  v
Writer
  |
  v
Vector Store
```

Isi wajah se Spring AI mein `List<Document>` ko samajhna bahut important hai.

## **9. Complete ETL Pipeline – End-to-End Flow**

**Teacher:** Ab mujhe complete ETL Pipeline samjhao.

**Student:** Sir, ETL Pipeline mein three main components hain:

- DocumentReader – Data read karta hai.
- DocumentTransformer – Data transform karta hai.
- DocumentWriter – Data destination mein write karta hai.

Complete flow:

**SOURCE**

PDF / JSON / TXT / DOCX

**1. DocumentReader**

Source data read karta hai

`List<Document>`

**2. DocumentTransformer**

Cleaning, metadata enrichment, splitting

`List<Document>` – transformed

**3. DocumentWriter**

Processed documents ko destination par write karta hai

**MariaDB Vector Store**

Document content + Metadata + Embeddings

**Next Stage: RAG Pipeline**

User question → Retrieval → Context → LLM

### **Ek practical example**

Suppose company ke paas 100 PDF documents hain.

| Step      | Operation                           | Output                       |
| --------- | ----------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| Extract   | 100 PDFs read kiye                  | Documents                    |
| Transform | Text clean aur split kiya           | Small chunks                 |
| Load      | MariaDB Vector Store mein save kiya | Stored vectors and documents |
| RAG       | User question search karta hai      | Relevant documents           |

Is tarah ETL Pipeline RAG ke liye knowledge base prepare karti hai.

## **10. ETL Pipeline aur RAG Pipeline mein difference**

**Teacher:** Kya ETL Pipeline aur RAG Pipeline same hain?

**Student:** Nahi sir. Dono ka purpose different hai, lekin ek doosre se connected hain.

| ETL Pipeline                          | RAG Pipeline                                     |
| ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |
| Knowledge base prepare karti hai      | Knowledge base se information retrieve karti hai |
| Data extract karti hai                | User question receive karti hai                  |
| Documents transform karti hai         | Relevant documents search karti hai              |
| Vector Store mein data load karti hai | Retrieved context LLM ko deti hai                |
| Mostly ingestion-time process         | Mostly query-time process                        |

**Simple example:**

ETL Pipeline:

```
PDF -> Read -> Split -> Embeddings -> MariaDB
```

RAG Pipeline:

```
Question -> Embedding -> MariaDB Search
         -> Relevant Documents -> LLM -> Answer
```

## **11. Video ka complete conceptual revision**

| Component               | Main responsibility                            |
| ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- |
| ETL                     | Extract, Transform, Load                       |
| DocumentReader          | Source se documents read karna                 |
| `read()`                | Documents return karna                         |
| Document                | Content + Metadata + Optional Media References |
| DocumentTransformer     | Document batch transform karna                 |
| `transform()`           | Transformed document list return karna         |
| TextSplitter            | Large documents ko chunks mein divide karna    |
| TokenTextSplitter       | Token-based splitting                          |
| KeywordMetadataEnricher | Metadata mein keywords enrich karna            |
| SummaryMetadataEnricher | Summary metadata enrich karna                  |
| DocumentWriter          | Documents destination mein write karna         |
| `write()`               | Documents write karna                          |
| MariaDBVectorStore      | MariaDB mein vector documents persist karna    |

## **12. Complete concept flow – Yaad rakhne ke liye**

```
             ETL PIPELINE
                  |
        ---------------------
        |         |         |
      EXTRACT  TRANSFORM   LOAD
        |         |         |
      Reader   Transformer  Writer
        |         |         |
     read()   transform()  write()
        |         |         |
        v         v         v
    Documents  Chunks   Vector Store
        |         |         |
        ---------------------
                  |
             MariaDB
                  |
             RAG Pipeline
                  |
          Relevant Documents
                  |
                 LLM
                  |
             Final Answer
```

**Video ka main takeaway:** Spring AI mein ETL Pipeline ka purpose raw data ko `Document` objects mein convert karna, documents ko required format mein transform karna aur unhe Vector Store mein persist karna hai. RAG implementation ke liye ye preparation step bahut important hai.

*Source coverage note:* Is video mein ETL architecture aur components ka conceptual explanation diya gaya hai. Actual PDF/JSON ingestion ka complete runnable implementation next video mein dikhane ka promise kiya gaya hai; is transcript mein uska code implement nahi kiya gaya.
========================================================================================================================
Let's build a **complete Spring AI ETL Pipeline project** that you can run and test yourself.

We'll use a real PDF file as input, extract its text, convert it into Spring AI `Document` objects, split it into chunks, add metadata, generate embeddings and store everything in **MariaDB Vector Store**.

At the end, we'll verify the stored documents in MariaDB and use the same data for a RAG question.

## **1. What we are going to build**

**Input: PDF file**

spring-ai-notes.pdf

**1. Extract – PagePdfDocumentReader**

PDF → List of Documents

**2. Transform – TextSplitter**

Clean and split into smaller chunks

**3. Enrich – Metadata**

Add source, file name, page information

**4. Load – MariaDB VectorStore**

Generate embeddings and save documents

**MariaDB Vector Database**

Ready for RAG retrieval

We'll also add logs at every stage:

```
[ETL-START]
[EXTRACT] Reading PDF...
[EXTRACT] Documents returned: 5
[TRANSFORM] Splitting documents...
[TRANSFORM] Chunks created: 20
[ENRICH] Adding metadata...
[LOAD] Sending chunks to MariaDB...
[LOAD] Documents saved successfully
[ETL-COMPLETE]
```

## **2. Create the Spring Boot project**

Create a Maven project with this package:

```
com.example.etl
│
├── EtlApplication.java
│
├── controller
│   └── EtlController.java
│
├── service
│   └── PdfEtlService.java
│
└── resources
    ├── application.yml
    └── documents
        └── spring-ai-notes.pdf
```

We'll expose two REST APIs:

| API                    | Purpose                                 |
| ---------------------- | --------------------------------------- |
| `POST /api/etl/ingest` | Read PDF, transform and save in MariaDB |
| `POST /api/etl/search` | Search the documents saved in MariaDB   |

### **Maven dependencies**

The following uses Spring AI's current starter naming. Keep all Spring AI dependencies under one compatible Spring AI BOM; don't assign different versions to individual Spring AI dependencies.

```
<properties>
    <java.version>17</java.version>
    <spring-ai.version>2.0.2</spring-ai.version>
</properties>

<dependencyManagement>
    <dependencies>
        <dependency>
            <groupId>org.springframework.ai</groupId>
            <artifactId>spring-ai-bom</artifactId>
            <version>${spring-ai.version}</version>
            <type>pom</type>
            <scope>import</scope>
        </dependency>
    </dependencies>
</dependencyManagement>

<dependencies>

    <!-- REST API -->
    <dependency>
        <groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
        <artifactId>spring-boot-starter-web</artifactId>
    </dependency>

    <!-- Ollama Chat + Embedding models -->
    <dependency>
        <groupId>org.springframework.ai</groupId>
        <artifactId>spring-ai-starter-model-ollama</artifactId>
    </dependency>

    <!-- MariaDB Vector Store -->
    <dependency>
        <groupId>org.springframework.ai</groupId>
        <artifactId>spring-ai-starter-vector-store-mariadb</artifactId>
    </dependency>

    <!-- PDF Document Reader -->
    <dependency>
        <groupId>org.springframework.ai</groupId>
        <artifactId>spring-ai-pdf-document-reader</artifactId>
    </dependency>

    <!-- MariaDB JDBC driver -->
    <dependency>
        <groupId>org.mariadb.jdbc</groupId>
        <artifactId>mariadb-java-client</artifactId>
        <scope>runtime</scope>
    </dependency>

</dependencies>
```

The PDF reader and MariaDB dependencies are documented in Spring AI's ETL and vector-store references.&#x20;

[image](https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=https://docs.spring.io\&sz=32)

**Version compatibility:** This example uses the 2.0.x API family. If your existing project is on Spring AI 2.0.1, keep your current BOM version rather than changing versions midway. Also ensure your Spring Boot version is compatible with that BOM.

## **3. Prepare MariaDB and Ollama**

### **MariaDB database**

Run this SQL:

```
CREATE DATABASE springai;

USE springai;
```

Use your actual MariaDB credentials and port in the configuration below.

### **Ollama models**

Run:

```
ollama pull nomic-embed-text
ollama pull codellama:latest
ollama list
```

- `nomic-embed-text`: converts document text and search queries into embeddings.
- `codellama:latest`: optional for this ETL project; we'll use it when we add a chat-based RAG endpoint.

For this ETL example, the embedding model is required; the chat model is not required to ingest or search documents.

## **4. application.yml**

Create `src/main/resources/application.yml`:

```
server:
  port: 8080

spring:
  application:
    name: spring-ai-etl-demo

  datasource:
    url: jdbc:mariadb://localhost:3308/springai
    username: root
    password: root1234
    driver-class-name: org.mariadb.jdbc.Driver

  ai:
    ollama:
      base-url: http://localhost:11434
      embedding:
        model: nomic-embed-text

    vectorstore:
      mariadb:
        initialize-schema: true
        distance-type: COSINE
        dimensions: 768
        table-name: vector_store

logging:
  level:
    root: INFO
    com.example.etl: DEBUG
```

Change `3308`, username and password to match your MariaDB installation. Confirm the embedding model produces 768 dimensions before using this configuration. The MariaDB table schema must have the same dimension as the embedding model.

The MariaDB vector store supports automatic schema initialization when `initialize-schema` is enabled.&#x20;

## **5. Add a sample PDF**

Create this file:

`src/main/resources/documents/spring-ai-notes.pdf`

Use any text-based PDF containing information about Spring Boot, Spring AI, RAG, embeddings or Vector Store.

For the first test, you can create a simple document in Word or Notepad, export it as PDF and copy it into this folder.

Example content:

```
Spring AI

Spring AI is a framework that helps Java developers
integrate AI models into Spring applications.

ChatClient

ChatClient is a fluent API used to communicate
with chat models.

Embeddings

Embeddings convert text into numerical vectors.
These vectors represent the semantic meaning of text.

Vector Store

A Vector Store stores documents and their embeddings.
It supports similarity search.

RAG

Retrieval Augmented Generation retrieves relevant
documents and provides them as context to a language model.
```

**Important:** Use a text-based PDF for the first test. A scanned PDF containing only images may require OCR; a normal PDF reader may not extract its text.

## **6. Main ETL service – complete implementation**

This is the most important class. It will perform all three ETL stages.

Create `service/PdfEtlService.java`:

```
package com.example.etl.service;

import org.slf4j.Logger;
import org.slf4j.LoggerFactory;

import org.springframework.ai.document.Document;
import org.springframework.ai.reader.pdf.PagePdfDocumentReader;
import org.springframework.ai.reader.pdf.config.PdfDocumentReaderConfig;
import org.springframework.ai.transformer.splitter.TokenTextSplitter;
import org.springframework.ai.vectorstore.SearchRequest;
import org.springframework.ai.vectorstore.VectorStore;

import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

import java.time.Instant;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;

@Service
public class PdfEtlService {

    private static final Logger log =
            LoggerFactory.getLogger(PdfEtlService.class);

    private static final String PDF_PATH =
            "classpath:/documents/spring-ai-notes.pdf";

    private final VectorStore vectorStore;

    public PdfEtlService(VectorStore vectorStore) {
        this.vectorStore = vectorStore;
    }

    public Map<String, Object> ingestPdf() {

        long startTime = System.currentTimeMillis();

        log.info("========== ETL PIPELINE STARTED ==========");
        log.info("PDF source: {}", PDF_PATH);

        // -------------------------------------
        // STEP 1: EXTRACT
        // -------------------------------------

        log.info("[EXTRACT] Starting PDF extraction");

        PagePdfDocumentReader pdfReader =
                new PagePdfDocumentReader(
                        PDF_PATH,
                        PdfDocumentReaderConfig.builder()
                                .withPageTopMargin(0)
                                .withPagesPerDocument(1)
                                .build()
                );

        List<Document> extractedDocuments =
                pdfReader.read();

        log.info("[EXTRACT] Documents extracted: {}",
                extractedDocuments.size());

        if (extractedDocuments.isEmpty()) {
            throw new IllegalStateException(
                    "No text extracted from PDF. Check the PDF file."
            );
        }

        for (int i = 0; i < extractedDocuments.size(); i++) {

            Document document = extractedDocuments.get(i);

            log.info("[EXTRACT] Document number: {}", i + 1);
            log.info("[EXTRACT] Metadata: {}",
                    document.getMetadata());

            log.debug("[EXTRACT] Extracted content:\n{}",
                    document.getContent());
        }


        // -------------------------------------
        // STEP 2: ADD METADATA
        // -------------------------------------

        log.info("[METADATA] Adding custom metadata");

        List<Document> enrichedDocuments =
                new ArrayList<>();

        for (Document document : extractedDocuments) {

            Map<String, Object> metadata =
                    new HashMap<>(document.getMetadata());

            metadata.put("source", "spring-ai-notes.pdf");
            metadata.put("document_type", "PDF");
            metadata.put("ingested_at", Instant.now().toString());

            Document enrichedDocument =
                    new Document(
                            document.getContent(),
                            metadata
                    );

            enrichedDocuments.add(enrichedDocument);

            log.info("[METADATA] Metadata added: {}", metadata);
        }

        log.info("[METADATA] Enriched documents: {}",
                enrichedDocuments.size());


        // -------------------------------------
        // STEP 3: TRANSFORM / CHUNKING
        // -------------------------------------

        log.info("[TRANSFORM] Starting text splitting");

        TokenTextSplitter splitter =
                TokenTextSplitter.builder()
                        .withChunkSize(100)
                        .withMinChunkSizeChars(50)
                        .withMinChunkLengthToEmbed(10)
                        .withMaxNumChunks(1000)
                        .withKeepSeparator(true)
                        .build();

        List<Document> chunks =
                splitter.apply(enrichedDocuments);

        log.info("[TRANSFORM] Original documents: {}",
                enrichedDocuments.size());

        log.info("[TRANSFORM] Total chunks created: {}",
                chunks.size());

        if (chunks.isEmpty()) {
            throw new IllegalStateException(
                    "No chunks generated. Check extracted PDF content."
            );
        }

        for (int i = 0; i < chunks.size(); i++) {

            Document chunk = chunks.get(i);

            log.info("[CHUNK {}] ID: {}", i + 1, chunk.getId());

            log.info("[CHUNK {}] Metadata: {}",
                    i + 1, chunk.getMetadata());

            log.debug("[CHUNK {}] Content:\n{}",
                    i + 1, chunk.getContent());
        }


        // -------------------------------------
        // STEP 4: LOAD INTO VECTOR STORE
        // -------------------------------------

        log.info("[LOAD] Sending {} chunks to MariaDB",
                chunks.size());

        vectorStore.write(chunks);

        log.info("[LOAD] Documents successfully saved");

        long totalTime =
                System.currentTimeMillis() - startTime;

        log.info("========== ETL PIPELINE COMPLETED ==========");
        log.info("Extracted documents: {}",
                extractedDocuments.size());
        log.info("Transformed chunks: {}", chunks.size());
        log.info("Total processing time: {} ms", totalTime);


        // -------------------------------------
        // STEP 5: RETURN SUMMARY
        // -------------------------------------

        Map<String, Object> response =
                new HashMap<>();

        response.put("status", "SUCCESS");
        response.put("source", PDF_PATH);
        response.put("extractedDocuments",
                extractedDocuments.size());
        response.put("generatedChunks", chunks.size());
        response.put("storedIn", "MariaDB Vector Store");
        response.put("processingTimeMs", totalTime);

        return response;
    }


    // -------------------------------------
    // SEARCH STORED DOCUMENTS
    // -------------------------------------

    public List<Map<String, Object>> searchDocuments(
            String question) {

        log.info("========== VECTOR SEARCH STARTED ==========");
        log.info("[SEARCH] User query: {}", question);

        SearchRequest searchRequest =
                SearchRequest.builder()
                        .query(question)
                        .topK(5)
                        .build();

        log.info("[SEARCH] Calling MariaDB Vector Store");

        List<Document> results =
                vectorStore.similaritySearch(searchRequest);

        log.info("[SEARCH] Documents returned: {}",
                results.size());

        List<Map<String, Object>> response =
                new ArrayList<>();

        for (Document document : results) {

            log.info("[SEARCH] Document ID: {}",
                    document.getId());

            log.info("[SEARCH] Similarity score: {}",
                    document.getScore());

            log.info("[SEARCH] Metadata: {}",
                    document.getMetadata());

            log.debug("[SEARCH] Content:\n{}",
                    document.getContent());

            Map<String, Object> item = new HashMap<>();

            item.put("id", document.getId());
            item.put("score", document.getScore());
            item.put("metadata", document.getMetadata());
            item.put("content", document.getContent());

            response.add(item);
        }

        log.info("========== VECTOR SEARCH COMPLETED ==========");

        return response;
    }
}
```

### **Understand what happens inside this service**

**Step 1 – Extract**

```
List<Document> extractedDocuments = pdfReader.read();
```

PDF reader PDF file ko read karta hai aur `List<Document>` return karta hai.

For example, agar PDF mein 5 pages hain aur hum `withPagesPerDocument(1)` use kar rahe hain, toh reader normally page-wise documents return karega.

**Step 2 – Metadata**

```
metadata.put("source", "spring-ai-notes.pdf");
metadata.put("document_type", "PDF");
```

Hum source aur file type add kar rahe hain. Isse future mein hum source-based filtering kar sakte hain.

**Step 3 – Transform**

```
List<Document> chunks = splitter.apply(enrichedDocuments);
```

Yahan `TokenTextSplitter` large documents ko smaller chunks mein convert karta hai. Original metadata derived chunks mein preserve hota hai.

**Step 4 – Load**

```
vectorStore.write(chunks);
```

Yahan `VectorStore` `DocumentWriter` ki tarah kaam karta hai. It generates embeddings using the configured embedding model and persists the content, metadata and embeddings in MariaDB.&#x20;

[image](https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=https://docs.spring.io\&sz=32)

Home+1



## **7. REST Controller**

Create `controller/EtlController.java`:

```
package com.example.etl.controller;

import com.example.etl.service.PdfEtlService;

import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/etl")
public class EtlController {

    private final PdfEtlService etlService;

    public EtlController(PdfEtlService etlService) {
        this.etlService = etlService;
    }

    @PostMapping("/ingest")
    public Map<String, Object> ingestPdf() {

        return etlService.ingestPdf();
    }

    @GetMapping("/search")
    public List<Map<String, Object>> search(
            @RequestParam String question) {

        return etlService.searchDocuments(question);
    }
}
```

## **8. Main application class**

```
package com.example.etl;

import org.springframework.boot.SpringApplication;
import org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.SpringBootApplication;

@SpringBootApplication
public class EtlApplication {

    public static void main(String[] args) {

        SpringApplication.run(
                EtlApplication.class,
                args
        );
    }
}
```

## **9. Run and test the project**

Run this command:

```
mvn clean spring-boot:run
```

Check that:

- MariaDB connection succeeds.
- Ollama is running.
- `nomic-embed-text` is installed.
- PDF file exists at the classpath location.
- Application starts on port 8080.

### **Test 1 – Ingest the PDF**

Open Postman.

POST

`http://localhost:8080/api/etl/ingest`

No request body is required.

Expected response:

```
{
  "status": "SUCCESS",
  "source": "classpath:/documents/spring-ai-notes.pdf",
  "extractedDocuments": 5,
  "generatedChunks": 12,
  "storedIn": "MariaDB Vector Store",
  "processingTimeMs": 3500
}
```

These numbers are examples. Your document count, chunk count and processing time will depend on your PDF.

### **Test 2 – Search the stored PDF**

GET

`http://localhost:8080/api/etl/search?question=What%20is%20Spring%20AI`

Expected response example:

```
[
  {
    "id": "generated-document-id",
    "score": 0.87,
    "metadata": {
      "source": "spring-ai-notes.pdf",
      "document_type": "PDF"
    },
    "content": "Spring AI is a framework that helps Java developers integrate AI models into Spring applications."
  }
]
```

The actual similarity score and content depend on your PDF and embedding model.

Try these questions too:

```
What is ChatClient?
What are embeddings?
What is a Vector Store?
Explain Retrieval Augmented Generation.
How does Spring AI work?
```

This verifies that the data you ingested can actually be retrieved.

## **10. Verify data directly in MariaDB**

Run:

```
USE springai;

SHOW TABLES;
```

The default vector-store table is `vector_store`.

Check how many chunks were stored:

```
SELECT COUNT(*)
FROM vector_store;
```

View stored documents:

```
SELECT
    id,
    content,
    metadata
FROM vector_store;
```

Check vector dimensions:

```
SELECT
    id,
    VECTOR_DIM(embedding)
FROM vector_store;
```

Expected dimension for `nomic-embed-text`:

```
768
```

MariaDB Vector support requires MariaDB 11.7 or later. Check your server version:

```
SELECT VERSION();
```

If you're running an older MariaDB version, upgrade it before attempting this vector-store implementation.&#x20;

[image](https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=https://docs.spring.io\&sz=32)

Home



## **11. Understand the console logs**

When you call `/api/etl/ingest`, you should see logs in this sequence:

```
========== ETL PIPELINE STARTED ==========

PDF source: classpath:/documents/spring-ai-notes.pdf

[EXTRACT] Starting PDF extraction

[EXTRACT] Documents extracted: 5

[EXTRACT] Document number: 1
[EXTRACT] Metadata: {page_number=1}

[METADATA] Adding custom metadata

[METADATA] Metadata added:
{
  source=spring-ai-notes.pdf,
  document_type=PDF
}

[TRANSFORM] Starting text splitting

[TRANSFORM] Original documents: 5

[TRANSFORM] Total chunks created: 12

[CHUNK 1] ID: generated-id
[CHUNK 1] Metadata: {...}

[LOAD] Sending 12 chunks to MariaDB

[LOAD] Documents successfully saved

========== ETL PIPELINE COMPLETED ==========

Extracted documents: 5
Transformed chunks: 12
Total processing time: 3500 ms
```

Illustrative log output. Actual counts and metadata depend on the PDF reader's output.

### **Where to debug if something goes wrong**

| Problem                   | What to check                                  |
| ------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- |
| PDF file not found        | `src/main/resources/documents/` path           |
| No documents extracted    | PDF format; scanned PDF may need OCR           |
| No chunks created         | Extracted content and splitter settings        |
| Embedding model not found | `ollama list`                                  |
| Connection refused        | MariaDB and Ollama services                    |
| Vector dimension error    | Embedding dimension and DB schema              |
| Duplicate data            | Repeated ingestion of the same PDF             |
| Search returns empty      | Ingestion success, database contents and query |

**Important:** This demo does not yet implement duplicate detection or deletion. If you call `/ingest` repeatedly, it can insert the same PDF content again with new document IDs. For production, add a source-based re-ingestion strategy.

## **12. Understand the most important Spring AI interfaces**

These are the three interfaces from your video:

| Interface             | Java abstraction                           | Main method   |
| --------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | ------------- |
| `DocumentReader`      | `Supplier<List<Document>>`                 | `read()`      |
| `DocumentTransformer` | `Function<List<Document>, List<Document>>` | `transform()` |
| `DocumentWriter`      | `Consumer<List<Document>>`                 | `write()`     |

The official Spring AI documentation also shows the compact ETL pipeline:

```
vectorStore.write(
    tokenTextSplitter.split(
        pdfReader.read()
    )
);
```

This is the same core flow we implemented separately in `PdfEtlService`, with extra logging and metadata enrichment.&#x20;

# **Spring AI Tutorial – Part 30: Audio Transcription (Speech to Text) using Spring AI**

**Teacher → Student | Detailed Hinglish Notes**

Is video mein hum **Spring AI ka use karke Audio ko Text mein convert (Speech-to-Text / Transcription)** karna seekhenge. Is practical mein OpenAI API use ki gayi hai, Spring Boot application ke through audio process kiya jayega.

## **1. Audio Transcription kya hota hai?**

**Teacher:** Audio Transcription kya hota hai?

**Student:** Audio transcription ka matlab hai kisi audio file mein jo speech (boli hui baat) hai, usko written text mein convert karna.

Speech → AI Model → Text

[sound](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/rSAE_4yikSTpaVElCYsSUBxKhWgxPNqU_bnFR8dbEvuIIFqmxbpz6rWqNgjm7pcZ4FbI2DhzA-odmqTfzOVEdRvdFSMGrfCIiWkvKJ0NVuwIvGdzk3bxTyWQwl0Er0DCUBOyJ_jnLALWoG1535fXXmcETx4tS2pMe0-dPuQuFIXrHHThy9A2YnKDo2r2gqRZ?purpose=inline)

**Input – Audio**

Hum audio file provide karte hain, jaise M4A ya MP3.

[음성인식 AI는 어떻게 말을 알아들을까?](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/4fSRlJuvYNBIH2M_i7J90QJp40rI1PkViDyEplxZwkuEm_BkeUkN4o20bJTyBPqF03HW8NB8OZofDo0K_33Tx92LOdlspSo2usskAXuOk6gtpgHygKVdbVDA3EkPPejIhMK3rz0KiL1qVP5UEiV3M0RInFgyYF78LhW87xMbyoY?purpose=inline)

**Processing – AI**

Spring AI audio ko transcription API ke through AI provider tak bhejta hai.

[Different Transcription Methods Explained: Which One Fits Your Needs | Votars](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/2zaiyNlsxRbzjDv5A0BF51KWKmbNs5yu3wfNlyoHguujwDZGt4_8m9DeBTeYV9Qsdviajjx0L3Sg_RDQ_9Anh1maccJns14eNj92dilQigiNceTnMy1cIMAHHJtLZAIXNrQU1xvbfLiPMeUFl2Ss8S3OiDu0JnlHg3RZFckBkdM?purpose=inline)

**Output – Text**

AI audio mein boli gayi baat ko text mein return karta hai.

### **Example**

Input Audio:

*"Spring Boot helps developers build production-ready Java applications."*

Output Text:

*Spring Boot helps developers build production-ready Java applications.*

Is process ko **Speech-to-Text (STT)** ya **Audio Transcription** kehte hain.

- **Speech-to-Text (STT):** Speech ko text mein convert karna.
- **Transcription:** Audio mein boli gayi language ko written text mein convert karna.
- **Audio file:** Recorded voice ya audio recording.
- **AI Model:** Audio ko analyze karke speech recognize karta hai.

## **2. Real-world mein Audio Transcription ka use kahan hota hai?**

**Teacher:** Hum apne Spring Boot project mein Audio Transcription kyu implement karenge?

**Student:** Kyunki AI application sirf text generate karne tak limited nahi hai. Hum audio, images aur different types of input ko bhi AI ke through process kar sakte hain.

### **Real-world use cases**

- AI Chat Application: User voice se question pooch sakta hai.
- Voice-to-Text Chat: User bolta hai aur application uski voice ko text mein convert karti hai.
- Meeting Transcription: Meetings ki recordings ko text mein convert karna.
- Customer Support: Customer calls ko text mein convert karna.
- Voice Assistant: Voice commands ko text ke through process karna.
- Document Creation: Audio se automatically notes generate karna.

### **Example: AI Chat Application**

Voice-based AI Chat Flow

User speaks

"Explain Spring Boot"

↓

Audio Transcription

Audio converted into text

↓

Text sent to Chat Model

Spring AI / LLM processes the question

↓

AI Response

Application returns the answer

Video mein hum abhi audio-to-text wala part implement kar rahe hain. Transcription ke baad text ko kisi ChatModel se process karna next step ho sakta hai.

## **3. Spring AI Audio Transcription ka architecture**

**Teacher:** Spring AI audio transcription kaise perform karta hai?

**Student:** Hum Spring Boot application mein audio file provide karenge. Spring AI ka Transcription API use hoga, jo configured AI provider ki transcription API ko call karega. Provider transcription karke text return karega.

Architecture

Spring Boot Application

Controller + Service

↓

Spring AI TranscriptionModel

Unified abstraction

↓

OpenAI Transcription API

Configured provider

↓

Transcribed Text

Returned to Spring Boot

### **Step-by-step flow**

1. Client audio file provide karta hai.
2. Spring Boot Controller request receive karta hai.
3. Controller Service ko call karta hai.
4. Service audio Resource ko Spring AI `TranscriptionModel` ke paas bhejti hai.
5. Spring AI configured provider ki API call karta hai.
6. AI provider audio ko process karta hai.
7. Provider converted text return karta hai.
8. Spring Boot text ko HTTP response ke through client ko return karta hai.

## **4. Project setup – Spring Boot dependencies**

**Teacher:** Video mein kaunsa Spring Boot version use kiya gaya hai?

**Student:** Video mein **Spring Boot 3.5.9** use kiya gaya hai.

Video mein Spring AI OpenAI starter ke liye compatible dependency setup use kiya gaya hai.

### **Dependencies ka purpose**

| Dependency                       | Purpose                        |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------ |
| `spring-boot-starter-web`        | REST APIs create karne ke liye |
| `spring-ai-starter-model-openai` | OpenAI integration ke liye     |

### **Maven dependencies**

```
<dependencies>

    <!-- REST API -->
    <dependency>
        <groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
        <artifactId>spring-boot-starter-web</artifactId>
    </dependency>

    <!-- Spring AI OpenAI -->
    <dependency>
        <groupId>org.springframework.ai</groupId>
        <artifactId>spring-ai-starter-model-openai</artifactId>
    </dependency>

</dependencies>
```

Important: Video mein Spring Boot 3.5.9 ka setup use kiya gaya hai. Spring AI dependencies aur API signatures release ke hisaab se change ho sakte hain. Isliye apne project ke Spring AI BOM/version ke saath compatible dependency use karein.

### **Q1.&#x20;**`spring-boot-starter-web`**&#x20;kyu use karte hain?**

**Student:** Is dependency ki help se hum REST API create kar sakte hain.

Is project mein hum API banayenge jiske through audio file process hogi aur text response return hoga.

Example:

```
POST /api/v1/audio/transcript
```

### **Q2.&#x20;**`spring-ai-starter-model-openai`**&#x20;kyu use karte hain?**

**Student:** Is dependency ki help se hum Spring AI ke through OpenAI services access kar sakte hain.

Spring AI hume predefined abstractions aur auto-configuration provide karta hai, jisse provider API integrate karna easier hota hai.

Is video mein OpenAI ka audio transcription feature use kiya gaya hai.

## **5. AI Provider kya hota hai?**

**Teacher:** AI Provider ka kya meaning hai?

**Student:** AI Provider ek service ya company hoti hai jo AI models aur AI APIs provide karti hai.

Examples:

- OpenAI
- Google
- Anthropic
- Other AI service providers

Hum Spring AI ke through different supported providers ke saath integrate kar sakte hain, lekin har provider har feature support nahi karta.

### **Example**

Agar humko Audio Transcription karna hai, toh humko check karna hoga:

- Provider transcription support karta hai ya nahi?
- Kaunsa model transcription support karta hai?
- Supported audio formats kya hain?
- API request ka format kya hai?
- Required options kya hain?

Important: Sirf provider available hone ka matlab ye nahi ki us provider ka har model audio transcription support karta hai.

## **6. Spring AI TranscriptionModel kya hai?**

**Teacher:** Spring AI mein `TranscriptionModel` kya hai?

**Student:** `TranscriptionModel` Spring AI ka core abstraction hai jo Speech-to-Text functionality provide karne ke liye use hota hai.

Is abstraction ki help se hum provider-specific implementation ke details se largely independent code likh sakte hain.

### **Without abstraction – Direct API approach**

Agar hum directly OpenAI API call karte:

- OpenAI API URL manage karna padta.
- Request format manage karna padta.
- Authentication manage karna padta.
- Audio request prepare karna padta.
- Response parse karna padta.
- Error handling manage karna padta.

### **With Spring AI**

Spring AI hume unified API provide karta hai.

Provider Abstraction

OpenAI

Provider B

Provider C

↓

Spring AI TranscriptionModel

Common abstraction for supported providers

↓

Application Service

### **Q. Is abstraction ka benefit kya hai?**

**Student:**

- Provider integration ko simplify karta hai.
- Application code ko provider implementation details se isolate karta hai.
- Supported providers ke beech code portability improve kar sakta hai.
- Spring AI auto-configuration ka benefit milta hai.
- Application service mein provider-specific code kam hota hai.

Note: Provider switch karna always completely plug-and-play nahi hota. Dependencies, configuration, supported options, models aur request formats provider ke hisaab se different ho sakte hain.

## **7. TranscriptionModel ke methods**

**Teacher:** `TranscriptionModel` ke through hum audio transcription kaise karte hain?

**Student:** Video mein transcription model ke through audio Resource pass kiya jaata hai.

Conceptually, Spring AI transcription interface mein audio transcription ke liye methods hote hain, jaise:

- Audio transcription prompt/request ke through.
- Resource aur transcription options ke through supported API method.

Video mein direct Resource pass karne wala method use kiya gaya hai aur second example mein options ke saath transcription demonstrate kiya gaya hai.

Conceptual example:

```
transcriptionModel.transcribe(audioResource);
```

Note: Ye conceptual representation hai. Actual method names aur overloads apne Spring AI version ke according verify karein; video ke exact API signatures version-dependent hain.

### **Resource kya hota hai?**

**Teacher:** Spring Framework ka `Resource` kya hota hai?

**Student:** `Resource` Spring Framework ka abstraction hai jo different locations se files aur resources access karne mein help karta hai.

Examples:

- Classpath resources
- File system resources
- URL resources
- Other supported resource locations

Video mein audio file ko Resource ke form mein TranscriptionModel ko provide kiya gaya hai.

## **8.&#x20;**`@Value`**&#x20;se classpath audio file load karna**

**Teacher:** Video mein audio file application ke andar kaise load ki gayi?

**Student:** Video mein `@Value` annotation use karke audio file ko classpath se load kiya gaya.

### **Project structure**

```
src
 └── main
     └── resources
         ├── sample.m4a
         └── sample2.m4a
```

Audio files ko `src/main/resources` ke andar rakha gaya.

### **Example**

```
@Value("classpath:sample.m4a")
private Resource inputAudio;
```

### **Is code ko samjho**

| Code         | Explanation                                    |
| ------------ | ---------------------------------------------- |
| `@Value`     | Spring property/resource injection ke liye     |
| `classpath:` | Classpath resource location indicate karta hai |
| `sample.m4a` | Audio file ka naam                             |
| `Resource`   | Loaded audio resource ko represent karta hai   |

### **Q. Classpath kya hota hai?**

**Student:** Classpath wo locations hain jahan Java/Spring application runtime par classes aur resources ko locate kar sakti hai.

Spring Boot application ke resources folder ki files application classpath resources ke roop mein accessible hoti hain.

### **Q.&#x20;**`@Value`**&#x20;ka benefit kya hai?**

**Student:** Hume resource manually open karne ke liye boilerplate code likhne ki zaroorat nahi hoti. Spring dependency injection mechanism ke through Resource provide kar sakta hai.

Important: Classpath file loading ka ye approach fixed file ke liye useful hai. Real application mein client se dynamically upload hone wali file ke liye `MultipartFile` approach use karna better hai.

## **9.&#x20;**`AudioService`**&#x20;create karna**

**Teacher:** Hum Controller mein directly AI API call kyu nahi karenge?

**Student:** Application mein responsibilities separate rakhne ke liye hum Service layer use karenge.

Controller

HTTP request & response

↓

AudioService

Business logic and transcription

↓

TranscriptionModel

AI transcription

### **Service layer ke benefits**

- Business logic separate hota hai.
- Controller clean rehta hai.
- Code reusable hota hai.
- Unit testing easier hoti hai.
- Maintenance easier hoti hai.

### **Project structure**

```
src/main/java/com/example/demo
│
├── controllers
│   └── AudioController.java
│
├── services
│   └── AudioService.java
│
└── DemoApplication.java
```

## **10. AudioService mein TranscriptionModel inject karna**

**Teacher:** `TranscriptionModel` ko service mein kaise use karenge?

**Student:** Constructor injection ke through.

Conceptually, service mein TranscriptionModel dependency inject hogi.

```
@Service
public class AudioService {

    private final TranscriptionModel transcriptionModel;

    public AudioService(TranscriptionModel transcriptionModel) {
        this.transcriptionModel = transcriptionModel;
    }
}
```

### **Line-by-line explanation**

**Line 1:**

```
@Service
```

Spring ko indicate karta hai ki ye class Service component hai.

Spring is class ko component scanning aur dependency injection ke through manage kar sakta hai.

**Line 2:**

```
private final TranscriptionModel transcriptionModel;
```

TranscriptionModel dependency declare ki gayi hai.

**Constructor:**

```
public AudioService(TranscriptionModel transcriptionModel) {
    this.transcriptionModel = transcriptionModel;
}
```

Spring constructor ke through dependency provide kar sakta hai.

### **Q. Constructor Injection kyu use karte hain?**

**Student:**

- Dependency clearly declare hoti hai.
- Testing easy hoti hai.
- `final` field use kar sakte hain.
- Required dependency missing hone par application configuration fail ho sakti hai.
- Constructor injection recommended approach hai.

## **11. Audio ko Text mein convert karne ka Service method**

**Teacher:** Audio ko text mein convert karne ke liye service mein kya implement karenge?

**Student:** Hum `TranscriptionModel` ka use karke audio Resource pass karenge aur returned transcription ko String mein return karenge.

Video ka core concept:

```
transcriptionModel.transcribe(inputAudio);
```

Video mein service ka method audio Resource receive karta hai aur transcribed text return karta hai.

### **Conceptual service implementation**

```

@Service
public class AudioService {

    private final TranscriptionModel transcriptionModel;

    public AudioService(TranscriptionModel transcriptionModel) {
        this.transcriptionModel = transcriptionModel;
    }

    public String convertAudioToText(Resource inputAudio) {

        return transcriptionModel.transcribe(inputAudio);
    }
}

```

Version warning: Upar ka code video ke conceptual flow ko represent karta hai. Aapke Spring AI release mein `transcribe(Resource)` method aur return type ko verify karna zaroori hai. Video mein transcription model ke API methods use kiye gaye hain, aur exact signatures release-specific ho sakte hain.

### **Flow**

1. Service ko audio Resource milta hai.
2. `TranscriptionModel` call hota hai.
3. Audio provider ke paas bheja jata hai.
4. Provider speech ko text mein convert karta hai.
5. Text return hota hai.

## **12.&#x20;**`AudioController`**&#x20;create karna**

**Teacher:** Controller ka kya role hoga?

**Student:** Controller HTTP request receive karega aur AudioService ko call karega.

Video mein controller ka base URL:

`/api/v1/audio`

Aur transcription endpoint:

`/text`

Isliye complete URL hoga:

`POST /api/v1/audio/text`

### **Controller ka conceptual code**

```

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/v1/audio")
public class AudioController {

    private final AudioService audioService;

    public AudioController(AudioService audioService) {
        this.audioService = audioService;
    }

    @PostMapping("/text")
    public ResponseEntity<String> transcriptAudio() {

        // Audio Resource will be provided from the classpath
        // AudioService will perform transcription

        return ResponseEntity.ok("Transcription response");
    }
}

```

Note: Ye controller snippet architecture ko samjhane ke liye hai. Video mein actual controller method classpath Resource ko Service mein pass karta hai. Exact working code apne project version ke according implement karna hoga.

### **Controller annotations**

| Annotation               | Purpose                                     |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------- |
| `@RestController`        | REST API controller                         |
| `@RequestMapping`        | Controller ka base URL                      |
| `@PostMapping`           | POST request handle karta hai               |
| `ResponseEntity<String>` | HTTP response aur text return karne ke liye |

### **Q.&#x20;**`ResponseEntity<String>`**&#x20;kyu use karte hain?**

**Student:** `ResponseEntity` ke through hum HTTP response return kar sakte hain.

Is example mein:

- Response body: Transcribed text
- HTTP status: `200 OK`

## **13. Controller aur Service ka interaction**

**Teacher:** Controller aur Service ke beech interaction kaise hota hai?

**Student:** Controller Service method call karta hai.

HTTP POST Request

Client calls /api/v1/audio/text

↓

AudioController

Request handling

↓

AudioService

convertAudioToText()

↓

TranscriptionModel

AI transcription

↓

String Response

Transcribed text returned

### **Responsibility separation**

- **Controller:** HTTP communication.
- **Service:** Application logic.
- **TranscriptionModel:** AI transcription abstraction.
- **AI Provider:** Actual audio recognition.

## **14. OpenAI API Key configuration**

**Teacher:** Spring AI OpenAI API call karne ke liye API key kyu required hai?

**Student:** OpenAI API ko authenticate karne ke liye API key required hoti hai.

API key ke bina request generally authenticate nahi ho paayegi.

### `application.properties`

Video mein OpenAI configuration use ki gayi hai.

Conceptually:

```
spring.ai.openai.api-key=${OPENAI_API_KEY}
server.port=8083
```

### **Explanation**

| Property                   | Meaning                      |
| -------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| `spring.ai.openai.api-key` | OpenAI authentication key    |
| `OPENAI_API_KEY`           | Environment variable         |
| `server.port=8083`         | Spring Boot application port |

### **API key environment variable se kyu store karte hain?**

API key ko source code mein hardcode nahi karna chahiye.

Recommended:

```
spring.ai.openai.api-key=${OPENAI_API_KEY}
```

Environment variable set karne ke baad application us key ko access kar sakti hai.

### **Example – Windows PowerShell**

```
$env:OPENAI_API_KEY="your-api-key"
```

Uske baad application run kar sakte hain.

Security: API key ko GitHub par commit nahi karna chahiye. `.env`, environment variables, secret manager ya secure deployment configuration use karein.

server.port=8083
