## **17. Endpoint mein 404 error kyu aaya?**

**Teacher:** Video mein initial request par 404 error kyu aaya?

**Student:** Video mein endpoint mapping ka issue tha. Initial URL mein `/text` ki jagah galat endpoint use kiya gaya tha. Mapping correct karne ke baad request successfully execute hui.

### **Video ka debugging process**

1. Postman mein API call ki.
2. 404 Not Found response aaya.
3. Controller mapping verify ki.
4. Endpoint URL correct kiya.
5. Dobara request send ki.
6. Transcription response receive hua.

### **404 Not Found kya hota hai?**

404 = Requested resource/endpoint was not found.

Common reasons:

- URL incorrect hai.
- Controller mapping incorrect hai.
- Endpoint path mismatch hai.
- HTTP method mismatch ho sakta hai.
- Controller register nahi hua.

### **Example**

Agar controller mapping hai:

```
@RequestMapping("/api/v1/audio")
@PostMapping("/text")
```

Toh endpoint:

```
POST /api/v1/audio/text
```

Agar client call karta hai:

```
POST /api/v1/audio/transcribe
```

Aur `/transcribe` mapping exist nahi karti, toh 404 aa sakta hai.

## **18. Different audio files ko test karna**

**Teacher:** Kya hum ek hi audio file transcribe kar sakte hain?

**Student:** Nahi. Hum different audio files bhi test kar sakte hain.

Video mein do audio files use ki gayi hain:

- `sample.m4a`
- `sample2.m4a`

Ek file mein English/Hinglish speech aur doosri file mein Hindi speech test ki gayi.

### **Example**

Sample 1

English speech

English/Hinglish audio transcription test.

Sample 2

Hindi speech

Hindi audio transcription test.

### **Video mein result**

English audio:

Speech → English text

Hindi audio:

Speech → Hindi text

### **Q. Kya transcription sirf English ke liye hota hai?**

**Student:** Nahi. Transcription model ki supported languages ke according multiple languages recognize ki ja sakti hain.

Language support model/provider par depend karta hai.

## **19. Audio Transcription Options kya hote hain?**

**Teacher:** Kya hum transcription ke liye additional options bhi pass kar sakte hain?

**Student:** Yes. Spring AI ke transcription API mein supported transcription options pass kar sakte hain.

Video mein options ke saath transcription ka example implement kiya gaya hai.

### **Options ka purpose**

Transcription options ke through hum supported configuration control kar sakte hain, jaise:

- Model
- Language
- Prompt
- Response format
- Provider-specific options

Important: Har option aur parameter har model mein available nahi hota. Provider aur API version ke according options change hote hain.

## **20.&#x20;**`AudioTranscriptionOptions`**&#x20;kya hai?**

**Teacher:** `AudioTranscriptionOptions` ka kya role hai?

**Student:** Ye audio transcription ke configuration options ko represent karta hai.

Spring AI transcription API mein Resource ke saath options provide karne ka supported overload ho sakta hai.

Conceptually:

```
transcriptionModel.transcribe(audioResource, options);
```

Video mein OpenAI provider-specific options use kiye gaye hain.

## **21. OpenAI-specific Audio Transcription Options**

**Teacher:** Agar hum OpenAI use kar rahe hain, toh OpenAI-specific options kaise create karenge?

**Student:** Video mein `OpenAiAudioTranscriptionOptions` builder ka use kiya gaya hai.

Conceptual example:

```
OpenAiAudioTranscriptionOptions options =
        OpenAiAudioTranscriptionOptions.builder()
                .model("whisper-1")
                .build();
```

API version warning: Builder methods aur exact model identifiers video ke Spring AI/OpenAI API version par depend karte hain. Is example ko apne current dependency ke saath verify karein.

### **OpenAI-specific options kyu?**

Spring AI common abstraction provide karta hai, lekin provider-specific capabilities ko configure karne ke liye provider-specific option classes use ki ja sakti hain.

Video mein OpenAI ki implementation class use ki gayi hai.

## **22. Builder Pattern kya hota hai?**

**Teacher:** Video mein `.builder()` aur `.build()` kyu use kiya gaya?

**Student:** Builder Pattern complex objects ko step-by-step create karne ka ek design pattern hai.

Example:

```
OpenAiAudioTranscriptionOptions.builder()
        .model("whisper-1")
        .build();
```

### **Step-by-step**

**Step 1:**

```
OpenAiAudioTranscriptionOptions.builder()
```

Builder object create hota hai.

**Step 2:**

```
.model("whisper-1")
```

Model configuration set ki jaati hai.

**Step 3:**

```
.build()
```

Final options object create hota hai.

### **Builder Pattern ka benefit**

- Readable code.
- Configuration step-by-step.
- Optional fields configure karna easy.
- Complex constructor ke multiple arguments avoid kar sakte hain.

## **23. Transcription options ke possible parameters**

Video mein options builder ke through different settings discuss ki gayi hain.

| Option                           | Purpose                              |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ |
| Model                            | Transcription model select karna     |
| Language                         | Supported language configure karna   |
| Prompt                           | Model ko additional context dena     |
| Response format                  | Supported output format select karna |
| Temperature                      | Provider-supported behavior control  |
| Other provider-specific settings | Model/API-specific configuration     |

Note: Video mein kuch options discuss kiye gaye hain, lekin har option har transcription model mein supported nahi hota. In options ko current OpenAI API documentation aur Spring AI release ke according verify karna chahiye.

## **24.&#x20;**`transcribe()`**&#x20;ke overloaded methods**

**Teacher:** Video mein `transcribe()` ke multiple methods kyu dikhaye gaye?

**Student:** Java mein **Method Overloading** ke through same method name ke different parameter combinations define kiye ja sakte hain.

Video mein transcription API ke different overloads discuss kiye gaye:

- Audio Resource ke saath transcription.
- Audio Resource aur transcription options ke saath transcription.

### **Conceptual example**

```
transcribe(audioResource);
```

Aur:

```
transcribe(audioResource, options);
```

### **Difference**

| Method                               | Use                               |
| ------------------------------------ | --------------------------------- |
| `transcribe(audioResource)`          | Default configuration ke saath    |
| `transcribe(audioResource, options)` | Additional configuration ke saath |

Version-dependent: Ye conceptual overloads hain. Apne Spring AI release mein actual method signature check karein.

### **Method Overloading kya hoti hai?**

Same class mein same method name, lekin different parameter lists.

Example:

```
public void process(String input) {
}

public void process(String input, int count) {
}
```

Method name same hai: `process`.

Lekin parameters different hain.

## **25. Fixed Resource se Client Upload tak**

**Teacher:** Video mein initial implementation mein audio application ke resources folder se aa raha tha. Real-world application mein hum audio kaise receive karenge?

**Student:** Hum client se audio upload karwa sakte hain.

Video ke next practical part mein `MultipartFile` use kiya gaya hai.

Dynamic Audio Upload

Frontend / Postman

Choose audio file

↓

Multipart HTTP Request

Form-data containing file

↓

Spring Boot Controller

MultipartFile receives audio

↓

Resource Conversion

MultipartFile → Resource

↓

Transcription

Speech → Text

### **Q.&#x20;**`MultipartFile`**&#x20;kya hai?**

**Student:** `MultipartFile` Spring MVC ka interface hai jo multipart HTTP request ke through upload hui file ko represent karta hai.

Iska use:

- Image upload
- Audio upload
- PDF upload
- Excel upload
- Other file upload

### **Q. Multipart request kya hoti hai?**

**Student:** Multipart request ek HTTP request format hai jisme different parts ke through data send kiya ja sakta hai, including binary files.

Client file upload karne ke liye generally `multipart/form-data` content type use karta hai.

## **26.&#x20;**`MultipartFile`**&#x20;ko&#x20;**`Resource`**&#x20;mein convert karna**

**Teacher:** Spring AI ko audio file kis format mein provide kar rahe hain?

**Student:** Video mein Spring AI transcription ke liye `Resource` use kiya gaya hai.

Client se file receive karne ke baad `MultipartFile` ke `getResource()` method se Resource obtain kiya ja sakta hai.

Conceptual example:

```
Resource audioResource = audioFile.getResource();
```

### **Flow**

MultipartFile

Client-uploaded audio

↓ `getResource()`

Resource

Spring resource representation

↓

TranscriptionModel

Audio processing

### **Q. Conversion kyu karte hain?**

**Student:** Kyunki Spring AI transcription API Resource-based input accept kar sakti hai, aur client ki uploaded file ko Resource ke form mein provide karna convenient hai.

## **27. Client se Audio File receive karne ke liye Controller**

**Teacher:** Ab hum classpath se audio file nahi lenge. Client se file receive karne ke liye kya use karenge?

**Student:** `MultipartFile` use karenge.

Video mein Controller ke andar ek additional endpoint create kiya gaya jahan audio file request parameter ke through receive hoti hai.

### **Conceptual Controller code**

```

@PostMapping("/speech-to-text")
public ResponseEntity<String> speechToText(
        @RequestParam("audioFile") MultipartFile audioFile) {

    Resource audioResource = audioFile.getResource();

    String text = audioService.convertAudioToText(audioResource);

    return ResponseEntity.ok(text);
}

```

Note: Ye conceptual working pattern hai, assuming aapke `AudioService` aur transcription model methods aapke installed Spring AI version ke according match karte hain.

### **Line-by-line explanation**

**1. Endpoint**

```
@PostMapping("/speech-to-text")
```

POST request receive karta hai.

**2. File receive karna**

```
@RequestParam("audioFile") MultipartFile audioFile
```

- `@RequestParam` request parameter ko bind karta hai.
- `audioFile` multipart upload ko represent karta hai.
- `"audioFile"` request ke form-data key ka naam hai.

**3. Resource obtain karna**

```
Resource audioResource = audioFile.getResource();
```

Uploaded file ka Resource representation obtain kiya.

**4. Service call**

```
String text = audioService.convertAudioToText(audioResource);
```

Service transcription perform karti hai.

**5. Response**

```
return ResponseEntity.ok(text);
```

Transcribed text client ko return hota hai.

## **28. Postman se Dynamic Audio Upload kaise karenge?**

**Teacher:** Ab jab Controller `MultipartFile` receive karta hai, toh Postman se file kaise bhejenge?

**Student:** `Body → form-data` select karenge.

Postman Setup

POST

[http://localhost:8083/api/v1/audio/speech-to-text](http://localhost:8083/api/v1/audio/speech-to-text?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

Body → form-data

| Key       | Value                    |
| --------- | ------------------------ |
| audioFile | File → Select audio file |

Key ka naam Controller ke @RequestParam mein match hona chahiye.

### **Step-by-step**

1. Postman open karo.
2. New request create karo.
3. Method `POST` select karo.
4. URL enter karo:

```
http://localhost:8083/api/v1/audio/speech-to-text
```

5. `Body` select karo.
6. `form-data` select karo.
7. Key mein enter karo:

```
audioFile
```

8. Key ke type ko `File` select karo.
9. Audio file choose karo.
10. Send click karo.

### **Request structure**

| Key         | Type | Value        |
| ----------- | ---- | ------------ |
| `audioFile` | File | `sample.m4a` |

Important: Postman mein form-data key exactly `audioFile` honi chahiye, agar Controller mein `@RequestParam("audioFile")` use kiya hai.

### **Expected response**

```
Spring Boot is a Java framework used to build production-ready applications.
```

Actual text uploaded audio ke content par depend karega.

## **29. Classpath file vs MultipartFile**

**Teacher:** Classpath file aur MultipartFile approach mein kya difference hai?

| Classpath Resource                                     | MultipartFile                                  |
| ------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------- |
| File application ke resources mein stored hoti hai     | File client se upload hoti hai                 |
| Fixed file use karne ke liye convenient                | Dynamic file upload                            |
| Testing/demo ke liye useful                            | Real-world APIs ke liye useful                 |
| File application ke package/classpath ka part hoti hai | File request ke through aati hai               |
| `@Value` se Resource inject kiya ja sakta hai          | Controller multipart request receive karta hai |

### **Classpath approach**

```
Spring Boot Application
       ↓
Resources Folder
       ↓
sample.m4a
       ↓
TranscriptionModel
```

### **Multipart approach**

```
Client
       ↓
Upload Audio
       ↓
MultipartFile
       ↓
Resource
       ↓
TranscriptionModel
```

Real-world recommendation: Client-driven applications ke liye MultipartFile approach generally more flexible hai.

## **30. Audio Transcription Model kaun sa use hota hai?**

**Teacher:** Kya audio transcription ke liye normal ChatModel use hota hai?

**Student:** Video mein normal text generation ke liye use hone wale chat models aur audio transcription models ko distinguish kiya gaya hai.

Audio transcription ke liye provider ka dedicated transcription API/model use kiya jata hai.

Video mein OpenAI transcription API ke context mein **Whisper** discuss kiya gaya hai.

### **Chat model vs Transcription model**

| Chat Model                    | Transcription Model                         |
| ----------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
| Text prompt process karta hai | Audio speech ko text mein convert karta hai |
| Text generation/chat          | Speech-to-text                              |
| Chat completion               | Audio transcription                         |
| Chat-oriented API             | Transcription-oriented API                  |

Chat Model

Text Prompt

↓

AI Answer

Transcription Model

Audio

↓

Text

### **Q. Video mein kaunsa transcription model discuss kiya gaya?**

**Student:** Video mein OpenAI ke Whisper transcription model ko discuss kiya gaya hai, aur default configuration ke context mein Whisper model mention kiya gaya hai.

### **Q. Kya GPT chat model automatically audio transcription karega?**

**Student:** Video ka implementation dedicated transcription API ke through audio process karta hai. Normal chat completion aur audio transcription alag API capabilities hain. Model/API support verify karna important hai.

Model identifiers and defaults can change. Video mein discuss kiye model names ko current provider documentation se verify karna chahiye.

## **31. Speech-to-Text vs Text-to-Speech**

**Teacher:** Speech-to-Text aur Text-to-Speech mein kya difference hai?

**Student:** Dono audio AI capabilities hain, lekin direction opposite hoti hai.

Speech-to-Text (STT)

Audio / Voice

↓

Written Text

Text-to-Speech (TTS)

Written Text

↓

Audio / Voice

### **Speech-to-Text example**

*"Explain Spring AI"*

↓

Explain Spring AI

### **Text-to-Speech example**

"Hello, how are you?"

↓

*Audio voice saying: Hello, how are you?*

### **Q. Video mein kaunsa feature implement kiya?**

## **33. Important errors aur troubleshooting**

### **Error 1: 404 Not Found**

Problem: Endpoint not found.

**Possible reasons:**

- URL incorrect.
- Mapping incorrect.
- HTTP method incorrect.
- Controller not registered.

**Solution:**

Controller mapping aur Postman URL verify karein.

### **Error 2: Multipart request not received**

Problem: Client se file upload nahi hui ya request multipart format mein nahi hai.

**Possible reasons:**

- `form-data` select nahi kiya.
- Key name mismatch.
- Request body mein file select nahi ki.
- Multipart request ke badle plain text request send ki.

**Solution:**

Postman mein:

- `Body`
- `form-data`
- Correct key
- `File` type
- File selected

### **Error 3: Missing API Key**

**Problem:** OpenAI API authentication fail ho sakti hai.

**Possible reasons:**

- API key missing.
- Environment variable set nahi hai.
- Incorrect key.
- Provider authentication issue.

**Solution:**

Verify:

```
spring.ai.openai.api-key=${OPENAI_API_KEY}
```

Aur environment variable correctly set hai.

### **Error 4: Unsupported audio format**

**Problem:** Transcription API file ko accept nahi karti.

**Possible reasons:**

- Unsupported audio format.
- File corrupted.
- File size limit exceed.
- Incorrect MIME/content.

**Solution:**

Provider ke supported formats aur request requirements verify karein.

### **Error 5: TranscriptionModel Bean unavailable**

**Problem:** Spring application TranscriptionModel inject nahi kar pa rahi.

**Possible reasons:**

- Spring AI OpenAI dependency missing.
- Incorrect dependency version.
- Auto-configuration disabled.
- Configuration incomplete.
- Transcription capability/version mismatch.

**Solution:**

- Spring AI dependency verify karein.
- Spring AI BOM verify karein.
- OpenAI configuration verify karein.
- Logs check karein.
- Current version ki documentation check karein.

## **34. Complete flow – Video ka practical summary**

Audio Transcription – Complete Flow

Client

Postman / Frontend

↓

POST Request

multipart/form-data

↓

AudioController

Receive MultipartFile

↓

Resource

MultipartFile.getResource()

↓

AudioService

Business logic

↓

TranscriptionModel

Spring AI abstraction

↓

OpenAI Transcription API

Audio processing

↓

Transcribed Text

↓

HTTP Response

Text returned to client

### **Complete process in simple words**

1. User audio file select karta hai.
2. Client audio ko HTTP multipart request mein send karta hai.
3. Spring Boot Controller file receive karta hai.
4. File `MultipartFile` ke form mein available hoti hai.
5. File ka Resource obtain kiya jata hai.
6. Controller AudioService call karta hai.
7. AudioService Spring AI TranscriptionModel call karti hai.
8. Spring AI configured provider ki transcription API invoke karta hai.
9. Provider audio speech recognize karta hai.
10. Text return hota hai.
11. Spring Boot text client ko return karta hai.

## **35. Video mein cover kiye gaye important terms**

| Term                  | Simple meaning                                                |
| --------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Transcription         | Audio speech ko text mein convert karna                       |
| Speech-to-Text        | Voice to written text                                         |
| TranscriptionModel    | Spring AI transcription abstraction                           |
| AI Provider           | AI services provide karne wali company/service                |
| Resource              | Spring resource abstraction                                   |
| Classpath             | Java runtime par resources/classes locate karne ki locations  |
| `@Value`              | Spring resource/property injection                            |
| `@Service`            | Service component declaration                                 |
| Constructor Injection | Constructor ke through dependency inject karna                |
| `@RestController`     | REST API controller                                           |
| `@PostMapping`        | POST endpoint mapping                                         |
| `ResponseEntity`      | HTTP response wrapper                                         |
| `MultipartFile`       | Client-uploaded file representation                           |
| `multipart/form-data` | HTTP file-upload format                                       |
| Builder Pattern       | Configuration object step-by-step create karna                |
| Whisper               | OpenAI transcription model family/name discussed in the video |

## **36. Final revision – Teacher & Student**

**Teacher:** Spring AI Audio Transcription kya hai?

**Student:** Spring AI ke through audio speech ko text mein convert karne ki capability hai, using a supported provider's transcription API.

**Teacher:** TranscriptionModel kya karta hai?

**Student:** Ye Spring AI abstraction provide karta hai jiske through supported transcription implementations ke saath interact kar sakte hain.

**Teacher:** Audio ko Spring AI mein kis form mein provide kiya ja sakta hai?

**Student:** Video mein audio Resource ke form mein provide kiya gaya hai.

**Teacher:** Classpath file kaise load karte hain?

**Student:** Video mein `@Value` ke through classpath Resource inject kiya gaya hai.

**Teacher:** Client se audio kaise receive karte hain?

**Student:** Spring MVC `MultipartFile` aur `multipart/form-data` ke through.

**Teacher:** `MultipartFile` ko Resource mein kaise obtain kar sakte hain?

**Student:** `getResource()` ke through.

**Teacher:** Audio transcription ke liye normal ChatModel use karna zaroori hai?

**Student:** Video mein dedicated transcription API aur transcription model use kiya gaya hai. Normal text chat aur audio transcription separate capabilities hain.

**Teacher:** Transcription options kyu pass karte hain?

**Student:** Supported settings configure karne ke liye, jaise model, language, prompt ya response format.

**Teacher:** OpenAI API Key kyu required hai?

**Student:** OpenAI API authentication ke liye.

**Teacher:** Video ka final practical use case kya hai?

**Student:** Client se audio upload karke Spring Boot + Spring AI ke through speech ko text mein convert karna.

**Main takeaway:**

Spring AI hume audio transcription ke liye provider API integration ko simplify karne wala abstraction provide karta hai. Hum Spring Boot ke Controller aur Service layer ke through audio receive karke TranscriptionModel ko provide kar sakte hain aur converted text client ko return kar sakte hain.

Is video ka practical concept hai:

Client Audio → Spring Boot → Spring AI TranscriptionModel → AI Provider → Text Response

Ye capability voice-based chat applications, meeting transcription, voice assistants aur audio-processing applications build karne mein useful ho sakti hai.

# **Video 20: Complete Image Generation Tutorial with Spring AI and Spring Boot**

Source: Uploaded YouTube transcript | Duration: 39:45 | Language: Hinglish

Is video mein hum seekhenge ki **Spring Boot + Spring AI** ka use karke AI se images kaise generate kar sakte hain. Example ke liye OpenAI Image Generation API use ki gayi hai.

## **Part 1: Image Generation – Basic Concepts**

**Q1. Teacher: Spring AI mein Image Generation kya hota hai?**

**Student:** Image Generation ka matlab hai user ke diye hue text prompt ke basis par AI automatically image create karta hai.

Example:

User prompt:

```
A boy playing cricket in a beautiful green ground.
```

AI is prompt ko understand karega aur cricket ground mein cricket khelte hue boy ki image generate karega.

Is process ko **Text-to-Image Generation** kehte hain.

**Q2. Teacher: Image Generation ke real-world use cases kya hain?**

**Student:** Iske multiple use cases ho sakte hain.

1. **AI Thumbnail Generator**
   - Content creator video ka title aur description dega.
   - AI uske basis par thumbnail generate karega.
   - User generated thumbnails mein se ek select kar sakta hai.
2. **E-commerce Product Image Generation**
   - Product ke liye automatically images generate karna.
   - Product listing ke liye images create karna.
3. **Marketing Images**
   - Advertisement banners generate karna.
   - Social media posts ke liye images banana.
4. **Content Creation**
   - Blog images.
   - YouTube thumbnails.
   - Promotional images.

**Q3. Teacher: Video mein kaunsa real-world project demonstrate kiya gaya hai?**

**Student:** Instructor ne ek AI Thumbnail Generator project demonstrate kiya.

Is project mein do dashboards hain:

**1. Admin Dashboard**

Admin:

- Thumbnail templates create kar sakta hai.
- Template ke andar base prompt define kar sakta hai.
- Different thumbnail styles maintain kar sakta hai.

**2. User Dashboard**

User:

- Direct prompt dekar thumbnail generate kar sakta hai.
- Ya template library se template select kar sakta hai.
- Apna video title aur description provide kar sakta hai.

**Example:**

Admin template prompt:

```
Create a professional YouTube thumbnail
with attractive colors, bold text and cinematic lighting.
```

User prompt:

```
Java Spring Boot Microservices Tutorial
```

Application dono prompts combine karke image generation ke liye final prompt prepare kar sakti hai.

## **Part 2: Spring AI Image Generation Architecture**

**Q4. Teacher: Spring AI mein text generation aur image generation ke liye kaun-se interfaces use hote hain?**

**Student:** Spring AI different AI tasks ke liye different abstractions provide karta hai.

| Task             | Spring AI abstraction      |
| ---------------- | -------------------------- |
| Text generation  | `ChatClient` / `ChatModel` |
| Image generation | `ImageModel`               |

Text generation ke liye hum `ChatClient` use karte hain.

Image generation ke liye hum **ImageModel** use karte hain.

**Q5. Teacher: ImageModel kya hai?**

**Student:** `ImageModel` Spring AI ka interface hai jo image generation models ke saath interact karne ke liye use hota hai.

Iska package:

```
org.springframework.ai.image.ImageModel
```

Ye ek abstraction layer hai.

Matlab hume har AI provider ka alag implementation manually use karne ki zarurat nahi hoti. Spring AI provider-specific implementation provide karta hai.

Example:

- OpenAI Image Model
- Azure OpenAI Image Model
- Stability AI Image Model
- Other supported image providers

**Q6. Teacher: ImageModel internally kya karta hai?**

**Student:** ImageModel ke through hum image generation request bhejte hain.

Basic flow:

**User Prompt**

A boy playing cricket

**ImagePrompt**

Prompt + Image Options

**ImageModel**

Provider-specific implementation

**ImageResponse**

Generated image information

**Generated Image**

Image URL or Base64 data

**Q7. Teacher: ImagePrompt kya hota hai?**

**Student:** `ImagePrompt` ek input object hai jo image generation model ko prompt aur optional image options provide karta hai.

Ismein main components hain:

- **Messages** – Image generation ke liye prompt information.
- **ImageOptions** – Model, image count, quality, size jaise options.

Example:

```
ImagePrompt imagePrompt = new ImagePrompt(
    "A boy playing cricket"
);
```

Video mein simple string prompt ke saath default options use kiye gaye hain.

**Q8. Teacher: ImageResponse kya hota hai?**

**Student:** `ImageResponse` image generation operation ka response object hai.

Ismein generated image ki information milti hai.

Important methods:

```
imageResponse.getMetadata();

imageResponse.getResults();

imageResponse.getResult();
```

- `getMetadata()` – Response metadata.
- `getResults()` – Generated images ki list.
- `getResult()` – First generated image ka result.

**Q9. Teacher: ImageGeneration kya hota hai?**

**Student:** `ImageGeneration` generated image ka individual result represent karta hai.

Ismein image data aur related information mil sakti hai.

Video mein instructor ne image access karne ke liye ye methods demonstrate kiye:

```
imageResponse.getResult().getOutput().getUrl();

imageResponse.getResult().getOutput().getB64Json();
```

Yahan:

- `getUrl()` – Generated image ka URL.
- `getB64Json()` – Image ka Base64 encoded data.

## **Part 3: Spring AI Project Setup**

**Q10. Teacher: Image Generation ke liye kaunsi dependency add karni hoti hai?**

**Student:** Video mein instructor ne OpenAI provider use kiya hai.

Maven dependency:

```
<dependency>
    <groupId>org.springframework.ai</groupId>
    <artifactId>spring-ai-starter-model-openai</artifactId>
</dependency>
```

**Important:** Spring AI version ke according dependency artifact name aur configuration verify karna chahiye. Video mein project ke existing Spring AI setup ka use kiya gaya hai.

**Q11. Teacher: OpenAI API key kahan store karni chahiye?**

**Student:** API key ko kabhi bhi Java source code mein hardcode nahi karna chahiye.

Environment variable use karna chahiye.

Windows CMD:

```
set OPENAI_API_KEY=your_api_key
```

PowerShell:

```
$env:OPENAI_API_KEY="your_api_key"
```

Linux:

```
export OPENAI_API_KEY=your_api_key
```

Application configuration mein environment variable reference kar sakte hain.

```
spring.ai.openai.api-key=${OPENAI_API_KEY}
```

**Q12. Teacher: Kya hum ChatClient se image generate kar sakte hain?**

**Student:** Video mein instructor ne explain kiya ki `ChatClient` text generation ke liye use hota hai.

Image generation ke liye `ImageModel` use karna chahiye.

```
private final ImageModel imageModel;
```

Isse Spring AI configured provider ki implementation inject kar sakta hai.

## **Part 4: Image Generation Service Implementation**

**Q13. Teacher: Image generation ke liye service class kyun banate hain?**

**Student:** Business logic ko controller se separate rakhne ke liye service class banate hain.

Controller request receive karega aur service ko call karega.

Service:

1. User prompt receive karegi.
2. ImagePrompt create karegi.
3. ImageOptions configure karegi.
4. ImageModel call karegi.
5. ImageResponse return karegi.
6. Required image output extract karegi.

**Q14. Teacher: ImageService ka basic implementation kaise likhenge?**

**Student:** Video mein instructor ne constructor injection ke through `ImageModel` inject kiya hai.

Example:

```
@Service
public class ImageService {

    private final ImageModel imageModel;

    public ImageService(ImageModel imageModel) {
        this.imageModel = imageModel;
    }

    public String generateImage(String prompt) {

        ImagePrompt imagePrompt =
                new ImagePrompt(prompt);

        ImageResponse imageResponse =
                imageModel.call(imagePrompt);

        return imageResponse
                .getResult()
                .getOutput()
                .getUrl();
    }
}
```

**Explanation:**

- `ImageModel` – Image generation interface.
- `ImagePrompt` – User ka prompt.
- `imageModel.call()` – AI model ko request bhejta hai.
- `ImageResponse` – AI ka response.
- `getResult()` – First generated image result.
- `getOutput()` – Generated image output.
- `getUrl()` – Image URL return karta hai.

Note: Ye URL-based output ka simplified example hai. Actual supported output format aur method provider/model configuration par depend karte hain.

**Q15. Teacher: ImageOptions kya hota hai?**

**Student:** ImageOptions ke through hum image generation ko customize kar sakte hain.

Video mein instructor ne `OpenAiImageOptions` use kiya.

Example:

```
OpenAiImageOptions options =
        OpenAiImageOptions.builder()
                .N(1)
                .quality("hd")
                .build();
```

Iska purpose:

- Kitni images generate karni hain.
- Kaunsa model use karna hai.
- Image quality kya honi chahiye.
- Image size kya honi chahiye.

**Q16. Teacher: N parameter kya hota hai?**

**Student:** `N` parameter batata hai ki ek request mein kitni images generate karni hain.

Example:

```
.N(1)
```

Matlab ek image generate hogi.

```
.N(3)
```

Matlab teen images generate karne ki request hogi, agar selected model support karta hai.

Video mein instructor ne bataya ki discussed options mein N ki range 1 se 10 tak thi. Actual supported range model/API ke according verify karni chahiye.

**Q17. Teacher: Quality parameter kya hota hai?**

**Student:** Quality parameter generated image ki quality control karne ke liye use hota hai.

Video mein examples:

- `low`
- `medium`
- `high`
- `hd`
- `standard`

Lekin **important point:** Har model same quality values support nahi karta.

Example:

```
.quality("hd")
```

Agar selected model `hd` support nahi karta hai to API error aa sakti hai.

**Q18. Teacher: Image model parameter kya hota hai?**

**Student:** Is parameter se hum decide karte hain ki kaunsa image generation model use karna hai.

Example:

```
.model("gpt-image-1")
```

Video mein different image models ko test karne ka demonstration diya gaya.

Important: Model name aur availability current OpenAI API documentation se verify karni chahiye.

**Q19. Teacher: Width, height aur size options ka kya use hai?**

**Student:** Inka use generated image ke dimensions aur output size ko control karne ke liye hota hai.

Example:

```
.width(1024)
.height(1024)
```

Video mein instructor ne explain kiya ki kuch models specific dimensions support karte hain, jabki kuch models different size configurations support karte hain.

Isliye selected model ki documentation check karni zaroori hai.

## **Part 5: Controller Implementation and API Testing**

**Q20. Teacher: Image generation API kaise create karenge?**

**Student:** Hum `@RestController` aur `@PostMapping` use karenge.

Video mein endpoint ka example:

```
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/images")
public class ImageController {

    private final ImageService imageService;

    public ImageController(ImageService imageService) {
        this.imageService = imageService;
    }

    @PostMapping("/generate")
    public String generateImage(
            @RequestParam String prompt) {

        return imageService.generateImage(prompt);
    }
}
```

**Q21. Teacher: Postman se API kaise test karenge?**

**Student:** Application start karne ke baad Postman mein request send karenge.

POST

`http://localhost:8083/api/images/generate`

**Params**

| Key    | Value                                   |
| ------ | --------------------------------------- |
| prompt | A boy playing cricket in a green ground |

Expected response (URL-based output)

```
https://generated-image-url...
```

**Q22. Teacher: Image generate hone mein time kyun lagta hai?**

**Student:** Image generation ek external AI API call hai.

Request OpenAI server tak jaati hai. Model prompt process karta hai aur image generate karta hai.

Isliye text generation ke comparison mein image generation mein zyada time lag sakta hai.

Video mein instructor ne debugger aur breakpoints ke through ye process demonstrate kiya.

## **Part 6: ImageResponse Debugging**

**Q23. Teacher: Debugging ke time ImageResponse mein kya-kya check karna chahiye?**

**Student:** Video mein instructor ne following information check ki:

1. Image options.
2. Original prompt.
3. Image response metadata.
4. Generated image list.
5. Individual image result.
6. Image URL ya Base64 data.

Example logging:

```
log.info("Image Options: {}", options);

log.info("Image Response: {}", imageResponse);

log.info("Image Results: {}",
        imageResponse.getResults());
```

Isse hume samajh aata hai ki request mein kya send hua aur response mein kya receive hua.

**Q24. Teacher: ImageResponse metadata kya information provide karta hai?**

**Student:** Metadata mein response ke baare mein additional information milti hai.

Video mein instructor ne creation time aur response-related information debugger mein inspect ki.

**Q25. Teacher: Revised Prompt kya hota hai?**

**Student:** Video mein instructor ne generated response ke metadata mein revised prompt bhi demonstrate kiya.

Revised prompt ka matlab hai model ne original prompt ko internally modify ya enhance kiya ho.

Example:

Original prompt:

```
A boy playing cricket.
```

Revised prompt ka illustrative example:

```
A young boy playing cricket on a
beautiful green field, cinematic lighting,
realistic photography, detailed background.
```

Model ki taraf se revised prompt milna provider aur model support par depend karta hai.

## **Part 7: URL vs Base64 Image Response**

**Q26. Teacher: Generated image ka response kin formats mein mil sakta hai?**

**Student:** Video mein do important formats explain kiye gaye hain:

1. **URL**
2. **Base64 JSON**

**Q27. Teacher: URL response kya hota hai?**

**Student:** URL response mein generated image ko access karne ke liye URL milta hai.

Example:

```
https://example.com/generated-image.png
```

Application is URL ko frontend ko return kar sakti hai.

Frontend image display kar sakta hai:

```
<img src="IMAGE_URL" alt="Generated Image"/>
```

Video mein instructor ne temporary URL ka demonstration diya.

**Q28. Teacher: Base64 response kya hota hai?**

**Student:** Base64 ek encoding format hai jisme image ka binary data text representation mein milta hai.

Example:

```
iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAA...
```

Is data ko decode karke actual image file create kar sakte hain.

**Q29. Teacher: Base64 ko PNG file mein kaise convert karenge?**

**Student:** Java mein `Base64.getDecoder()` ka use karenge.

Video mein instructor ne ye approach demonstrate ki:

```
public void convertAndWrite(String base64Data)
        throws IOException {

    byte[] imageBytes =
            Base64.getDecoder().decode(base64Data);

    Files.write(
        Paths.get("image.png"),
        imageBytes
    );
}
```

**Step-by-step:**

1. Base64 string receive karo.
2. `Base64.getDecoder()` se decoder lo.
3. `decode()` se byte array generate karo.
4. `Files.write()` se image file save karo.

**Q30. Teacher: Base64 image ko cloud storage mein kaise save kar sakte hain?**

**Student:** Hum Base64 ko byte array mein convert karke cloud storage mein upload kar sakte hain.

Example flow:

**AI Image Response**

**Base64 String**

**Decode to byte[]**

**Upload to Cloud Storage**

**Get Image URL**

**Return URL to Frontend**

Video mein instructor ne bataya ki cloud upload logic implement karke generated image ka URL return kar sakte hain.

## **Part 8: Model Compatibility and Errors**

**Q31. Teacher: Kya har image model same options support karta hai?**

**Student:** Nahi. Ye video ka ek important concept hai.

**Different models different parameters support karte hain.**

Example:

- Model A `hd` quality support kar sakta hai.
- Model B `low`, `medium`, `high`, `auto` support kar sakta hai.
- Kisi model mein specific image size supported nahi ho sakta.

Isliye blindly same options har model ke saath use nahi karne chahiye.

**Q32. Teacher: Video mein quality option change karne par kaunsi error aayi thi?**

**Student:** Instructor ne ek different model select kiya aur `hd` quality pass ki.

API ne error return ki:

```
Invalid value of 'hd'
```

Reason:

Selected model `hd` support nahi karta tha.

Solution:

Supported quality value use karni thi, jaise:

```
.quality("medium")
```

**Q33. Teacher: GPT Image model use karne par organization verification error kyun aayi thi?**

**Student:** Video mein instructor ne `gpt-image-2` model test kiya tha.

Error message:

```
Your organization must be verified
to use the model.
```

Iska matlab selected model use karne ke liye OpenAI organization verification required thi.

Instructor ne verification ke liye OpenAI platform par organization verify karne ko kaha.

**Q34. Teacher: Image model ke supported parameters kaise check karenge?**

**Student:** Model-specific official documentation check karenge.

Check karne wale parameters:

- Model name.
- Quality.
- Image size.
- Image format.
- Background support.
- Output format.
- URL/Base64 support.
- Organization verification requirements.

**Important:** Har model ke parameters same nahi hote. Unsupported parameter pass karne par API error aa sakti hai.

**Q35. Teacher: Kya GPT Image models aur DALL-E models same output format support karte hain?**

**Student:** Video mein instructor ne explain kiya ki URL aur Base64 output format ka support model-specific hai.

Unke demonstration mein DALL-E 2 aur DALL-E 3 ke liye URL response option discuss hua, jabki GPT Image models ke liye Base64 output use kiya gaya.

Isliye output format select karne se pehle current provider documentation check karna chahiye.

## **Part 9: Spring AI ke bina Image Generation**

**Q36. Teacher: Kya image generation ke liye Spring AI compulsory hai?**

**Student:** Nahi.

Image generation ke liye multiple approaches use kar sakte hain:

1. Spring AI.
2. Java LangChain4j.
3. Direct REST API call.

**Q37. Teacher: Direct REST API se image generate kaise kar sakte hain?**

**Student:** Hum OpenAI API ko directly HTTP request send kar sakte hain.

Basic flow:

1. OpenAI API key generate karo.
2. Authorization header set karo.
3. Prompt aur model information send karo.
4. API response receive karo.
5. Image URL ya Base64 data extract karo.

Video ke according authorization header mein Bearer token send karna hota hai.

Example header:

```
Authorization: Bearer YOUR_API_KEY
Content-Type: application/json
```

Is approach mein Spring AI abstraction ki requirement nahi hoti.

# **Part 10: Complete Concept Revision**

## **Important Spring AI Image Generation Classes**

| Class / Interface    | Purpose                           |
| -------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| `ImageModel`         | Image generation model interface  |
| `ImagePrompt`        | Image generation request          |
| `ImageOptions`       | Image generation options          |
| `OpenAiImageOptions` | OpenAI-specific image options     |
| `ImageResponse`      | Image generation response         |
| `ImageGeneration`    | Individual generated image result |

## **Complete Execution Flow**

1. **User sends prompt**

   Example: A boy playing cricket.
2. **Controller receives prompt**

   POST request from Postman or frontend.
3. **Service creates ImagePrompt**

   Prompt and image options prepare hote hain.
4. **ImageModel calls AI provider**

   Request OpenAI tak jaati hai.
5. **AI generates image**

   Model image create karta hai.
6. **ImageResponse received**

   URL ya Base64 output milta hai.
7. **Application returns result**

   Frontend image display ya cloud storage mein save kar sakta hai.

## **Interview Questions – Quick Revision**

**Q1. Which interface is used for image generation in Spring AI?**

Answer: `ImageModel`.

**Q2. What is ImagePrompt?**

Answer: It represents the input prompt and optional image generation options.

**Q3. What is ImageResponse?**

Answer: It represents the response received after image generation.

**Q4. What is ImageOptions?**

Answer: It configures image generation parameters like model, quality, number of images and size.

**Q5. What is the difference between URL and Base64 response?**

Answer: URL provides an image access link, while Base64 provides encoded image data that can be decoded into a file.

**Q6. Can we generate multiple images in one request?**

Answer: Yes, using the `N` option, if the selected model supports it.

**Q7. Why can an image generation request fail even when the API key is correct?**

Answer: The selected model may not support the requested quality, size, output format or other parameters. Organization verification or billing requirements can also cause errors.

**Q8. Is Spring AI mandatory for image generation?**

Answer: No. We can use Spring AI, LangChain4j or direct REST API calls.

**Q9. Why should API keys be stored in environment variables?**

Answer: To avoid exposing sensitive credentials in source code.

**Q10. What is the role of ImageModel?**

Answer: It provides an abstraction to interact with image generation providers.

## **Practical Checklist**

**Implementation progress**

0/10 completed

Add Spring AI OpenAI dependency

Configure OpenAI API key using environment variable

Inject ImageModel into ImageService

Create ImagePrompt using user prompt

Configure OpenAiImageOptions

Call imageModel.call()

Inspect ImageResponse

Extract URL or Base64 data

Save image or upload to cloud storage

Test endpoint using Postman

**Important takeaway:** Spring AI mein image generation ke liye `ImageModel` use hota hai. User prompt ko `ImagePrompt` mein pass karte hain, optional `ImageOptions` configure karte hain, aur `ImageResponse` se generated image ka URL ya Base64 data extract karte hain.

*Notes are based on the uploaded video transcript. Model names, supported parameters and API requirements should be checked against current provider documentation before implementing.*

# **Video #40 – MCP (Model Context Protocol) with Spring AI**

YouTube: [Watch Video #40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eScSGh2kAX4\&utm_source=chatgpt.com) · Topic: MCP Client and MCP Server Practical Implementation

Ye notes video ke transcript ke basis par banaye gaye hain. Language simple Hinglish rakhi hai, taaki concepts easily samajh aayein aur interview revision mein bhi help ho.

# Video #40: MCP (Model Context Protocol) with Spring AI

## 1. What is MCP?

**Teacher:** MCP ka full form kya hai?

**Student:** MCP ka full form **Model Context Protocol** hai.

MCP ek **open-source standard protocol** hai jo AI-powered applications ko external systems aur tools ke saath interact karne ki facility deta hai.

Examples of external systems:

- PostgreSQL / MySQL
- Google Drive
- Slack
- Google Maps
- Filesystem
- Other third-party applications

**Teacher:** MCP ki need kyun padi?

**Student:** Ek AI application ke paas LLM hota hai. LLM ko hum application ka brain bol sakte hain.

LLM ke paas:

- Understanding capability
- Thinking capability
- Reasoning capability
- Natural language processing capability

Lekin sirf LLM hone se application external systems mein actual operations perform nahi kar sakta.

Example:

User: "Mere liye ek Java file create karo aur usmein Hello World print karo."

Normal AI application:

- Java code generate karke de sakta hai.
- Lekin actual computer par file create nahi kar sakta, jab tak usko suitable tool access na diya jaye.

MCP ke through AI application external tools use kar sakta hai aur actual file create karwa sakta hai.

**Teacher:** MCP ko simple definition mein kaise explain karenge?

**Student:** MCP ek standard protocol hai jo AI-powered applications ko external tools aur systems ke saath communicate karne deta hai.

---

## 2. Why MCP? (Problem and Solution)

**Teacher:** MCP se pehle external systems ke saath interaction kaise hota tha?

**Student:** Pehle hum function calling aur tool calling jaise approaches use karte the.

Lekin video ke according:

- Different vendors ke integration methods alag ho sakte hain.
- Har external system ke liye custom integration karna pad sakta hai.
- Integration complexity badh sakti hai.
- Vendor-specific implementation ki wajah se vendor lock-in ka risk ho sakta hai.

Isliye ek common standard ki need thi.

**Teacher:** MCP kya solution provide karta hai?

**Student:** MCP common rules and specifications provide karta hai. Isse AI applications aur external tools ek standard protocol ke through communicate kar sakte hain.

### Example

Suppose hamare paas Spring Boot + Spring AI application hai.

Application ko in systems se interact karna hai:

1. PostgreSQL
2. Google Drive
3. Slack
4. Local Filesystem

MCP ke through in systems ke tools expose kiye ja sakte hain. AI application available tools use karke required operation perform kar sakta hai.

### MCP Analogy – USB-C

USB-C ka example samjho:

Pehle different devices ke liye different connectors hote the. USB-C ne ek common connection standard provide kiya.

Similarly:

- USB-C = Devices ke beech common connection standard
- MCP = AI applications aur external tools ke beech common communication standard

**Important:** MCP ko AI applications ke liye USB-C jaisa standard kaha ja sakta hai.

---

## 3. MCP Architecture

**Teacher:** MCP architecture mein main components kaun-kaun se hain?

**Student:** Video mein three main components explain kiye gaye hain:

1. MCP Host
2. MCP Client
3. MCP Server

Inke saath LLM bhi important role play karta hai.

### 3.1 MCP Host

**Teacher:** MCP Host kya hota hai?

**Student:** MCP Host ek AI-powered application hota hai jo MCP clients ko coordinate karta hai.

Video ke practical example mein:

**Spring Boot Application = MCP Host**

Spring Boot application ke andar Spring AI aur MCP Client use kiya gaya hai.

### 3.2 MCP Client

**Teacher:** MCP Client ka kya kaam hota hai?

**Student:** MCP Client communication layer ki tarah kaam karta hai.

Responsibilities:

- MCP Server se available tools ki information lena.
- Tools ke saath communication manage karna.
- Required tool select karne ke flow mein participate karna.
- MCP protocol request server ko send karna.
- Server se tool execution result receive karna.

Simple language mein:

**MCP Client = AI Application aur MCP Server ke beech communication layer.**

### 3.3 MCP Server

**Teacher:** MCP Server kya hota hai?

**Student:** MCP Server external tools expose karta hai.

Actual tool implementation server side par execute hoti hai.

Example:

Filesystem MCP Server tools provide kar sakta hai:

- Read file
- Write file
- Create directory
- List directory
- Search files
- Edit file
- Move file

Agar AI ko file create karni hai, toh MCP Server actual filesystem operation perform karega.

### 3.4 LLM

**Teacher:** LLM ka role kya hai?

**Student:** LLM application ka brain hai.

LLM:

- User query understand karta hai.
- User ka intention identify karta hai.
- Decide karne mein help karta hai ki kaunsa tool use hona chahiye.
- Tool result ko natural language mein convert karta hai.

### Architecture Diagram

User\
|\
v\
Spring Boot Application (MCP Host)\
|\
v\
Spring AI + LLM\
|\
v\
MCP Client\
|\
\| MCP Protocol Request\
v\
MCP Server\
|\
v\
External System / Tool\
|\
v\
Tool Execution Result\
|\
v\
LLM generates final response\
|\
v\
User

---

## 4. MCP Request Flow – Step by Step

**Teacher:** Jab user MCP ke through file create karne ki request karta hai, toh internally kya hota hai?

**Student:** Chalo step-by-step samajhte hain.

Example:

User query:\
"Create a file named test.java and write a main method with Hello World."

Step 1: User request Spring Boot application ko send karta hai.

Step 2: Spring AI user query ko LLM tak forward karta hai.

Step 3: LLM query understand karta hai.

LLM identify karta hai ki user ko file creation operation perform karna hai.

Step 4: MCP Client ke paas MCP Server se available tools ki information hoti hai.

Step 5: Appropriate tool select kiya jata hai.

Example: write_file tool.

Step 6: MCP Client MCP protocol request MCP Server ko send karta hai.

Step 7: MCP Server actual file operation perform karta hai.

Step 8: File creation ka result MCP Client aur application tak return hota hai.

Step 9: LLM result ko natural language mein convert karta hai.

Final response:

"Your test.java file has been successfully created."

**Remember:**

- LLM = Understand and reason
- MCP Client = Communication
- MCP Server = Tool execution
- External System = Actual resource

---

## 5. MCP Architecture Layers

**Teacher:** MCP mein kitni main layers explain ki gayi hain?

**Student:** Video mein two layers explain ki gayi hain:

1. Data Layer
2. Transport Layer

### 5.1 Data Layer

Data Layer MCP communication ke messages aur protocol-level information se related hoti hai.

Is layer mein client aur server ke beech request-response aur available capabilities ki information exchange hoti hai.

### 5.2 Transport Layer

Transport Layer ka kaam client aur server ke beech messages transfer karna hai.

Video mein two transport mechanisms explain kiye gaye hain:

1. STDIO
2. Streamable HTTP

---

## 6. MCP Transport Mechanisms

### 6.1 STDIO Transport

**Teacher:** STDIO ka full form kya hai?

**Student:** STDIO ka matlab Standard Input / Output hai.

STDIO transport local process-to-process communication ke liye use hota hai.

Example:

- Spring Boot application local machine par run ho rahi hai.
- MCP Server bhi local machine par run ho raha hai.
- MCP Client server process ko start karke standard input/output ke through communicate karta hai.

**Use case:** Jab MCP Client aur MCP Server same local environment mein communicate kar rahe hon.

### 6.2 Streamable HTTP Transport

**Teacher:** Streamable HTTP kya hota hai?

**Student:** Streamable HTTP remote MCP Server ke saath HTTP ke through communication support karta hai.

Features mentioned in video:

- HTTP-based communication
- Remote server communication
- Standard HTTP authentication methods
- Optional Server-Sent Events (SSE) support

**Use case:** Jab MCP Server remote machine ya server par hosted ho.

### STDIO vs Streamable HTTP

| Feature         | STDIO                   | Streamable HTTP             |
| --------------- | ----------------------- | --------------------------- |
| Communication   | Standard Input/Output   | HTTP                        |
| Typical usage   | Local process           | Remote server               |
| Server location | Local environment       | Remote environment possible |
| Example         | Local filesystem server | Remote MCP server           |

**Interview Question:** MCP kya sirf HTTP use karta hai?

**Answer:** Nahi. Video mein STDIO aur Streamable HTTP dono transport mechanisms explain kiye gaye hain.

---

## 7. MCP SDK and Supported Languages

**Teacher:** MCP SDK ka kya purpose hai?

**Student:** MCP SDK developers ko different programming languages mein MCP clients aur servers develop karne mein help karta hai.

Video mein ye languages mention ki gayi hain:

- TypeScript
- Python
- Java
- Kotlin
- C#
- PHP

Video ke according Spring AI internally Java MCP SDK use karta hai.

---

## 8. MCP Servers – Reference and Third-Party Servers

**Teacher:** MCP Server ke examples kahan mil sakte hain?

**Student:** MCP ecosystem mein reference servers aur third-party integrations available hain.

### 8.1 Reference Servers

Video mein examples:

- Filesystem
- Git
- Memory
- Sequential Thinking
- Time

Filesystem server files aur directories ke operations expose karta hai.

### 8.2 Third-Party Integrations

Video mein different company integrations ke examples discuss kiye gaye hain, jaise:

- Google Calendar
- Notion
- Figma
- AWS
- Azure

**Important:** Video archived reference servers ke baare mein caution deta hai. Archived servers maintained nahi hote, isliye production usage se pehle maintenance status verify karna chahiye.

# PART 2: Practical – Spring AI MCP Client with Filesystem MCP Server

## 9. Practical Architecture

Application ka role:

- Spring AI = LLM integration

## 11. application.properties Configuration

Video mein application ka port 8081 configure kiya gaya hai.

```
server.port=8081
```

OpenAI API key environment variable se configure ki gayi hai.

```
spring.ai.openai.api-key=${OPENAI_API_KEY}
```

**Teacher:** API key kahan se generate hogi?

**Student:** OpenAI API Platform se.

ChatGPT login aur OpenAI API billing/credits same cheez nahi hain.

**Important Security Point:**

- API key source code mein hardcode nahi karni chahiye.
- Environment variable use karna chahiye.
- API key GitHub ya public repository mein commit nahi karni chahiye.

---

## 12. ChatController Implementation

**Teacher:** Spring AI mein ChatClient ka use kyun karte hain?

**Student:** ChatClient ke through hum LLM ke saath interact kar sakte hain.

Video mein ChatClient.Builder inject karke ChatClient create kiya gaya hai.

Basic flow:

```
ChatClient chatClient = chatClientBuilder.build();

String response = chatClient
        .prompt()
        .user(query)
        .call()
        .content();
```

Is flow mein:

- prompt() = Prompt start karta hai.
- user(query) = User query provide karta hai.
- call() = Model call execute karta hai.
- content() = Generated response return karta hai.

### REST Endpoint

Video mein POST endpoint `/ai/chat` use kiya gaya hai.

Example:

```
POST http://localhost:8081/ai/chat?query=Hey how are you
```

Postman se request send karke response check kiya ja sakta hai.

## 13. SimpleLoggerAdvisor

**Teacher:** SimpleLoggerAdvisor kyun use kiya gaya?

**Student:** SimpleLoggerAdvisor request aur response logging mein help karta hai.

Isse hum inspect kar sakte hain:

- LLM ko kya request send hui.
- LLM se kya response aaya.
- AI interaction ke logs.

Video mein ChatClient builder ke saath advisor configure kiya gaya hai:

```
chatClientBuilder.defaultAdvisors(
        new SimpleLoggerAdvisor()
);
```

Spring AI advisor package ke liye debug logging enable karne ka bhi demonstration diya gaya hai.

**Important:** Exact logging properties aur builder APIs Spring AI version ke according verify karni chahiye.

## 14. Without MCP vs With MCP

**Teacher:** MCP ke bina aur MCP ke saath kya difference hai?

**Student:**

### Without MCP

User:\
"Create test.java file and write Hello World."

AI:

- Java code generate kar sakta hai.
- User ko code show kar sakta hai.
- Actual local file create karne ke liye configured external tool access nahi hai.

### With MCP

User:\
"Create test.java file and write Hello World."

AI:

1. User intention understand karta hai.
2. Filesystem tool select karta hai.
3. MCP Client server ko request send karta hai.
4. Filesystem MCP Server file create karta hai.
5. Result user ko return hota hai.

**Main Difference:** MCP ke through AI application ko actual external tool operations perform karne ki capability milti hai.

## 18. servers.json Configuration

**Teacher:** MCP Client ko kaise pata chalega ki Filesystem MCP Server ko start karna hai?

**Student:** Video mein `servers.json` file create karke MCP Server configuration define ki gayi hai.

Illustrative configuration:

```
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "filesystem": {
      "command": "npx",
      "args": [
        "-y",
        "@modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem",
        "C:\\Users\\YourUser\\mcp",
        "C:\\Users\\YourUser\\personal-data"
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

Explanation:

- `mcpServers` = MCP Servers ki configuration.
- `filesystem` = Server ka name.
- `command` = Server start karne ka command.
- `npx` = Node package execute karne ke liye.
- `args` = Package aur allowed directory paths.

**Important:** Windows paths mein JSON escaping ka dhyan rakho. Apne system ke correct absolute paths use karo.

---

## 19. application.properties – MCP STDIO Configuration

**Teacher:** Spring AI ko servers.json ka path kaise provide karenge?

**Student:** Video mein property use ki gayi hai:

```
spring.ai.mcp.client.stdio.servers-configuration=classpath:/servers.json
```

Iska meaning:

- `spring.ai.mcp.client` = MCP Client configuration.
- `stdio` = STDIO transport.
- `servers-configuration` = Server configuration file ka path.
- `classpath:/servers.json` = Resources folder mein servers.json file.

Expected project structure:

```
src
 └── main
     ├── java
     │    └── com.example
     │         └── ...
     └── resources
          ├── application.properties
          └── servers.json
```

Application start hone par configured STDIO MCP Server ko launch karne ka process automatically manage hota hai.

## 21. ToolCallbackProvider

**Teacher:** MCP Server ke tools ko ChatClient tak kaise pahunchayenge?

**Student:** Video mein `ToolCallbackProvider` use kiya gaya hai.

`ToolCallbackProvider` different sources se available tool callbacks represent karta hai.

MCP Server ke tools ko Spring AI ke tool-calling mechanism se use karne ke liye ye provider ChatClient builder mein configure kiya gaya hai.

Conceptual example:

```
// ToolCallbackProvider bean inject karo

chatClientBuilder.defaultToolCallbacks(
        toolCallbackProvider
);
```

**Important:**

- MCP Server tools provide karta hai.
- ToolCallbackProvider un tools ko represent karta hai.
- ChatClient builder mein configure karne se LLM tool-calling flow mein un tools ka use kar sakta hai.

Exact method signature Spring AI version ke according verify karna chahiye.

## 24. Security – Allowed Directories

**Teacher:** Kya MCP Server poore computer ki files access kar sakta hai?

**Student:** Video ke practical mein Filesystem MCP Server ko specific directories provide ki gayi hain.

Example:

```
Allowed:
C:\Users\YourUser\mcp
C:\Users\YourUser\personal-data
```

Agar AI application configured allowed directories ke bahar kisi file ko access karne ki request kare, toh server access deny kar sakta hai.

Video mein outside allowed directory access ke liye access-denied behavior demonstrate kiya gaya hai.

**Important Security Concept:**

- Only required directories allow karo.
- Sensitive directories unnecessarily expose mat karo.
- MCP Server permissions carefully configure karo.
- Production environment mein access control aur security review zaroor karo.

## 25. Logging and Debugging

**Teacher:** MCP implementation debug kaise karenge?

**Student:** Video mein Spring AI logs aur MCP tool callback logs inspect kiye gaye hain.

Logs se hum identify kar sakte hain:

- User request kya thi?
- LLM ko kya request gayi?
- LLM ne kya response diya?
- Kaunsa tool call hua?
- Tool execution ka result kya tha?
- Koi access-denied error aaya ya nahi?
- Token usage kitna hua?

### Token Usage

Video mein response metadata mein token usage bhi show kiya gaya hai.

Important terms:

- Prompt Tokens = Input side par use hue tokens.
- Completion Tokens = Model ke generated response ke tokens.
- Total Tokens = Prompt aur completion tokens ka total.

Example:

```
Prompt Tokens     : 300
Completion Tokens : 700
Total Tokens      : 1000
```

Ye sirf example values hain; actual token usage request ke according change hota hai.

# PART 4: Interview Preparation

## 26. Important Interview Questions and Answers

**Q1. What is MCP?**

MCP stands for Model Context Protocol. It is an open-source standard protocol that allows AI-powered applications to interact with external tools and systems.

**Q2. Why do we need MCP?**

LLMs can understand and reason about user requests, but they need tools to perform external operations. MCP provides a standard way to connect AI applications with external tools.

**Q3. What are the main components of MCP architecture?**

- MCP Host
- MCP Client
- MCP Server

LLM also plays an important role in understanding user intent and selecting tools.

**Q4. What is MCP Host?**

MCP Host is an AI-powered application that coordinates MCP clients. In the video practical, the Spring Boot application acts as MCP Host.

**Q5. What is MCP Client?**

MCP Client handles communication between the host application and MCP Server. It discovers available tools and sends protocol requests.

**Q6. What is MCP Server?**

MCP Server exposes tools and performs the actual tool operations, such as reading or writing files.

**Q7. What is the role of LLM in MCP?**

LLM understands the user request, reasons about the required action, and helps select an appropriate tool. It also generates the final natural-language response.

**Q8. What are the two transport mechanisms discussed in the video?**

- STDIO
- Streamable HTTP

**Q9. What is STDIO transport?**

STDIO uses standard input and output for communication between local processes.

**Q10. What is Streamable HTTP?**

It is an HTTP-based transport mechanism used for communication with MCP servers, including remote servers.

**Q11. What is Filesystem MCP Server?**

It is an MCP Server that exposes filesystem tools such as reading files, writing files, listing directories, creating directories, editing files and moving files.

**Q12. Why do we use servers.json?**

It defines MCP server startup configuration, including the command, arguments and allowed directory paths.

**Q13. Why do we use ToolCallbackProvider?**

It represents available tool callbacks from different sources so that Spring AI ChatClient can use them in tool-calling workflows.

**Q14. Can MCP work without OpenAI?**

MCP is a protocol, not an LLM provider. The video practical uses OpenAI, but MCP itself is not limited to OpenAI.

**Q15. What is the difference between MCP Client and MCP Server?**

MCP Client communicates with the server and invokes tools. MCP Server exposes tools and executes their operations.

**Q16. What happens if a user asks to access a file outside the allowed directory?**

The Filesystem MCP Server can deny access because the requested path is outside its configured allowed directories.

**Q17. What is the benefit of MCP?**

It standardizes the connection between AI applications and external tools, reducing the need for separate custom integrations.

---

# PART 5: Quick Revision

## Important Terms

| Term                 | Meaning                             |
| -------------------- | ----------------------------------- |
| MCP                  | Model Context Protocol              |
| MCP Host             | AI-powered application              |
| MCP Client           | Communication layer                 |
| MCP Server           | Exposes and executes tools          |
| LLM                  | Brain of AI application             |
| STDIO                | Standard Input / Output             |
| Streamable HTTP      | HTTP-based transport                |
| ToolCallbackProvider | Represents available tool callbacks |
| Filesystem Server    | Provides file operation tools       |
| servers.json         | MCP server configuration            |
| Prompt Tokens        | Input tokens                        |
| Completion Tokens    | Generated output tokens             |

## One-Minute Revision

1. MCP = Model Context Protocol.
2. MCP is an open-source standard protocol.
3. MCP connects AI applications with external tools.
4. Main components = Host, Client, Server.
5. LLM understands the user request.
6. MCP Client communicates with MCP Server.
7. MCP Server exposes and executes tools.
8. Two transports = STDIO and Streamable HTTP.
9. Video practical uses Spring Boot + Spring AI + OpenAI.
10. Filesystem MCP Server provides file operations.
11. `servers.json` defines server startup configuration.
12. `ToolCallbackProvider` provides tool callbacks to ChatClient.
13. Allowed directories help restrict filesystem access.
14. Logs help debug tool calls and inspect token usage.

## Practical Architecture in One Line

User → Spring Boot Host → Spring AI / LLM → MCP Client → MCP Server → Filesystem → Result → User

## Final Understanding

MCP ka main purpose AI application ko sirf answer generate karne tak limited nahi rakhna hai. MCP ke through AI application external tools use kar sakta hai aur real-world operations perform karwa sakta hai.

Example: File create karna, file read karna, directory list karna aur external applications ke tools use karna.

**Remember: LLM is the brain, MCP Client is the communication layer, and MCP Server provides the tools.**

**Revision tip:** Interview ke liye sabse pehle MCP Host, Client, Server ka difference aur complete request flow achhe se samjho. Ye is video ke sabse important concepts hain.

## **7. Complete pom.xml**

Replace your existing `pom.xml` with this:

```

<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<project xmlns="http://maven.apache.org/POM/4.0.0"
         xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
         xsi:schemaLocation="http://maven.apache.org/POM/4.0.0
         https://maven.apache.org/xsd/maven-4.0.0.xsd">

    <modelVersion>4.0.0</modelVersion>

    <parent>
        <groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
        <artifactId>spring-boot-starter-parent</artifactId>
        <version>3.5.15</version>
        <relativePath/>
    </parent>

    <groupId>com.example</groupId>
    <artifactId>mcp-ollama-client</artifactId>
    <version>0.0.1-SNAPSHOT</version>

    <properties>
        <java.version>17</java.version>
        <spring-ai.version>1.1.8</spring-ai.version>
    </properties>

    <dependencyManagement>
        <dependencies>
            <dependency>
                <groupId>org.springframework.ai</groupId>
                <artifactId>spring-ai-bom</artifactId>
                <version>${spring-ai.version}</version>
                <type>pom</type>
                <scope>import</scope>
            </dependency>
        </dependencies>
    </dependencyManagement>

    <dependencies>

        <!-- REST API -->
        <dependency>
            <groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
            <artifactId>spring-boot-starter-web</artifactId>
        </dependency>

        <!-- Spring AI Ollama -->
        <dependency>
            <groupId>org.springframework.ai</groupId>
            <artifactId>spring-ai-starter-model-ollama</artifactId>
        </dependency>

        <!-- Spring AI MCP Client -->
        <dependency>
            <groupId>org.springframework.ai</groupId>
            <artifactId>spring-ai-starter-mcp-client</artifactId>
        </dependency>

        <!-- Testing -->
        <dependency>
            <groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
            <artifactId>spring-boot-starter-test</artifactId>
            <scope>test</scope>
        </dependency>

    </dependencies>

    <build>
        <plugins>
            <plugin>
                <groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
                <artifactId>spring-boot-maven-plugin</artifactId>
            </plugin>
        </plugins>
    </build>

</project>

```

We are using the official Spring AI Ollama and MCP Client starters.&#x20;

## **9. Configure application.properties**

`src/main/resources/application.properties`

```

spring.application.name=mcp-ollama-client

server.port=8081

# -----------------------------
# Ollama Configuration
# -----------------------------

spring.ai.ollama.base-url=http://localhost:11434

spring.ai.ollama.chat.options.model=qwen2.5:3b

spring.ai.ollama.chat.options.temperature=0.1

# -----------------------------
# MCP Client Configuration
# -----------------------------

spring.ai.mcp.client.type=SYNC

spring.ai.mcp.client.request-timeout=60s

spring.ai.mcp.client.stdio.servers-configuration=classpath:mcp-servers.json

# -----------------------------
# Logging
# -----------------------------

logging.level.org.springframework.ai=DEBUG

logging.level.io.modelcontextprotocol=DEBUG

```

**Configuration explanation:**

| Property                | Purpose                       |
| ----------------------- | ----------------------------- |
| `ollama.base-url`       | Ollama Docker API address     |
| `chat.options.model`    | Model used by Spring AI       |
| `temperature`           | Controls response randomness  |
| `mcp.client.type`       | Synchronous MCP Client        |
| `servers-configuration` | MCP Server configuration file |
| `request-timeout`       | MCP request timeout           |

The Ollama default endpoint is `http://localhost:11434`.&#x20;

[image](https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=https://docs.spring.io\&sz=32)

docs.spring.io+1



## **10. Configure MCP Server – mcp-servers.json**

Create this file:

`src/main/resources/mcp-servers.json`

```

{
  "mcpServers": {
    "filesystem": {
      "command": "cmd.exe",
      "args": [
        "/c",
        "npx",
        "-y",
        "@modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem",
        "C:\\mcp-demo\\workspace"
      ]
    }
  }
}

```

**Understand this configuration:**

- `filesystem` – MCP Server name.
- `command` – Command used to start the server.
- `cmd.exe` – Windows command interpreter.
- `/c` – Execute the following command.
- `npx` – Execute the Node.js package.
- `@modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem` – Filesystem MCP Server package.
- `C:\mcp-demo\workspace` – Allowed filesystem directory.

When Spring Boot starts, Spring AI will launch this MCP Server process and connect to it using STDIO.

Windows-specific `cmd.exe /c` handling is documented by Spring AI.&#x20;

[image](https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=https://docs.spring.io\&sz=32)

docs.spring.io



## **11. Create ChatClientConfig.java**

This is one of the most important classes.

`ChatClientConfig.java`

```

package com.example.mcp.config;

import org.springframework.ai.chat.client.ChatClient;
import org.springframework.ai.tool.ToolCallbackProvider;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;

@Configuration
public class ChatClientConfig {

    @Bean
    public ChatClient chatClient(
            ChatClient.Builder builder,
            ToolCallbackProvider mcpToolCallbackProvider) {

        return builder
                .defaultToolCallbacks(mcpToolCallbackProvider)
                .build();
    }
}

```

**Understand this code:**

What is `ToolCallbackProvider`?

It represents the tools discovered from MCP Server.

For example, the Filesystem MCP Server provides tools such as:

```
read_file
write_file
list_directory
search_files
create_directory
edit_file
move_file
```

Spring AI MCP Client discovers these tools and makes them available through the provider.

What does this line do?

```
.defaultToolCallbacks(mcpToolCallbackProvider)
```

It registers MCP tools with ChatClient so the LLM can request their execution.

**Important:** We don't need to manually create the filesystem tools in Java. They are provided by the external MCP Server.

This is the official Spring AI integration approach.&#x20;

[image](https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=https://docs.spring.io\&sz=32)

docs.spring.io+1



## **12. Create ChatRequest.java**

```
package com.example.mcp.dto;

public record ChatRequest(
        String message
) {
}
```

## **13. Create ChatResponse.java**

```
package com.example.mcp.dto;

public record ChatResponse(
        String response
) {
}
```

## **14. Create ChatController.java**

This controller accepts the user's request and sends it to Ollama through Spring AI.

`ChatController.java`

```

package com.example.mcp.controller;

import com.example.mcp.dto.ChatRequest;
import com.example.mcp.dto.ChatResponse;

import org.springframework.ai.chat.client.ChatClient;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api")
public class ChatController {

    private final ChatClient chatClient;

    public ChatController(ChatClient chatClient) {
        this.chatClient = chatClient;
    }

    @PostMapping("/chat")
    public ChatResponse chat(@RequestBody ChatRequest request) {

        System.out.println("=================================");
        System.out.println("USER MESSAGE: " + request.message());
        System.out.println("=================================");

        String response = chatClient
                .prompt()
                .system("""
                    You are an AI assistant connected to a
                    filesystem MCP server.

                    For file creation, reading and listing
                    requests, use the available filesystem tools.

                    Work only inside the configured workspace.

                    Do not claim that a file was created or read
                    unless the corresponding tool successfully
                    executes.

                    Do not delete or move files.
                    If the user requests deletion or moving,
                    explain that this demo does not allow it.
                    """)
                .user(request.message())
                .call()
                .content();

        System.out.println("=================================");
        System.out.println("AI RESPONSE: " + response);
        System.out.println("=================================");

        return new ChatResponse(response);
    }
}

```

### **What happens here?**

```
chatClient.prompt()
```

Starts a new prompt.

```
.system("...")
```

Defines the assistant's instructions.

```
.user(request.message())
```

Passes the user's query.

```
.call()
```

Executes the chat interaction, including the tool-calling cycle.

```
.content()
```

Returns the final text response.

Spring AI's ChatClient handles the tool-calling interaction and returns the model's final response.&#x20;

[image](https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=https://docs.spring.io\&sz=32)

docs.spring.io+1

## **18. Understand what happens internally**

This is the most important part of the practical.

Suppose you send:

```
Create HelloWorld.java
```

The internal flow is:

1. **ChatController**

   Receives the Postman request.
2. **ChatClient**

   Passes the user's message and available MCP tool definitions into the model interaction.
3. **Ollama – Qwen 2.5**

   Understands that the user wants a file created and requests a suitable tool call.
4. **Spring AI MCP Client**

   Executes the requested MCP tool callback.
5. **Filesystem MCP Server**

   Receives the request and creates the actual file.
6. **Tool result**

   Result is returned to the LLM.
7. **Final response**

   LLM generates a natural-language response and Spring Boot returns it to Postman.

## **19. Common errors and solutions**

| Error                                    | What to check                                                                |
| ---------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `404 model not found`                    | Run `ollama list` and verify `qwen2.5:3b`                                    |
| `Connection refused: 11434`              | Check Docker container and published port                                    |
| `npx is not recognized`                  | Install Node.js and check PATH                                               |
| MCP server process fails                 | Check `cmd.exe /c` and JSON arguments                                        |
| `NoSuchBeanDefinition` for tool provider | Check MCP starter dependency and server initialization                       |
| Tool is not called                       | Check model supports tool calling and prompt clearly requests file operation |
| Access denied                            | Check the configured workspace path                                          |

**One more thing about your Ollama version:** You previously mentioned Ollama `0.40.6`. Spring AI documents tool calling support from Ollama `0.2.8` onward, so that version is above the stated minimum for non-streaming tool calling. The model must also support tool calling.&#x20;

[image](https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=https://docs.spring.io\&sz=32)

docs.spring.io+1



If you run Spring Boot inside Docker instead of IntelliJ on Windows, the Ollama URL will also need to change to a Docker-reachable address such as `host.docker.internal:11434` (assuming Ollama's port is published on the host).

Bilkul. Hum ek **AI Database Assistant** banayenge jisme aap Hinglish ya English mein question poochoge aur aapka local Ollama model MySQL database se actual data fetch karke answer dega.

Aapke existing **Spring Boot + Spring AI + Ollama + MCP Client** project ko hi continue karenge. Is baar MySQL ke liye ek separate MCP Server Java mein banayenge.

**Final architecture**

**Postman**

User question

**Spring Boot AI Application**

REST API + Spring AI ChatClient

**Ollama**

Understands the question and selects a tool

**MCP Client**

Discovers and invokes MySQL MCP tools

**MySQL MCP Server**

Separate Spring Boot application

**MySQL Database**

Executes read-only SQL queries

We will use **Streamable HTTP** between the two Spring Boot applications. Spring AI supports this transport and automatically exposes registered server tools to the MCP client.&#x20;

[image](https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=https://docs.spring.io\&sz=32)

docs.spring.io+2



**What we will implement:**

1. `list_tables` – database ke tables list karega.
2. `describe_table` – table ka structure batayega.
3. `execute_read_only_query` – SELECT query execute karega.
4. Ollama natural-language questions ko tools ke saath connect karega.

For safety, first version mein database par sirf read operations allowed rahenge. INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE aur DROP implement nahi karenge.
